|\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta^{\prime})-\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})|=o_{k_{2}}(1)
,
then when
t=H_{k}
for integer
k
with
\log_{2}k_{2}\leq k\leq\log_{2}\widehat{n}_{1}

\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{1,-}-2k_{6}\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{j})\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{1,+}+2k_{6}.

Now the oscillations of
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta^{\prime})
for
t\in[H_{k},H_{k+1}]
are controlled, on
\mathscr{G}_{n}
(see (
2.8
)), by something which is
o_{k_{2}}(1).
Thus the conclusion holds with
p=2
, which absorbs the extra
k_{6}
term.
∎

This does not complete the analysis of the real case. We will also need that the Prüfer phases do not oscillate much. This we reduce to a conditional first moment estimate on
\Psi
.

Proposition 2.9
.

(Real case
\sigma=1
) For any
k_{2},k_{4},k_{5},k_{6}

and for any
p\leq 4
,

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\frac{(\log k_{1})^{25}k_{1}}{\widehat{k}_{1}}\times\!\!\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\!\!\mathbb{E}[e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{p}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]=0,\operatorname{a.s.}

We note that the factor
(\log k_{1})^{25}
should be considered as a rate in this statement: the remainder of the constants would correctly balance the sum if the
\mathscr{O}^{\Psi}_{j}
term were removed. Indeed our first application of this will be to show:

Corollary 2.10
.

(Real case
\sigma=1
) For any
k_{2},k_{4},k_{5},k_{6}

and for any
p\leq 4
,

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{W_{j}\in[-k_{6},k_{6}]\}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{p}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]=0\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

Proof of Corollary
2.10
.

On the event
\mathscr{O}_{j}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n},
for all
n
sufficiently large, the event
W_{j}\in[-k_{6},k_{6}]
is contained in the event that for one of the
Ck_{5}k_{1}
elements
\theta\in I_{j}
and a constant
C(\beta,k_{6})
sufficiently large

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}(\theta)-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta)\geq\sqrt{2}\log(n/\widehat{n}_{1})+(\sqrt{2}(\log(\widehat{n}_{1})-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n)-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j}))-C(\beta,k_{6}).

(2.29)

Thus conditioning on
\mathscr{F}_{\widehat{n}_{1}}

and
using the standard Gaussian tail bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\{W_{j}\in[-k_{6},k_{6}]\}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{p}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

(2.30)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}\frac{k_{5}k_{1}\widehat{n}_{1}e^{C(\beta,k_{6})}}{n}\mathbb{E}\biggl[e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{p}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{O}_{j}}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr].

By how
\widehat{n}_{1}
is defined (see (
1.18
)) and how the barrier is defined (
2.13
), this tends to
0
with
k_{1}\ll n
(deterministically).
Thus the result follows from Proposition
2.9
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.9
.

Using the bound on the difference of
\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})
and
\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}
,
it suffices to show

\displaystyle\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\frac{(\log k_{1})^{25}k_{1}}{\widehat{k}_{1}}

(2.31)

\displaystyle\times\!\!\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}\bigr)\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{p}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]=0\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

Let
\theta_{j}^{\mp}
be the largest and smallest element of
\widehat{I}_{j}
respectively.
Introduce the event

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{j}=\biggl\{\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}|\Psi_{k}(\theta^{-}_{j})-\Psi_{k}(\theta^{+}_{j})|\leq\tfrac{k_{1}(\log k_{1})^{50}}{2k_{1}^{+}}\biggr\}.

(2.32)

We emphasize the ray event
\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})
runs to a much later time (when
k_{1}
is large) than the oscillation event
\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{j}.

Our main task will be to show that conditionally on

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j}),

\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},

and
\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1}),
the probability of the event
\bigl(\widehat{\mathscr{O}_{j}}\bigr)^{c}
is
controlled.
We show in Corollary
4.7
(recall (
2.7
) for the change in notation)
that
there is a constant
C_{\beta}>0
sufficiently large that
for all
n,k_{1}
large, uniformly in
\theta_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\mathscr{O}_{j}}^{c}\cap\mathscr{R}_{j}^{p}(\widehat{n}_{1})\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{\beta}\frac{\left(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})+k_{6}\right)_{+}\left(\sqrt{2}m_{n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})\right)_{+}}{(\log k_{1})^{50}\log(\widehat{n}_{1}/k_{2})}.

(2.33)

We can now return to
(
2.31
), and substitute in the left hand side the last estimate.
We can treat the exponential of

\sqrt{2}(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})-\log(\widehat{n}_{1}/k_{2})

as a change of measure. Under this tilted measure
{\mathbb{Q}}
the increment

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}

has mean
\sqrt{2}\log(\widehat{n}_{1}/k_{2}).

Then recalling the definition of
\widehat{n}_{1}
(see (
1.18
))
for all
n
sufficiently large

{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\in[A_{\widehat{n}_{1}}^{p,-},A_{\widehat{n}_{1}}^{p,+}]}\right\}\left(\sqrt{2}m_{n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\right)_{+}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\right)\leq C_{\beta}\frac{(\log k_{1})^{\tfrac{19}{20}\tfrac{4p+1}{4p+2}}}{\sqrt{\log(\widehat{n}_{1}/k_{2})}}.

Thus we
conclude
for all
n
sufficiently large
using the Brownian Bridge ballot theorem (
[
KS91
, (3.40)]
),

\displaystyle\frac{k_{1}}{\widehat{k}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[

\displaystyle\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}\bigr)\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{p}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

(2.34)

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta,k_{6})\frac{k_{1}(\log n)^{3/2}}{n(\log\widehat{n}_{1}/k_{2})^{3/2}(\log k_{1})^{49}}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})-\log(k_{2})}\left(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})+k_{6}\right)_{+}.

Hence summing over
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}
and
sending
n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\frac{k_{1}}{\widehat{k}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}\bigr)\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{p}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

(2.35)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\beta}}{2\pi(\log k_{1})^{49}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)-\log(k_{2})}\bigl(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)+k_{6}\bigr)_{+}d\theta\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

Hence this tends to
0
on multiplying by
(\log k_{1})^{25}
and sending
k_{1}\to\infty.

∎

Proposition 2.11
.

(Imaginary case
\sigma=i
) For any
k_{5},k_{6}
and
p=2

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\frac{(\log k_{1})^{25}k_{1}}{\widehat{k}_{1}}\times\!\!\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{p}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]=0\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

Furthermore, for all
k_{2}
sufficiently large and
p=2

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{W_{j}\in[-k_{6},k_{6}]\}\bigl((1-\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{p}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\})+(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\bigr)\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]=0\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

Proof.

When
\sigma=i,

\varphi_{k}(\theta)=\Psi_{k}(\theta)-(k+1)\theta
for all
k,\theta.
The proof of the first claim is identical to the real case Proposition
2.9
.

We turn to the second claim. From Proposition
2.5
, we may assume that whenever
\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})
occurs for some
j
then so does
\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})
. We will further show now that whenever
\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})
occurs, so does the event

\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\theta)\coloneqq\biggl\{\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}|\Psi_{k}(\theta-\tfrac{2\pi k_{1}}{n})-\Psi_{k}(\theta+\tfrac{2\pi k_{1}}{n})|\leq\tfrac{k_{1}(\log k_{1})^{50}}{2k_{1}^{+}}\biggr\}.

In particular, in a manner similar to Proposition
2.9
, we can derive

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{4k_{5}n}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})^{c}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\xrightarrow[k_{5},k_{4},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

(2.36)

We comment briefly on the proof of (
2.36
) at the end, and we turn to deriving the second claim of this proposition. The event
\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\theta)
, for any
\theta\in I_{j}
, directly implies
\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}
(recalling (
2.26
)), from the monotonicity of the Prüfer phases. On the other hand, on the event
W_{j}\in[-k_{6},k_{6}]
, we have a
\theta\in I_{j}
so that
\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)\cap\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\theta)
holds. Using monotonicity of the Prüfer phases and
\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\theta)
, for any
k
with
k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}

\Psi_{k}(\theta_{j})\leq\Psi_{k}(\theta+\tfrac{2\pi k_{1}}{n})\leq\Psi_{k}(\theta-\tfrac{2\pi k_{1}}{n})+\tfrac{k_{1}(\log k_{1})^{50}}{2k_{1}^{+}}\leq\Psi_{k}(\theta)+\tfrac{k_{1}(\log k_{1})^{50}}{2k_{1}^{+}}.

Furthermore, trivially by monotonicity,
\Psi_{k}(\theta_{j})\geq\Psi_{k}(\theta)
, and so
\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}|\Psi_{k}(\theta_{j})-\Psi_{k}(\theta)|=o_{k_{1}}(1)
. Subtracting the mean behavior from
\Psi_{k}
for
\theta
in this range also is vanishing, i.e:
\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}|\varphi_{k}(\theta_{j})-\varphi_{k}(\theta)|=o_{k_{1}}(1)
. As the oscillations of
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
for
t\in[H_{k},H_{k+1}]
are
o_{k_{2}}(1)
on
\mathscr{G}_{n}
and
|\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k}}-\varphi_{k}|\leq k_{6}
we conclude for all
H_{k_{2}}\leq t\leq H_{n_{1}^{+}}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{j})\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta)+o_{k_{2}}(1)+2k_{6}\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{1,+}+o_{k_{2}}(1)+2k_{6},

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{j})\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta)-o_{k_{2}}(1)-2k_{6}\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{1,-}-o_{k_{2}}(1)-2k_{6},

which hence implies the event
\mathscr{R}^{1.1}_{j}({n}_{1}^{+})
for all
k_{2}
sufficiently large.
Thus we have reduced the second claim to showing

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{1.1}_{j}({n}^{+}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{2}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})^{c}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}\}\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\theta)\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr]\xrightarrow[k_{5},k_{4},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

(2.37)

We separately consider the events that
\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})
fails due to

\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{j})\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{p,+}

or due to

\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{p,-}\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{j})

for some
t
in
[H_{n_{1}^{+}},H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}],
which we call
\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}
and
\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{-}
respectively.
We start with the failure event
\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}
.
For any
\theta\in I_{j}
, on the event
\mathscr{L}(\theta)
, we must have

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}(\theta)-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{{n}_{1}}}(\theta)\geq\sqrt{2}\log(n/{n}_{1})+(\sqrt{2}(\log({n}_{1})-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n)-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{{n}_{1}}}(\theta)).

Thus conditioning on
\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}}

and
using the standard Gaussian tail bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{1.1}_{j}({n}^{+}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}\}\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\theta)\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr]

(2.38)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{n}_{1}}{n}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}e^{\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{{n}_{1}}}(\theta)-2m_{{n}_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}\}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{1.1}({n}_{1}^{+})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr].

From monotonicity of the Prüfer phases,
for
\theta\in I_{j}

\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta)\leq\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})+o_{k_{1}}(1)
,
and hence on
\mathscr{G}_{n}
we have the same estimate for
\mathfrak{Z}
up to a constant depending on
k_{6}
.
Thus

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{1.1}_{j}({n}^{+}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}\}\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\theta)\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\frac{Ck_{5}k_{1}{n}_{1}e^{C(k_{6})}}{n}\mathbb{E}\biggl[e^{\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{{n}_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}\}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{1.1}({n}_{1}^{+})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr].

Hence it suffices to estimate above the
\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}
–conditional probability of
\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}
, which requires that the Brownian motion
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
for
t\in[H_{n_{1}^{+}},H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}]
exceeds a barrier. This barrier can be bounded below by the linear barrier

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\leq\sqrt{2}(t-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n)-(\log k_{1})^{\tfrac{19}{20}\tfrac{1}{4p+2}},\quad t\in[H_{n_{1}^{+}},H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}]

(2.39)

with
p=2.
We also have that
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
cannot exceed the barrier

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\leq\sqrt{2}(t-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n)+2(\log k_{1})^{1/100},\quad t\in[H_{n_{1}^{+}},H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}]

(2.40)

which is implied by the event
\mathscr{G}_{n}
for all
k_{1}
sufficiently large.

The
\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\vee\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n_{1}}})
–conditional probability that
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}>\sqrt{2}A_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}^{1,+}
is vanishingly small as the variance of the Brownian bridge is at least
\tfrac{1}{2}(\log k_{1})^{19/20}
at this time.
Moreover for a standard Brownian bridge, the probability that it stays below a straight line with intercepts
a,b>0
is
1-e^{-2ab}
(
[
KS91
, (3.40)]
). It follows that

\mathbb{P}(\eqref{eq:2025_1}\mid\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\vee\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}))=1-\exp\biggl(-2\tfrac{(\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n_{1}^{+}}}-(\log k_{1})^{\tfrac{19}{20}\tfrac{1}{10}})(\sqrt{2}m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}-(\log k_{1})^{\tfrac{19}{20}\tfrac{1}{10}})}{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}-H_{{n}_{1}^{+}}}\biggr).

Uniformly over allowed
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n_{1}}^{+}}
(which are restricted by the event
\mathscr{R}^{(1.1)}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})
) and uniformly over
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\in[\sqrt{2}A_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}^{1,-},\sqrt{2}A_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}^{1,+}]

\mathbb{P}(\eqref{eq:2025_1}\mid\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\vee\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}))=1-\exp\biggl(-2\tfrac{(\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n_{1}^{+}}})(\sqrt{2}m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}})}{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}-H_{{n}_{1}^{+}}}(1+o_{k_{1}}(1))\biggr).

The same holds for

\mathbb{P}(\eqref{eq:2025_2}\mid\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\vee\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}))
, and so we conclude that

\mathbb{P}(\eqref{eq:2025_2}\setminus\eqref{eq:2025_1}\mid\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\vee\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}))=o_{k_{1}}(1),

as either both probabilities are close to
1
(and so no cancellation within the exponentials is needed), or they cancel. We conclude that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[e^{\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{{n}_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}\}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{1.1}({n}_{1}^{+})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\vee\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq o_{k_{1}}(1)\cdot\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\widehat{n}_{1}}{n_{1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[e^{\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{1.1}({n}_{1}^{+})\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\vee\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}})\biggr],

so that we have shown

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{1.1}_{j}({n}^{+}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}\}\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\theta)\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq o_{k_{1}}(1)\cdot\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\frac{Ck_{5}k_{1}{n}_{1}^{+}e^{C(k_{6})}}{n}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{{n}_{1}^{+}}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{{n}_{1}^{+}}\bigr)\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}_{j}^{1.1}({n}_{1}^{+})\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr].

This conditional expectation we then evaluate, giving

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{1.1}_{j}({n}^{+}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{+}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}\}\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\theta)\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq o_{k_{1}}(1)\cdot\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\frac{Ck_{5}k_{1}e^{C(k_{6})}}{n}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{\beta/2}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})-\log k_{2}\bigr)\bigl(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})+k_{6}\bigr)_{+}.

Hence on sending
k_{1}\to\infty
and using Theorem
1.6
, this tends to
0
.

Recalling the definition below (
2.37
), we turn to the case of
\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{-}
. On this event, for some
t\in[H_{n_{1}^{+}},H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}]
we have
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{j})\leq\sqrt{2}(t-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n-(\log n-t)^{\tfrac{9}{10}}).

From monotonicity, it follows that for all
\theta\in I_{j}
and some
t\in[H_{n_{1}^{+}},H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}]

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta)\leq\sqrt{2}(t-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n-(\log n-t)^{\tfrac{9}{10}})+C(\beta,k_{6}).

On the other hand, on the event
\widehat{R}(\theta),
we have

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta)\geq\sqrt{2}(t-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n-(\log n-t)^{\tfrac{5}{6}}).

Hence these are incompatible and so we have that trivially

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{1.1}_{j}({n}^{+}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{REsc}_{j}^{-}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}\}\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\theta)\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr]\xrightarrow[k_{5},k_{4},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

(2.41)

We finish by briefly commenting on the proof of (
2.36
). We begin by taking a conditional probability of the event
\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})^{c}
. From Corollary
4.7
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})^{c}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{\beta}\frac{\left(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})+k_{6}\right)_{+}\left(\sqrt{2}m_{n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{\widehat{n}_{1}}}(\theta_{j})\right)_{+}}{(\log k_{1})^{50}\log(\widehat{n}_{1}/k_{2})}.

This is the same control that we had in the real part (compare to (
2.3.4
)), and so the proof can be completed in a similar way to Proposition
2.9
.
∎

2.4
Decoupling and a diffusion approximation

Our main tool for showing a Poisson approximation will be to modify the process
\varphi_{k}
for
k\geq n_{1}^{+}
which shows both that in a sense
\varphi_{k}(\theta)-\varphi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\theta)
stabilizes as
n\to\infty
and simultaneously gives actual
(\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})
–conditional independence of these processes for sufficiently separated
\theta.
Set
T_{+}=\log k_{1}
and
T_{-}=\log(k_{1}/k_{1}^{+}).
We shall construct a family of standard complex Brownian motions

\{\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{j}:T_{-}\leq t\leq T_{+},j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}\},

(2.42)

which will have the property that they are independent from one another when the relevant arcs are separated by a small power of
n.

Now with respect to these Brownian motions we define the complex diffusions
(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}:t\in[T_{-},T_{+}],\theta\in{\mathbb{R}},j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}})
as the (strong) solution of the stochastic differential equation

\displaystyle d\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}(\theta)=d\mathfrak{L}_{t}(\theta)

\displaystyle={i\theta e^{t}}{k_{1}^{-1}}dt+\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}e^{i\Im\mathfrak{L}_{t}(\theta)}d\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{j},\text{ and }

(2.43)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{t}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-\Re\bigl(\sigma\bigl(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}(\theta)-i\theta k_{1}^{-1}e^{t}\bigr)\bigr)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-2\log\Phi_{n_{1}^{+}}^{*}(\exp(i(\theta_{j}+\tfrac{\theta}{n})))+\tfrac{i\theta}{k_{1}}e^{T_{-}},\quad\text{for}\quad\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}.

The diffusion
\mathfrak{L}^{j}(\theta)
will serve as a proxy for the evolution of

-2\log\Phi^{*}_{k(t)}(\theta_{j}+\theta/n)+i\theta e^{t}/k_{1}

where
k(t)\approx n_{1}e^{t}
up to rounding errors, and in particular its
imaginary part will mimick the evolution of the Prüfer phases.
When
\sigma=1
, the diffusion
\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{t}(\theta)

is designed to be a proxy for
2\log|\Phi^{*}_{k(t)}(\theta_{j}+\theta/n)|
.

Later, we shall consider a different
initial conditions for the SDE in (
2.43
), which will be enforced at
t=T_{-}
; Namely, we shall enforce
constant initial conditions (the latter process will be denoted
\mathfrak{L}_{t}^{o,j}
or
\mathfrak{L}_{t}^{o}
, see e.g. (
2.52
) below).
We note that if the initial conditions do not depend on
n
then the law of the process in (
2.43
) does not depend on
n
; moreover if the initial conditions are constant in
\theta
, then the law of this diffusion is just a translation of a
0
–initial condition process by the initialization.

Proposition 2.12
.

Fix

C>0
. Then,
there exists
\delta_{0}>0
so that for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
there
exists a family of Brownian motions
\{\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{j}:T_{-}\leq t\leq T_{+},j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}\}
so that the following holds.
Let
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}.

Let
(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}:t\in[T_{-},T_{+}])
solve (
2.43
).
For all
n
sufficiently large (with respect to
k_{1}
),

\sup_{|\theta|\leq n^{8\delta}}\!\mathbb{P}\biggl[\mathscr{T}_{n_{1}^{+}}\cap\biggl\{\sup_{t\in[T_{-},T_{+}]}|\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}(\theta)\!-i\tfrac{\theta}{k_{1}}e^{t}\!+\!2\log\Phi_{k(t)}^{*}(\exp(i(\theta_{j}+\tfrac{\theta}{n})))|\!>\!n^{-\delta}\biggr\}\!~\bigg|~\!\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\biggr]\leq n^{-C}\operatorname{a.s.},

where
\log k(t)=\log k_{n}(t)
is a linear function with
k(T_{-})=n_{1}^{+}
and
k(T_{+})=n.

Moreover for any
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}
,

\sigma\left\{\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{i}:t\in[T_{-},T_{+}],d_{\mathbb{T}}(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})\leq n^{-1+8\delta}\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma\left\{\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{i}:t\in[T_{-},T_{+}],d_{\mathbb{T}}(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})\geq 4n^{-1+8\delta}\right\}

are conditionally independent given
\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}.

This allows the comparison of
2\log\Phi_{k(t)}^{*}(\exp(i(\theta_{j}+\tfrac{\theta}{n})))
to different
\mathfrak{L}^{j^{\prime}}
(for
j^{\prime}\neq j
)
provided that
|\theta_{j}-\theta_{j^{\prime}}|
is small enough.
We give the proof in Section
5
.

2.4.1
Simplifying the decoration

Now, since the probability is so high that
|\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{t}(\theta)-\varphi_{k(t)}(\theta_{j}+\tfrac{\theta}{n})+\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}|<n^{-\delta}
for all
t\in[T_{-},T_{+}]
,
we may just work on the event

\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}=\biggl\{\sup_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\,\,\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\\
|\theta|\leq n^{8\delta}\end{subarray}}\,\,\sup_{t\in[T_{-},T_{+}]}|\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{t}(\theta)-\varphi_{k(t)}(\theta_{j}+\tfrac{\theta}{n})+\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}|<n^{-\delta}\biggr\}.

(2.44)

We will also introduce another barrier event, now specific to
\mathfrak{U}^{j}
and only concerning
k\geq n_{1}^{+}.

We define a decoration–ray event with a (once more) enlarged barrier function.
These are given by for
t\in[T_{-},T_{+}]

\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\pm}\coloneqq(t-T_{+})-(T_{+}-t)^{1/2\mp 3/7}.

(2.45)

Note the exponents coincide with those of
A_{t}^{3,\pm}
.
So we introduce a decoration ray event

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\forall~t\in[T_{-},T_{+}-k_{4}]:\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\mathcal{A}_{t}^{-}\leq\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{t}(\theta)\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\mathcal{A}_{t}^{+}\right\}

(2.46)

\displaystyle\cap\left\{\forall~t\in[T_{+}-k_{4},T_{+}]:\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{t}(\theta)\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\bigl(t-T_{+}+\bigl({T_{+}-t+(\log k_{5})^{50}}\bigr)^{1/50}\bigr)\right\},

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigcap_{\theta\in I_{j}}\bigl(\left\{\mathfrak{U}_{T_{+}}(\theta)<-k_{6}\right\}\cup\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)\bigr),

the latter of which states that all lattice–point near-leaders (
\theta\in I_{j}
for which
\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(\theta)
is large) reached that height by staying within (an enlarged) banana–like tube. On the event
\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n},
if the event
\widehat{\mathscr{R}_{j}}
holds (see (
2.28
) and (
2.15
)) then deterministically
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}
holds as well (once
k_{1}
and
k_{4}
are sufficiently large).

Instead of looking at the local maximum of
\varphi_{n}

we may look at the local maximum of
\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T^{+}}.

Moreover, we define the analogues of
W_{j}
and
V_{j}
(see (
2.25
) and (
1.19
)) for this new process

W_{j}^{\prime}\coloneqq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]\cap\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\\
\mathbf{1}{\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)}=1\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(\theta)\quad\text{and}\quad V_{j}^{\prime}\coloneqq\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n_{1}^{+}}}{(\theta_{j})}.

(2.47)

We also define a new decoration process
D_{j}^{\prime}
by first defining it on a mesh by the formula

D_{j}^{\prime}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{aligned} &\biggl(e^{-\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{+}}\!\!(\theta)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}+i(\theta_{j}+\tfrac{\theta}{n})(n+1)}\times\mathbf{1}{\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)}:\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]\cap\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\biggr),\quad\text{if $\sigma=1$ or if not,}\quad\\
&\biggl(e^{\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}\!\!(\theta)}\times\mathbf{1}{\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)}:\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]\cap\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\biggr).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.48)

We extend
D_{j}^{\prime}
to be piecewise linear between the mesh points.

2.4.2
Continuity of the decoration processes around near maxima

We need to show that near local maxima, the decoration process is well–behaved, and moreover that the meshing accurately captures the continuum process. Define the good event:

Definition 2.13
.

We let
{\mathscr{N}}_{j}
be the event that the following holds.

1.

The maximum over the grid,
W_{j},
is in
[-k_{6},k_{6}].

2.

For some
\epsilon_{\beta}
(to be specified), in the neighborhood of a near-maximal grid point
\theta\in I_{j}
the field
\varphi_{n}(\theta)
does not vary too much. Specifically: at any point
\theta\in I_{j}
at which
\varphi_{n}(\theta)\in\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}+[-k_{6},\infty)
(i.e.
\mathscr{L}(\theta)
holds), all points
\theta^{\prime}
with
|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|\leq\frac{2\pi}{4k_{5}n}
satisfy
|\varphi_{n}(\theta)-\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})|\leq k_{5}^{-\epsilon_{\beta}}.

3.

For any point
\theta\in\widehat{I}_{j}
at which
\varphi_{n}(\theta)\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}-k_{6}
, there is a point
\theta^{\prime}\in I_{j}
with
|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|\leq\frac{2\pi}{4k_{5}n}
at which
|\varphi_{n}(\theta)-\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})|\leq k_{5}^{-\epsilon_{\beta}}.

By a combination of a first moment argument and interpolation theorems for polynomials, we show that in any arc where
\widehat{W}_{j}
is large, we also have
\mathscr{N}_{j}.

Proposition 2.14
.

Any interval in which
\widehat{W}_{j}
is large must also have
\mathscr{N}_{j}
occur in that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{W}_{j}\in[-k_{7},k_{7}]\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{N}_{j}^{c}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}({n}_{1}^{+})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\xrightarrow[k_{6},k_{5},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

We give the proof in Section
3
.

2.5
Summary of first–moment reductions

The reductions made in Section
2.3
will now be summarized.
Define for
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{j}=\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}({n}_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}^{+}\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{A}^{\prime}_{j}=\mathscr{N}_{j}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{A}_{j}

(2.49)

Using these events,
define for Borel subsets of
[0,2\pi]\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathcal{C}([-2\pi k_{1},0],{\mathbb{C}}),

the following approximation

\operatorname{Extr}_{n}=\operatorname{Extr}_{n}^{k_{1},k_{2},k_{4},k_{5},k_{6}}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{\prime}e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{A}_{j}}\right\}

(2.50)

Proposition 2.15
.

For any
k_{7}
the restrictions of

\operatorname{Extr}_{n}
and
\operatorname{Ext}_{n}
to
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
satisfy

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\operatorname{Ext}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{6},k_{5},k_{4},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Proof.

By definition of the metric
\partial_{2}
we may restrict the point processes to good events and conclude

\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\{\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}}\right\}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\leq\mathbb{P}((\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n})^{c}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}),

and hence from Lemma

2.4

and Proposition
2.12
,
this tends to
0
in that

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\{\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}}\right\}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

The same holds for
\operatorname{Extr}_{n}
replaced by
\operatorname{Ext}_{n},
and so we may as well restrict to the event
{\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}}.

The point process
\operatorname{Ext}_{n}
we then further thin by defining

\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j},D_{j}e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{A}^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}

and

\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j},D_{j}e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{A}^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(\widehat{n}_{1})\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}}\right\}.

We have that as point processes

\operatorname{Ext}_{n}\geq\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime}\geq\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime\prime}.

Hence from the definition of the
\partial_{2}
-distance it suffices to show that
\operatorname{Ext}_{n}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})-\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0
as it then follows that all of these point processes are close on restriction to
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
.

Recall that the events

\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(\widehat{n}_{1})\subseteq\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}({n}_{1}^{+})

and

\mathscr{O}_{j}\subseteq\mathscr{O}_{j}^{+}.
We claim that the combination of
Propositions

2.6
,

2.7
,

2.11
,
and
2.14
together with Corollary
2.10

show that on the good event
{\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}}
the expected number of points lost by performing this thinning tends to
0,
i.e.

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\operatorname{Ext}_{n}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})-\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})\bigr)\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\bigr]

(2.51)

Proposition
2.6
implies that for any triple
(\theta_{j},V_{j},D_{j}e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}})\in\Gamma_{k_{7}}
,
we may include the good event that
{W_{j}}\in[-k_{6},k_{6}]
.
Proposition
2.7

implies for
\sigma=1
(the real case) that for any
{W_{j}}\in[-k_{6},k_{6}]
we may include the good events both
\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(\widehat{n}_{1})
and
\mathscr{O}_{j}
.
Corollary
2.10
implies for
\sigma=1
(the real case) that we may further add the good event that
\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}
occurs.
Proposition
2.11
implies the analogue of the previous two statements in the imaginary case.
Proposition
2.14
finally adds the
\mathscr{N}_{j}
event.

We also introduce a thinning

\operatorname{Extr}_{n}^{\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{\prime}e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{A}^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}\leq\operatorname{Extr}_{n}.

On the events
\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}
we have

\max_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}|V_{j}^{\prime}-V_{j}|\leq n^{-\delta}\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}|D_{j}^{\prime}-D_{j}|\leq e^{k_{6}+n^{-\delta}}n^{-\delta}.

In particular,

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\{\operatorname{Extr}_{n}^{\prime}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}}\right\}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Finally we undo the thinning on
\operatorname{Extr}_{n}.
We just note that
since on the event
\mathscr{G}^{1}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}
, the processes
(V_{j}^{\prime},V_{j})
and
(D_{j}^{\prime},D_{j})
are sufficiently close, we have that

(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}-\operatorname{Extr}_{n}^{\prime})(\Gamma_{k_{7}})\leq(\operatorname{Ext}_{n}-\operatorname{Ext}_{n}^{\prime\prime})(\Gamma_{2k_{7}}).

As (
2.51
) holds for any
k_{7}
, the result follows.
∎

2.6
Second approximation: removing the oscillations at level
n_{1}^{+}

The first–moment reductions allow us to assume that the profile at level
n_{1}^{+}
in the neighborhoods of high decorations are flat. However, they still carry
n
–dependence, and we still need to show that these small oscillations at level
n_{1}^{+}
propagate to small oscillations at the level
n.

So, we introduce a new diffusion which solves (
2.43
) after an intermediate time
T_{\dagger}=\log(k_{1}/\widehat{k}_{1})
, has
0
initial conditions at time
T_{-}
, and between
[T_{-},T_{\dagger}]
it is a single Brownian motion.

\displaystyle d\mathfrak{L}^{o,j}_{t}(\theta)=d\mathfrak{L}^{o}_{t}(\theta)

\displaystyle={i\theta e^{t}}{k_{1}^{-1}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{t\geq T_{\dagger}}\right\}dt+\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}e^{i\Im\mathfrak{L}^{o,j}_{t}(\theta)}e^{i\Im\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0)}d\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{j},

(2.52)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{U}^{o,j}_{t}(\theta)=\mathfrak{U}^{o}_{t}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-\Re\bigl(\sigma\bigl(\mathfrak{L}^{o}_{t}(\theta)-i\theta k_{1}^{-1}(e^{t}-1)\bigr)\bigr)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\log k_{1}^{+},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{o}_{T_{-}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=0.

These initial conditions are flat, in that they do not vary over the interval
\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]
(note that the value of the initial condition, when flat, does not change the law of the increments). We note that there is a fixed phase
\Im\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0)
that appears in the diffusion, which does not affect the law of the process as it rotates the complex white noise. We shall show that
\mathfrak{L}^{o,j}_{t}(\theta)+\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0)
is a good approximation for
\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}(\theta)
, and so we need to carry this
\mathscr{F}_{T_{-}}
–measurable constant
\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0)
whenever we make a comparison.

We also change the barrier event by dropping the barrier on
[T_{-},T_{\dagger}]
; we also include a shift
h
, which measures the additional (positive) amount the process
\mathfrak{U}_{t}^{o,j}
will need to climb (recall (
2.45
)):

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{j}(\theta,h)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\forall~t\in[T_{\dagger},T_{+}-k_{4}]:\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\mathcal{A}_{t}^{-}\leq\mathfrak{U}^{o,j}_{t}(\theta)-\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}h\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\mathcal{A}_{t}^{+}\right\},

(2.53)

\displaystyle\cap\left\{\forall~t\in[T_{+}-k_{4},T_{+}]:\mathfrak{U}^{o,j}_{t}(\theta)-\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}h\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\bigl(t-T_{+}+\bigl({T_{+}-t+(\log k_{5})^{50}}\bigr)^{1/50}\bigr)\right\}.

In analogy with (
2.48
), we define a decoration process in which we replace the
\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}
by
\mathfrak{L}^{o,j}_{t}
:

D_{j}^{o}(h)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{aligned} &\biggl(e^{-\mathfrak{L}^{o,j}_{T_{+}}\!\!(\theta)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\log k_{1}^{+}-i\Im\mathfrak{L}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)+i\theta_{j}(n+1)}\times\mathbf{1}{\mathscr{P}_{j}(\theta,h)}:\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]\cap\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\biggr),\quad\text{if $\sigma=1$,}\quad\\
&\biggl(e^{\mathfrak{U}^{o,j}_{T_{+}}\!\!(\theta)}\times\mathbf{1}{\mathscr{P}_{j}(\theta,h)}:\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]\cap\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\biggr),\quad\text{if $\sigma\neq 1$}\end{aligned}\right.

(2.54)

and where we linearly interpolate between these
\theta
to give continuous functions on
[-2\pi k_{1},0].
We introduce
W_{j}^{o}
as

W_{j}^{o}=\max_{\theta}\log|D_{j}^{o}(\theta)|-\sqrt{\frac{4}{\beta}}V_{j}^{\prime},

which plays the same role as
W_{j}^{\prime}
.
We also define the Markov kernel
\mathfrak{s}(\cdot,\cdot)=\mathfrak{s}_{k_{1},k_{5}}(\cdot,\cdot)

\mathfrak{s}(h,\cdot)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}(\exp(i\Im\mathfrak{L}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0))D_{j}^{o}(h)\in\cdot),&\quad\text{if $\sigma=1$},\\
\mathbb{P}(D_{j}^{o}(h)\in\cdot),&\quad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(2.55)

which is deterministic and depends neither on
j
nor on
n
.

Remark 2.16
.

By construction, the decoration takes as input at time
T_{\dagger}
the process
\mathfrak{L}^{o,j}_{T_{\dagger}}
, which is flat. The time

T_{\dagger}

is negative by a sublogarithmic time in
k_{1}
. This we show to be interchangeable with a decoration that uses a process that solves (
2.43
) with
flat initial condition at time
T_{-}.
Indeed, we could as well start the initial condition at
-\infty
, but we do not pursue this.

Remark 2.17
.

The effect of restricting the processes to a grid is
for technical convenience.
In fact, we could now remove the linear interpolation at this stage of the proof if we so desire: see Remark
4.9
. However, this is not necessary to complete the main results, and so we do not pursue it.

We introduce a new point process in which the decoration has been replaced by this one, and unnecessary events have been dropped.

\operatorname{Extre}_{n}=\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{k_{1},k_{2},k_{4},k_{5},k_{6}}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}.

(2.56)

Here we use the shorthand
D_{j}^{o}=D_{j}^{o}(V_{j}^{\prime}).

Proposition 2.18
.

For any
k_{7}
the restrictions of

\operatorname{Extr}_{n}
and
\operatorname{Extre}_{n}
to
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
satisfy

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\operatorname{Extre}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{6},k_{5},k_{4},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Proof.

Recall (
2.48
) and let
E_{j}
be the event that

\sup_{\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]}|D_{j}^{o}e^{-\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}}-D_{j}^{\prime}|\geq 3e^{-(\log k_{1})^{1/100}}.

This event is typical in that for
\theta
for which both
D_{j}^{o}e^{-\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}}
and
D_{j}^{\prime}
are below
e^{-(\log k_{1})^{1/100}}
it holds trivially, whereas if a single one is above this level, we do control the difference (and moreover the difference is much smaller). By Proposition
6.1
, by a simple union bound, on the event
\mathscr{O}_{j}^{+}\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}

(recall (
1.10
)), for all
k_{1}
sufficiently large

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E_{j}\cap\bigl\{\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{\prime}e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}})}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})>0\bigr\}~|\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})\leq Ck_{5}(k_{1}/k_{1}^{+})e^{-\delta(\log k_{1})^{19/20}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E_{j}\cap\bigl\{\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})>0\bigr\}~|\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})\leq Ck_{5}(k_{1}/k_{1}^{+})e^{-\delta(\log k_{1})^{19/20}}.

The probability that
E_{j}^{c}
is sufficiently high that we can essentially assume it always holds. In other words, if we define the process

\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{*}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\biggl(\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}+\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{\prime}\exp({\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}}))}\biggr)\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap E_{j}}\right\},

(2.57)

then

\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{*}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})\xrightarrow[k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

To see this just note using the estimate
Lemma
4.4

and the control on
\varphi_{n_{1}^{+}}
given by

\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{*}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq\frac{c(k_{5})}{n_{1}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}

\displaystyle e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j}))-\log(k_{2})}\bigl(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\times\exp\bigl(O((\log k_{1})^{\tfrac{9}{10}})-\delta(\log k_{1})^{19/20}\bigr).

This tends to
0
almost surely on taking
n\to\infty
followed by
k_{1}\to\infty.

As in the proof of Proposition
2.15
, we introduce comparison point processes,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}^{4}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}}\right\}

(2.58)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{{\mathscr{R}^{4}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}}\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}}\right\}

We note that all of these processes are thinnings of one another in that

\operatorname{Extre}_{n}\geq\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\prime}\geq\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\prime\prime}

and so if we show that the number of points in
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
that are thinned tends to
0
in probability between each of these.
We justify each of these thinnings below.

The first thinning.

Note that since
\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{*}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})
converges to
0
, we may assume that
E_{j}^{c}
whenever
\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})=1
.
On the event
\mathscr{G}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}^{1}\cap E_{j}^{c}
,
if
\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})=1,
then it follows that for some
\theta\in I_{j}
,
\mathscr{P}_{j}(\theta,V_{j}^{\prime})
held (because, if not, by definition
D_{j}^{o}
is identically
0
, contradicting the
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
condition). As
E_{j}^{c}
holds, we also have that

\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)
holds.
By Proposition
2.7
and the fact that
\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})
holds, we therefore have that deterministically

\mathscr{R}_{j}^{4}(\widehat{n}_{1})\cap\mathscr{O}_{j}
holds.

The second thinning.

We have that the difference

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\prime}-\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\prime\prime})(\Gamma_{k_{7}})\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{G}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}^{1}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{\widehat{n}_{1}}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\left\{{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})\in\Gamma_{k_{7}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\{{\mathscr{R}}^{4}_{j}(\widehat{n}_{1})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}\}(1-\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{O}_{j}^{\Psi}\})\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{\widehat{n}_{1}}].

We can dominate the event

\mathbf{1}\left\{{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})\in\Gamma_{k_{7}}}\right\}\leq\sum_{\theta\in I_{j}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{P}_{j}(\theta,V_{j}^{\prime})}\right\}.

As we work on
{\mathscr{G}_{n}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}^{1}}
,
for one of these rays to succeed, we must have that a Gaussian of variance
\log(n/\widehat{n}_{1})
climbs distance

-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})+\sqrt{2}m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}+\sqrt{2}\log(n/\widehat{n}_{1})+O_{k_{4}}(1).

Using that
\log(n/\widehat{n}_{1})=\log\widehat{k}_{1}\gg\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{8}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})-m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}+O_{k_{4}}(1)
on our event, the probability of the Gaussian
exceedance is bounded above by

\exp\bigl({-\bigl(-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})\!+\!\sqrt{2}m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}\!+\!\sqrt{2}\log(n/\widehat{n}_{1})\!+\!O_{k_{4}}(1)\bigr)^{2}/2\log\widehat{k}_{1}}\bigr)\!\leq\!\frac{C(k_{4})}{\widehat{k}_{1}}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}\bigr).

There are
O_{k_{5}}(k_{1})
of these Gaussians, and so by a union bound

\mathbb{P}({(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})\in(\Gamma_{k_{7}})}~|~\mathscr{F}_{\widehat{n}_{1}})\leq C(k_{4})\frac{k_{1}}{\widehat{k}_{1}}\times\exp\bigl(\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{j})-2m_{\widehat{n}_{1}}\bigr).

The claim now follows from Proposition
2.9
(or
2.11
in the imaginary case).

The third thinning.

If we introduce now

\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\dagger}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{A}_{j}}\right\},

then we have that on
\Gamma_{k_{7}}

\operatorname{Extre}_{n}\geq\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\dagger}\geq\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\prime\prime},

and so

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extre}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\dagger}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{6},k_{5},k_{4},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Finally by direct computation on the event
\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{*}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})=0

\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{\dagger}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}},\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}})\leq 3e^{-(\log k_{1})^{1/100}},

as this holds with probability tending to
1,
the proof is complete.
∎

2.7
Initial Poisson approximation

We are now in a position to introduce the first Poisson process approximation that we make.
Define the
(\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})
–measurable random measures on
({\mathbb{T}},{\mathbb{R}},\mathcal{C}([-2\pi k_{1},0],\mathbb{C}))

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{j}(d\theta,dv,df)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}({n}_{1}^{+})}\right\}\delta_{\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime}}(d\theta,dv)\mathfrak{s}_{k_{1},k_{5}}\bigl(V_{j}^{\prime},\exp\bigl({i\Psi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\theta_{j})-i(n_{1}^{+}+1)\theta_{j}}\bigr)df\bigr)&\text{if $\sigma=1$},\\
\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}({n}_{1}^{+})}\right\}\delta_{\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime}}(d\theta,dv)\mathfrak{s}_{k_{1},k_{5}}\bigl(V_{j}^{\prime},df\bigr)&\text{if $\sigma=i$},\\
\end{cases}

(2.59)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{D}}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathfrak{m}_{j}.

This is the intensity of
\operatorname{Extre}_{n}
conditioned on
\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}
.
We show that
\operatorname{Extre}_{n}
can be compared to a Poisson process of the same intensity. Here and throughout, we write
\Pi(\Lambda)
for a Poisson process of intensity
\Lambda
.

To execute the proof, we will need to use some second moment machinery for events depending on the behavior of pairs of rays between times
k_{2}
and
n_{1}^{+}
. To do this, we leverage an important technical tool from
[
CMN18
]
.
Specifically
[
CMN18
]
introduces another, auxiliary Gaussian process
k\mapsto{Z}_{2^{k}}^{k_{2}}(\theta)
, defined for each
k_{2}
, which is shown to be close to
\varphi
. The process
{Z}_{2^{k}}^{k_{2}}(\theta)
is similar to the Gaussian random walk
G_{k}
, albeit with some changes to make the process simpler on short blocks. We will not need the exact form of the process; it is given by
[
CMN18
, (5.2)]
.
Define the event

\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}=\left\{\forall~\log_{2}k_{2}\leq k\leq\log_{2}{n}^{+}_{1},\theta\in[0,2\pi]:|{Z}_{2^{k}}^{k_{2}}(\theta)-2(\log\Phi^{*}_{2^{k}}(e^{i\theta})-\log\Phi^{*}_{k_{2}}(e^{i\theta}))|<\frac{1}{k_{3}}\right\}.

(2.60)

Using results of
[
CMN18
]
, this event is typical:

Lemma 2.19
.

For any
k_{3}

\liminf_{k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})=1.

Proof.

See
[
CMN18
, Proposition 5.2]
(note that the notation differs in that their
\left\{Z_{k}\right\}
is our
\left\{G_{k}\right\}
and their process
\{Z^{(2^{r},\Delta)}_{k}\}
is our
{Z}_{2^{k}}^{r}(\theta)
)

\lim_{k_{2}\to\infty}\sup_{2^{n}\geq k_{2}}\sup_{\theta\in[0,2\pi]}|{Z}_{2^{n}}^{k_{2}}(\theta)-\log\Phi^{*}_{2^{n}}(e^{i\theta})-\log\Phi^{*}_{k_{2}}(e^{i\theta})|=0\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

∎

Using this process
[
CMN18
]
are able to get good two-ray estimates that mimic branching random walk behavior. We need slightly different estimates, but at its heart they are small (albeit not easily verified) modifications to the estimates of
[
CMN18
]
. We summarize the estimates we need in the following. Define a function, with
x_{j}=\sqrt{2}H_{k_{2}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}(\theta_{j})
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})=\mathscr{Q}^{(k_{2},k_{3},n_{1}^{+})}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})

(2.61)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}\cap{\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}({n}_{1}^{+})}\cap{\mathscr{R}^{2}_{\ell}({n}_{1}^{+})}\cap\operatorname{EP}_{j}\cap\operatorname{EP}_{l}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})

where we define the event

\operatorname{EP}_{j}\coloneqq\{\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n_{1}^{+}}}(\theta_{j})\in[z_{j},z_{j}+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{k_{3}}}]\}.

The second moment estimates we import in the following. We show how these can be derived from modifications of
[
CMN18
]
in Appendix
B
.

Proposition 2.20
.

The two ray estimate satisfies the three following upper bounds. Let
\mathtt{k}
be the time of branching between
\theta_{j}
and
\theta_{\ell},
which we can take to be
\mathtt{k}\coloneqq\lfloor-\log_{2}|e^{i\theta_{j}}-e^{i\theta_{\ell}}|\rfloor.
Let
\mathtt{k}_{+}=\mathtt{k}_{+}(k_{2},n)
be defined by

\mathtt{k}_{+}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathtt{k}+3(e^{\sqrt{\log\log_{2}k_{2}}}+100(\log^{2}\mathtt{k}))&\text{if }\mathtt{k}\leq(\log_{2}n)/2,\\
\mathtt{k}+3(\log k_{2}/100)+100(\log^{2}(\log_{2}n-\mathtt{k}))&\text{if }\mathtt{k}>(\log_{2}n)/2.\end{cases}

The following second moment estimates hold

1.

(Time of branching
\mathtt{k}\leq(\log_{2}k_{2})/2
) For any
k_{3}
and all
k_{2}
large enough if
\mathtt{k}\leq(\log_{2}k_{2})/2,

\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})\leq(1+\eta_{k_{2},k_{3}})\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{z_{j}x_{j}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\frac{z_{\ell}x_{\ell}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}(z_{j}-x_{j}+z_{\ell}-x_{\ell})\bigr)

where
\eta_{k_{2},k_{3}}\to 0
as
k_{2}\to\infty
followed by
k_{3}
.
See Appendix Lemma
B.5
.

2.

(Time of branching
\mathtt{k}\leq(\log_{2}n)/2
) For all
k_{2}
sufficiently large and all
n\gg k_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})

\displaystyle\leq c(k_{3})\begin{cases}\frac{z_{j}x_{j}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\frac{z_{\ell}x_{\ell}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}(z_{j}-x_{j}+z_{\ell}-x_{\ell})\bigr)&\text{if }\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq\log_{2}k_{2},\\
\frac{z_{j}z_{\ell}x_{j}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\frac{2^{\mathtt{k}}}{n_{1}^{+}}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}(z_{j}-x_{j}+z_{\ell})\bigr)e^{-c(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{1/10}}&\text{if }\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq\log_{2}k_{2}.\end{cases}

See Appendix Lemma
B.4
.

3.

(Time of branching
(\log_{2}n)/2\leq\mathtt{k}\leq\log_{2}n_{1}^{+}
) For all
k_{2}
sufficiently large and all
n\gg k_{2}
,

\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})\leq c(k_{3})\frac{x_{j}z_{j}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\frac{2^{\mathtt{k}}}{n_{1}^{+}}e^{\sqrt{2}(z_{j}-x_{j}+z_{\ell})}e^{-c(\log_{2}n-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{1/10}}

See Appendix Lemma
B.6
.

4.

(Time of branching
\mathtt{k}\geq\log_{2}n_{1}^{+}
)
While useful for the range of
\mathtt{k}
described, this holds for all
\mathtt{k}
:

\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})\leq c(k_{3})\frac{z_{j}x_{j}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}(z_{j}-x_{j})\bigr).

This is a triviality which follows from Lemma
4.4
and bounding above the two-ray event by a one-ray event.

Remark 2.21
.

All these upper bounds also hold if in
\mathscr{Q}
we replace the ray event
\mathscr{R}_{j}^{p}(n_{1}^{+})
by one which only holds at times
H_{2^{k}}
(instead of a continuous barrier), see (
2.71
). Likewise, if we further replace the process

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{2^{k}}}
by
Z_{2^{k}}^{k_{2}}+\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}
, in the discrete barrier event,
the bound holds. In this case, we no longer need to work on the good event
\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}.

Using these second moment estimates, we turn to the first Poisson approximation.

Proposition 2.22
.

For any
k_{4},\dots,k_{7}
the restrictions of
\operatorname{Extre}_{n}
and
\Pi(\mathfrak{m})
to
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
satisfy

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extre}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}},\Pi(\mathfrak{m})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}})\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\xrightarrow[k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

To give the proof, we need to develop some first and second moment estimates for the process near the end of the ray.

2.8
Decoration Process estimates

We turn to giving some conditional first and second moment estimates for rays.

Lemma 2.23

(Coarse intensity bound)
.

The intensity measure
\mathfrak{m}_{j}
satisfies

\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})\leq\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})=c(k_{4})(1+o_{k_{4}})e^{T_{-}}\frac{V_{j}^{\prime}}{(T_{+}-T_{-})^{3/2}}e^{-\sqrt{2}V_{j}^{\prime}-(V_{j}^{\prime})^{2}/2(T_{+}-T_{-})}.

Proof.

To estimate
\mathfrak{m}_{j}
we first observe that (recalling
W_{j}^{o}
from (
2.54
))

\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})=\mathbb{P}(W_{j}^{o}\in[-k_{7},\infty)~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}.

Define the first moment

m_{1}=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]\cap\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\bigl\{\mathfrak{U}^{o}_{T_{+}}(\theta)-\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}V_{j}^{\prime}\in[-k_{7},\infty)\bigr\}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}(\theta)}\right\}~\big|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\biggr].

Hence we can bound by first moment on the event
\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})
,

\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})\leq m_{1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in[-2\pi k_{1},0]\cap\tfrac{2\pi}{4k_{5}}{\mathbb{Z}}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\biggl(\bigl\{\mathfrak{U}^{o}_{T_{+}}(\theta)-\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}V_{j}^{\prime}\in[-k_{7},\infty)\bigr\}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}(\theta)~\big|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\biggr).

We then apply Lemma
10.1
to each of these summands.
Hence we arrive at

m_{1}=(1+o_{k_{4}})k_{1}k_{5}e^{-(T_{+}-T_{-})}\frac{c(k_{7},\infty)V_{j}^{\prime}\sqrt{k_{4}}}{(T_{+}-T_{-})^{3/2}}e^{-\sqrt{2}V_{j}^{\prime}-(V_{j}^{\prime})^{2}/2(T_{+}-T_{-})},

which simplifies to the claimed result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.22
.

We shall use the Poisson process machinery stated in Theorem
A.1
, which we shall translate into this context. Due to the nature of the Poisson approximation, we shall use the nonatomicity of the derivative martingale proved in Theorem
1.6
. We let
\eta>0
be a parameter which shall be taken to
0
after
k_{2}
to establish the convergence in probability.

Step 1: restricting the measures.

As the measure
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}
is finite and nonatomic, we have that there is
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
sufficiently large that

\mathbb{P}(\max_{j=1,\dots,m}\mathscr{D}_{\infty}([\tfrac{2\pi(j-1)}{m},\tfrac{2\pi(j+1)}{m}])>\eta)\leq\eta,

(2.62)

where for the case
j=m
, we consider the arc as part of the torus.
Hence for all
k_{2}
sufficiently large, we have

\mathbb{P}(\max_{j=1,\dots,m}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}([\tfrac{2\pi(j-1)}{m},\tfrac{2\pi(j+1)}{m}])>\eta)\leq 2\eta.

Let
\mathcal{E}
denote the event that there exists a
j=1,\dots,m
such that

\mathscr{D}_{\infty}([\tfrac{2\pi(j-1)}{m},\tfrac{2\pi(j+1)}{m}]^{c})\leq\eta.

From (
2.62
) we have that

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\cap\{\mathscr{D}_{\infty}([0,2\pi])>2\eta\})\leq\eta.

As we can relate
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}([0,2\pi])
to intensity of both point processes, this in effect is saying that neither point process has any points.
Indeed, for either process
\Xi=\operatorname{Extre}_{n}
or
\Pi(\mathfrak{m})

\partial_{2}(\Xi\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}},0)\leq\mathbb{P}(\Xi(\Gamma_{k_{7}})\geq 1~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})\leq\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}).

Using Lemmas
4.4
and

2.23
,

\mathbb{E}(\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}))\leq\frac{c(k_{4})}{n_{1}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j}))-\log(k_{2})}\bigl(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})\bigr)_{+}

On taking
n\to\infty
, we conclude

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}(\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}))\leq c(k_{4})\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}([0,2\pi]).

And hence
we conclude

\limsup_{k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\cap\{\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extre}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}},\Pi(\mathfrak{m}))>4c(k_{4})\eta\})\leq\eta.

Hence it suffices to work on the event
\mathcal{E}^{c}
.

Step 2: setting up the Poisson approximation.

We let
\delta>0
be the same constant as in (
2.44
) (so that Proposition
2.12
applies), and define for
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}

\mathcal{B}_{j}=\left\{k\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}:d_{\mathbb{T}}(\theta_{j},\theta_{k})\leq n^{-1+8\delta}\right\},\quad{\text{and}}\quad B_{j}=\left\{k\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}:d_{\mathbb{T}}(\theta_{j},\theta_{k})\leq 4n^{-1+8\delta}\right\},

with
d_{\mathbb{T}}
the distance in the quotient space
{\mathbb{R}}/(2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}).
Recall (
2.47
) and (
2.54
).
By construction, for all
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}
,

\{(V_{k}^{\prime},D_{k}^{o}):k\in\mathcal{B}_{j}\}\quad\text{is $\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}}\bigr)$--conditional independent of}\quad\{(V_{k}^{\prime},D_{k}^{o}):k{\not\in}B_{j}\}.

Define for any
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}},

\Xi_{j}\coloneqq\delta_{(\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime},D_{j}^{o})}\times\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\},\,P_{j}\coloneqq\Xi_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}),\,S_{j}\coloneqq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{B}_{j}\setminus\{j\}}P_{i},\,\text{and}\quad L_{j}\coloneqq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}\setminus B_{j}}\mathfrak{m}_{i}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}).

We note that
\mathbb{E}(P_{i}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})=\mathfrak{m}_{i}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}).

Theorem
A.1
shows that there is a numerical constant
C>0
so that with
\Pi=\Pi(\mathfrak{q}),

\displaystyle\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}},\Pi(\mathfrak{m})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}})

(2.63)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\partial_{2}(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}~|~\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}},\Pi(\mathfrak{m})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}~|~\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C[\operatorname{Var}(\operatorname{Extre}_{n}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})+3\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})]^{3/2}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\biggl(\frac{\mathbb{E}(P_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})\mathbb{E}(S_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})+\mathbb{E}(S_{j}P_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})+(\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}))^{2}}{L_{j}^{2}}\biggr).

We also note that the left hand side of (
2.63
) is bounded by
1,
and hence it suffices to bound the right hand side of (
2.63
) on any
\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{{n}_{1}^{+}}\bigr)
–measurable event with probability tending to
1
. Nonetheless, as input, we need estimates for the
\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}\bigr)
–conditional expectation of
P_{j}
and for pairs
P_{j}
and
P_{i}
where
i\in\mathcal{B}_{j}.
We note that

\mathbb{E}[P_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}]=\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}).

To complete the proof of the proposition, we claim that there are events
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(n,(k_{j}))
so that on
\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{E}^{c}
the following hold

1.

\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})=O_{k_{2}}(1)
,

2.

\operatorname{Var}(\operatorname{Extr}_{n}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})=O_{k_{2}}(1)
,

3.

L_{j}^{2}\geq c(k_{4})\eta^{2}
,

and so that
\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathcal{F}}\right\}\xrightarrow[k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}1
.

Step 3: completing the proof on the good event
\mathcal{F}
.

Assuming the claim holds, we have reduced the problem to showing that
on the event
\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\biggl(\underbracket{\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})\mathbb{E}(S_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})}_{(i)}+\underbracket{\mathbb{E}(S_{j}P_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})}_{(ii)}+\underbracket{\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})^{2}}_{(iii)}\biggr)\xrightarrow[k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

(2.64)

For the term
(iii),
we note that
\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}),
which goes to
0
uniformly in
j
faster than any power of
\log k_{1}
(from Lemma
2.23
). Hence by the boundededness of
\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})
on
\mathcal{F}
, this term tends to
0.

For term
(i),
we will use the second moment machinery. To prepare for it, we use Lemma
2.23
and, bounding the sum on
z_{j},z_{\ell}
by an integral, which holds up to a constant depending on
k_{3}
, arrive at

\mathbb{E}[~(i)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq c(k_{3})\bigl(\frac{k_{1}}{k_{1}^{+}}\bigr)^{2}\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}\int_{z_{j},z_{\ell}}\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})\frac{z_{j}z_{\ell}}{(\log k_{1})^{3}}e^{-\sqrt{2}(z_{j}+z_{\ell})}dz_{j}dz_{\ell}.

(2.65)

We divide the angles
\theta_{\ell}
according to whether or not

(\log_{2}n_{1}^{+}-\mathtt{k}_{+})\geq q
or
(\log_{2}n_{1}^{+}-\mathtt{k}_{+})\leq q
for a
q
chosen below as
(\log k_{1})^{1/100}
.
For the former case we use the third case of Proposition
2.20
.
For the latter case, we just bound
\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})
by the probability of the
\theta_{j}
–ray event.
We also note that the number of
\theta_{j}
in this latter case is
(k_{1}^{+}/k_{1})e^{q+100(\log\log k_{1})^{2}}
.
Applying the bound from this case, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[~(i)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c(k_{2})}{n_{1}}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j}))-\log(k_{2})}\bigl(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)\bigr)

(2.66)

\displaystyle\times(\log k_{1})^{3}\biggl\{\sum_{\varkappa=q}^{\delta\log n}\bigl\{\exp\bigl(-c\varkappa^{1/10}\bigr)\bigr\}+\exp\bigl(q+C(\log\log k_{1})^{2}-c(\log k_{1})^{1/10}\bigr)\biggr\}.

Note that the stretched exponential gain in the second term is simply from the entropic envelope.
As the event we consider restricts the location of
\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})
to be positive, we may use that

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\frac{1}{n_{1}}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j}))-\log(k_{2})}\bigl(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)\bigr)_{+}

converges on taking
n\to\infty
. Taking
q=(\log k_{1})^{1/100}
, the sum is on the order of
e^{-\Omega((\log k_{1})^{1/1000})}
.
Hence on taking
k_{1}\to\infty
,

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[~(i)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\xrightarrow[k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

The analysis for

\mathbb{E}[~(ii)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]
is similar, but with one important modification.
For “bushes”

\theta_{j}
and
\theta_{\ell}
that branch at or before
n_{1}^{+}
(i.e.
\mathtt{k}\leq\log_{2}n_{1}^{+}
) we claim that the bound in (
2.65
) holds as well:
we bound each indicator
\mathbf{1}\left\{{W_{j}^{o}\in[-k_{7},\infty)}\right\}
above by a sum of indicators of ray event and then use Lemma
10.1
.
For
\theta_{\ell}
which are close to
\theta_{j}
, we use the same strategy, although we instead lose the precise dependence on
z_{\ell}
and instead have just the factor
e^{-\Omega(\log k_{1})^{1/10}}.
For
\ell
so that
\mathtt{k}\geq\log_{2}n_{1}^{+}
, this produces the bound:

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}c(k_{3})\bigl(\frac{k_{1}}{k_{1}^{+}}\bigr)^{2}\sum_{\ell}\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})\frac{z_{j}}{(\log k_{1})^{3/2}}e^{-\sqrt{2}(z_{j})-c(\log k_{1})^{1/10}}.

Using the final case of Proposition
2.20
gives an estimate which is
e^{-\Omega((\log k_{1})^{1/10})}
.

Step 4: Proof of claim.

Point 1.
We have that

\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}),

and so using Lemmas
2.23

and
4.4
,

\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq\frac{c(k_{4})}{n_{1}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j}))-\log(k_{2})}\bigl(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)\bigr)_{+}.

This converges on taking
n\to\infty
almost surely to

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq c(k_{4})\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)\,d\theta,

(2.67)

and so remains bounded almost surely by Theorem
1.6
.

Point 2.
It suffices to bound the conditional second moment, which is to say

\operatorname{Var}(\operatorname{Extre}_{n}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})\leq\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{2}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}].

We then have

\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Extre}_{n}^{2}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}]\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[P_{j}S_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}]+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\sum_{\ell\not\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}\mathfrak{m}_{j}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})

The first term is nothing but
\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+})
which we have already controlled. The second term we controlled earlier in part
(ii)
above.
The third term we estimate in the same fashion as
(i)
in (
2.66
).

Point 3.
We let
\text{Arc}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,m
be
a Lipschitz function of the torus
{\mathbb{R}}/2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}
which is
1
on the complement of
[\tfrac{i-1}{m}2\pi,\tfrac{i+1}{m}2\pi]^{c}
and
0
on
[\tfrac{i-0.5}{m}2\pi,\tfrac{i+0.5}{m}2\pi]
.
Extend
\text{Arc}_{i}
to a function of
\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}
by setting
\text{Arc}_{i}(x,y,z)=\text{Arc}_{i}(x).

Then for each
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}
, for all
n
sufficiently large there is an
i\in 1,\dots,m
so that

L_{j}\geq\mathfrak{m}(\text{Arc}_{i}).

Hence it suffices to show that each of these
\mathfrak{m}(\text{Arc}_{i})
satisfies the claimed bound.

Now we claim that in fact

\mathfrak{m}(\text{Arc}_{i})

concentrates around its
\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}
–conditional mean and that its conditional mean has the claimed lower bound. In fact, we shall need this concentration argument at a later point as well, and so we formulate a general statement here.

Lemma 2.24
.

Let
f
be a non-negative, Lipschitz function from the torus
{\mathbb{R}}/(2\pi{\mathbb{Z}})\to{\mathbb{R}}
bounded above by
1
. Extend it to a function of
{\mathbb{R}}/(2\pi{\mathbb{Z}})\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathcal{C}([-2\pi k_{1},0],{\mathbb{C}})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}
by setting
f(x,y,z)=f(x)
.
Then there is positive constant
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}(k_{1},k_{4},k_{5},k_{7})
which is bounded (above and away from
0
, for
k_{7}
large), uniformly
in
k_{1}
, so that

\mathfrak{m}(f)-\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(f)\times\mathcal{H}\xrightarrow[k_{3},k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

The constant
\mathcal{H}
is given in (
2.68
).

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.24
.

If
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}(f)=0
then it suffices to show
\mathfrak{m}(f)
tends to
0
.
This follows directly from (
2.67
), and so it suffices to consider the case that
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}(f)>0.

The proof follows from a second moment method, restricted to the event
\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}
,
which complicates the first moment estimate.
We first compute the first moment without the restriction to
\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}
.
The conditional first moment of
\mathfrak{m}(f)
is given by

\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}(f)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}f(\theta_{j})\mathbb{E}[~\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})p_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

where
p_{j}=p(V_{j}^{\prime})=\mathbb{P}(W_{j}^{o}\in[-k_{7},\infty)~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}).

The function
p
is inexplicit and depends on
k_{4},k_{5},k_{7}
. Using Lemma
4.4
,

\mathbb{E}[~\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})p_{j}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq\frac{1}{n_{1}}\bigl(\sqrt{2}H_{k_{2}}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})\bigr)e^{\sqrt{\beta/2}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})-\log k_{2}+o_{k_{2}}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\int_{J}\frac{k_{1}^{+}}{k_{1}}e^{\sqrt{2}z}p(z)z\,dz

The interval
J
is
[(\log k_{1}^{+})^{1/10},(\log k_{1}^{+})^{9/10}]
. The constant
\mathcal{H}
is given by

\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\int_{J}\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}ze^{\sqrt{2}z}\biggr)\times\biggl(\frac{k_{1}^{+}}{k_{1}}p(z)\biggr)\,dz.

(2.68)

Note that the density that appears is
bounded by Lemma
2.23
(using
T_{-}=\log(k_{1}/k_{1}^{+})
and
T_{+}=\log k_{1}
), and we obtain

\mathcal{H}=c(k_{4})(1+o_{k_{4}})\int_{J}e^{-z^{2}/(2\log k_{1}^{+})}\frac{z^{2}dz}{(\log k_{1}^{+})^{3/2}}\leq c^{\prime}(k_{4}).

(2.69)

We return to this bound in a moment, but note that by combining the above, we have the upper bound for the conditional first moment:

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}(f)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(\theta)\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)d\theta\times\mathcal{H}.

(2.70)

Before proceeding we
show that
\mathcal{H}
is bounded below uniformly in
n,k_{1}
, at least for
k_{7}
large enough. Assume not. Take
f=1
.
Then necessarily,

\liminf_{k_{1}\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathfrak{m}(f)=0
, in probability.
Using Proposition
2.15
, we conclude then
that, at least for
k_{7}
large,

\liminf_{k_{1}\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\operatorname{Ext}_{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}=0,\;\textrm{in probability}.

But this contradicts the tightness in Theorem
1.3
.

To produce a lower bound for the first moment, we introduce two comparison measures
\mathfrak{m}_{j}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{j}^{\prime\prime}
, which serve as comparisons to
\mathfrak{m}_{j}
, and which are modified by slightly adjusting the ray event
\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})
. We introduce two ray events, which will only be used for this argument,

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}=\mathscr{U}(\theta_{j})\bigcap\{\forall~t\in[H_{k_{2}},H_{n_{1}^{+}}]\cap\{H_{2^{k}}:k\in{\mathbb{N}}\},~:~\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{j})\in\sqrt{2}[A_{t}^{5/2,-},A_{t}^{5/2,+}]\}

(2.71)

\displaystyle\bigcap\{-V_{j}^{\prime}\in[(\log k_{1})^{0.49},(\log k_{1})^{0.51}\},\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime\prime}=\mathscr{U}(\theta_{j})\bigcap\{\forall~t\in[H_{k_{2}},H_{n_{1}^{+}}]\cap\{H_{2^{k}}:k\in{\mathbb{N}}\},~:~Z^{k_{2}}_{2^{n}}(\theta_{j})\in\sqrt{2}[A_{t}^{2,-},A_{t}^{2,+}]\}

\displaystyle\bigcap\{-V_{j}^{\prime}\in[(\log k_{1})^{0.49},(\log k_{1})^{0.51}\}.

Let
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}
be the sum of all the
\mathfrak{m}_{j}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{j}^{\prime\prime}
respectively (see (
2.59
)).
From Lemma
4.4
and a direct first moment estimate

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[|\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}(f)-\mathfrak{m}(f)|~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq o_{k_{2}}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(f),\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

and hence on taking
k_{2}\to\infty,
this tends to
0
almost surely.
Moreover, on the event
\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}
, we have that
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\geq\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}
, and hence combining this with the display above

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}(f)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(f)\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}].

To evaluate this expectation, we lower bound the expectation without the restriction to the event
\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}
and then argue the event can be removed.

Using Lemma
C.1
and the Girsanov transformation,
we also can give a sharp estimate for the ray probability.
However, this is not given as a density estimate, but rather with a restriction of the endpoint
V_{j}^{\prime}
to land in an interval of length
1/\sqrt{k_{3}}
(as in Proposition
2.20
). Hence we partition the interval
J
into a grid
\bar{J}
of separation
1/\sqrt{k_{3}}
, whose elements are parametrized by
z
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(f)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime\prime}}\right\}p_{j}~\big|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\frac{1}{n_{1}}\bigl(\sqrt{2}H_{k_{2}}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})\bigr)e^{\sqrt{\beta/2}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})-\log k_{2}+o_{k_{2}}}

\displaystyle\times(1+\eta_{k_{2},k_{3}})\frac{k_{1}^{+}}{k_{1}}\sum_{z\in\bar{J}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}ze^{\sqrt{2}z}\times\min_{x\in[0,1/\sqrt{k_{3}}]}\frac{p(z+x)}{\sqrt{k_{3}}},

where the factor
\eta_{k_{2},k_{3}}\to 0
as
k_{2}\to\infty
followed by
k_{3}\to\infty
.
In Lemma
10.2
we show that the function
p(z)
satisfies the estimate

p(w+x)=(e^{\sqrt{2}x}+o_{k_{1}})p(w)\quad\text{uniformly over $|x|\leq 1$ and $w\in J$. }

(2.72)

Hence, we can uniformly approximate the sum over
z
by an integral and arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(f)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

\displaystyle\geq(1+o_{k_{3}})\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\frac{1}{n_{1}}\bigl(\sqrt{2}H_{k_{2}}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})\bigr)e^{\sqrt{\beta/2}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{j})-\log k_{2}}\frac{k_{1}^{+}}{k_{1}}\int_{J}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}ze^{\sqrt{2}z}p(z)\,dz.

This is exactly
\mathcal{H}
, and so we have

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(f)~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\geq(1+o_{k_{3}})\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)\,d\theta\times\mathcal{H}.

(2.73)

To complete the first moment analysis, it suffices to show that

\limsup_{k_{2}\to\infty}\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(f)\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}^{c}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\xrightarrow[k_{3}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0,

as then from (
2.73
) and (
2.70
),

\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}(f)\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)\,d\theta\times\mathcal{H}\xrightarrow[k_{3},k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

(2.74)

We shall show using the second moment machinery in Proposition
2.20
(and see Remark
2.21
), that

\limsup_{k_{2}\to\infty}\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\bigl(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(\text{Arc}_{i})\bigr)^{2}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]<\infty\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

(2.75)

And hence it follows by Cauchy–Schwarz and Lemma
2.19

\limsup_{k_{2}\to\infty}\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(\text{Arc}_{i})\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}^{c}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\xrightarrow[k_{3}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

We turn to the conditional second moment, and note that this argument will give the same upper bound as was needed for the second moment of
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}
in (
2.75
)
(see Remark
2.21
):

\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{m}(f)^{2}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\leq\sum_{j,\ell}f(\theta_{j})f(\theta_{\ell})\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{R}_{\ell}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}p_{j}p_{\ell}~\bigg|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr),

(2.76)

where
p_{j}=p(V_{j}^{\prime})=\mathbb{P}(W_{j}^{o}\in[-k_{7},\infty)~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}})
and the sum is over all
j,\ell\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}.

We divide the angle pairs
(\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell})
into two classes
\operatorname{NR}
and
\operatorname{FR}
, where the latter means
\theta_{j}
and
\theta_{\ell}
have branchpoint
\mathtt{k}
(in the notation of Proposition
2.20
) less than
\tfrac{1}{2}\log_{2}k_{2}
. We let
\operatorname{NR}
be all other pairs.
For the well–separated angles
\operatorname{FR}
, we can afford only multiplicative errors of the form
1+o_{k_{3}}
. Now Proposition
2.20
gives such an estimate, albeit only after asking the endpoint to be in a bin of size
(1/\sqrt{k_{3}})
.

Thus by partitioning the two-ray expectation in (
2.76
) according to the values of
V_{j}^{\prime}
and
V_{\ell}^{\prime}
(using bins of size
(1/\sqrt{k_{3}})
so to apply Proposition
2.20
), we have
that for
(\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell})\in\operatorname{FR}
the contribution to (
2.76
) is bounded above by

\sum_{z_{j},z_{\ell}}\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})\times\max_{u_{j},u_{\ell}\leq 1/\sqrt{k_{3}}}p(z_{j}+u_{j})p(z_{\ell}+u_{\ell}).

By (
2.72
),
the maximum value of
p
over a short interval of length
1/\sqrt{k_{3}}
is its average value over the same interval up to an error
o_{k_{3}}
.
Using the first case of Proposition
2.20
, we conclude with that notation

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell}\in\operatorname{FR}}\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j_{1}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j_{2}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}p_{j_{1}}p_{j_{2}}~\bigg|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr)
