\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta_{k_{2},k_{3}}+o_{k_{3}})\sum_{\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell}}\int_{J}\int_{J}\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{z_{j}x_{j}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\frac{z_{\ell}x_{\ell}k_{2}}{n_{1}^{+}}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}(z_{j}-x_{j}+z_{\ell}-x_{\ell})\bigr)p(z_{1})p(z_{2})dz_{1}dz_{2},

where the sum is over all
\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell}
in the arc and
J
is as before.
Thus on taking
n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell}\in\operatorname{FR}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j_{1}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j_{2}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}p_{j_{1}}p_{j_{2}}~\bigg|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr)

(2.77)

\displaystyle\leq(1+o_{k_{3}})\biggl(\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(\theta)\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)d\theta\times\mathcal{H}\biggr)^{2}.

For the near terms, we need to use all the cases of the 2-ray bound Proposition
2.20
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell}\in\operatorname{NR}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j_{1}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j_{2}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}p_{j_{1}}p_{j_{2}}~\bigg|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq c(k_{3})\sum_{\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell}\in\operatorname{NR}}\int_{J}\int_{J}\mathscr{Q}(\theta_{j},x_{j},z_{j};\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell},z_{\ell})p(z_{j})p(z_{\ell})dz_{j}dz_{\ell}.

We then partition these near terms into which case of Proposition
2.20
the pairs land. That is we define the sets:

1.

\operatorname{NR}_{1}(\theta_{j})
are all those
\ell
so that
\mathtt{k}\geq(\log_{2}k_{2})/2
but
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq\log_{2}k_{2}
.

2.

\operatorname{NR}_{2}(\theta_{j})
are all those
\ell
not in
\operatorname{NR}_{1}(\theta_{j})
but so that
\mathtt{k}\leq(\log_{2}n)/2
.

3.

\operatorname{NR}_{3}(\theta_{j})
are all those
\ell
not in
\operatorname{NR}_{i}(\theta_{j})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
but so that
\mathtt{k}\leq\log_{2}n_{1}^{+}
.

4.

\operatorname{NR}_{4}(\theta_{j})
are all those
\ell
not in
\operatorname{NR}_{i}(\theta_{j})
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
.

In all cases, we have sharp dependence on
x_{j}
and
z_{j}
, and so we can integrate over
z_{j}
to give exactly
\mathcal{H}
up to an error depending on
k_{3}
. For the terms in
\operatorname{NR}_{1}(\theta_{j})
, we also have sharp dependence on
x_{\ell}
and
z_{\ell}
up to a multiplicative error, and hence we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\sum_{\ell\in\operatorname{NR}_{1}(\theta_{j})}\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j_{1}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j_{2}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}p_{j_{1}}p_{j_{2}}~\bigg|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq c(k_{3})\biggl(\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)\,d\theta\times\mathcal{H}\biggr)\times\max_{\theta}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}([\theta,\theta+1/\sqrt{k_{2}}]).

By nonatomicity of
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}
,

\max_{\theta}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}([\theta,\theta+1/\sqrt{k_{2}}])\xrightarrow[k_{2}\to\infty]{\operatorname{a.s.}}0.

For the terms of the second type, the bound in Proposition
2.20
loses the dependence in
x_{\ell},
but we gain a factor due to the entropic barrier, which is summable

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}\sum_{\ell\in\operatorname{NR}_{2}(\theta_{j})}\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j_{1}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j_{2}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}p_{j_{1}}p_{j_{2}}~\bigg|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq c(k_{3})\biggl(\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)\,d\theta\times\mathcal{H}\biggr)\times e^{-c(\log k_{2})^{1/10}}.

The same bound holds for terms of the third type, using the entropic barrier gain, but now gaining a factor
e^{-c(\log k_{1})^{1/10}}
. Finally for the terms of fourth type, we use Lemma
2.23
, due to which we gain the same
e^{-c(\log k_{1})^{1/10}}
. In all, we have that the sum over all
\operatorname{NR}
pairs satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{j},\theta_{\ell}\in\operatorname{NR}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl(\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}_{j_{1}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j_{2}}^{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\right\}p_{j_{1}}p_{j_{2}}~\bigg|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}\biggr)\leq o_{k_{2}}\biggl(\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)\,d\theta\times\mathcal{H}\biggr).

Combining this with (
2.77
), we have that

\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}\operatorname{Var}(\mathfrak{m}(f)\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{Z}_{k_{2}}}\right\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{3},k_{2}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

This together with (
2.74
) proves the lemma.
∎

2.9
Third Poisson approximation: concentration of the intensity

The final Poisson approximation replaces the intensity
\mathfrak{m}
by (essentially) its
\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}
–conditional expectation. This is done by a first and second moment computation.
The measure to which we compare
\mathfrak{m}
is the one given in the introduction (
1.21
), namely with
I(v)
as in (
1.21
)
and
\mathfrak{s}
as in (
2.55
):

\mathfrak{n}(\theta,v,f)\coloneqq\mathscr{D}_{k_{2}}(\theta)d\theta\times I(v)dv\times\mathfrak{p}(v,df)\quad\text{where}\quad\mathfrak{p}(v,f)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathfrak{s}_{k_{1},k_{5}}(v,e^{i\xi}df)\tfrac{d\xi}{2\pi},&\text{ if $\sigma=1$,}\\
\mathfrak{s}_{k_{1},k_{5}}(v,df),&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

(2.78)

Proposition 2.25
.

For any
k_{7}
the restrictions of
\Pi(\mathfrak{m})
and
\Pi(\mathfrak{n})
to
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
satisfy

\partial_{2}(\Pi(\mathfrak{m})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\Pi(\mathfrak{n})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{3},k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Proof.

In the first step, we replace the measure
\mathfrak{m}
by one in which the decoration is averaged, that is, we compare to

\overline{\mathfrak{m}}(\theta,v,f)\coloneqq\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{D}}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}({n}_{1}^{+})}\right\}\delta_{\theta_{j},V_{j}^{\prime}}(d\theta,dv)\mathfrak{p}\bigl(v,df\bigr).

(2.79)

Note that this is identical to
\mathfrak{m}
in the case
\sigma=i
.
The intensities
\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})
and
\overline{\mathfrak{m}}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})
are tight in all parameters (see (
2.67
))
\{n,k_{1},\dots,k_{6}\}
.
Using Theorem
A.2
, it thus suffices to show
d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\mathfrak{m},\overline{\mathfrak{m}})\to 0
to conclude that

\partial_{2}(\Pi(\mathfrak{m})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\Pi(\overline{\mathfrak{m}})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{3},k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Letting
F
be the functions in
\mathcal{C}((-2\pi k_{1},0),{\mathbb{C}})
with max modulus in
[e^{-k_{7}},e^{k_{7}}]
, we can bound

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\mathfrak{m},\overline{\mathfrak{m}})

\displaystyle\leq(\mathfrak{m}(\Gamma_{k_{7}})+\overline{\mathfrak{m}}(\Gamma_{k_{7}}))

\displaystyle\times\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{D}}_{n/k_{1}}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}({n}_{1}^{+})}\right\}d_{\operatorname{BL}}\biggl(\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathfrak{s}_{k_{1},k_{5}}(V_{j}^{\prime},e^{i\xi-\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}}df)\tfrac{d\xi}{2\pi},\mathfrak{s}_{k_{1},k_{5}}(V_{j}^{\prime},e^{i\alpha_{j}-\sqrt{4/\beta}V_{j}^{\prime}}df)\biggr),

where
\alpha_{j}\coloneqq\Psi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\theta_{j})-(n_{1}^{+}+1)\theta_{j}
and the bounded Lipschitz norm is restricted to
F
.
We show in Corollary
10.5
that this distance is bounded by
\mathcal{O}(e^{-(\log k_{1})^{19/20}})p(V_{j}^{\prime})
where
p(V_{j}^{\prime})=\mathbb{P}(W_{j}^{o}\in[-k_{7},\infty)~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}).
Thus like in (
2.67
)

d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\mathfrak{m},\overline{\mathfrak{m}})\xrightarrow[k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

For the final step, we show

\partial_{2}(\iota\#(\Pi(\mathfrak{n})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}})~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}},\iota\#(\Pi(\overline{\mathfrak{m}})\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}})~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}})\xrightarrow[k_{3},k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

We again use Theorem
A.2
to reduce this to controlling the bounded Lipschitz norm on
\Gamma_{k_{7}}
. Let
\bar{\iota}\#\mathfrak{n}
and
\bar{\iota}\#\overline{\mathfrak{m}}
be the restriction of
\iota\#(\mathfrak{n}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}})
and
\iota\#(\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\cap\Gamma_{k_{7}})
to
[0,2\pi]\times\mathcal{C}([-2\pi k_{1},0],{\mathbb{C}})
,
and note that the restriction to
\mathcal{C}([-2\pi k_{1},0],{\mathbb{C}})

is identical for both processes except for for a additive random variable, taking value in a compact (
k_{7}
-dependent) set.
From Corollary
A.3
and the fact that after the push forward by
\iota
, the second coordinate in
\Gamma
is continuously determined by the third, there is a finite list of non-negative Lipschitz functions
\{f_{j}\}
, depending on
k_{7}
, for which it suffices to show that

\bar{\iota}\#\mathfrak{n}(f_{j})-\bar{\iota}\#\overline{\mathfrak{m}}(f_{j})\xrightarrow[k_{3},k_{2},k_{1},n\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

(2.80)

(We note that the use of
\iota
was precisely to reduce the collections of functions
\{f_{j}\}
to a collection that does not depend on
k_{1}
.)
Now, (
2.80
) follows from Lemma
2.24
.
∎

3
Interpolation–based regularity arguments

In this section we give a proofs of Proposition
2.6
and Proposition
2.14
; both rely on certain
a priori
regularity properties of
\varphi_{n},
but the second one additionally uses a substantial probabilistic input, Proposition
4.8
in the case
\sigma=1
. The
\sigma=i
case is substantially simpler; in effect the regularity is much better for imaginary
\sigma
owing to the monotonicity of the Prüfer phases. We introduce the following notation for working with the case of real
\sigma.
For any
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}},
define (with
\delta_{\beta}
given by half of
\delta
from Proposition
4.8
)

J(\theta)\coloneqq\left\{\theta^{\prime}\in\frac{2\pi}{4k_{5}n}{\mathbb{Z}}:|\theta^{\prime}-\theta|\leq\frac{\pi}{nk_{5}^{1-\delta_{\beta}}}\right\}.

(3.1)

The deterministic result we need is the following:

Lemma 3.1
.

For
\sigma=i,

and any
\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta

\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})\leq\varphi_{n}(\theta)+(n+1)(\theta-\theta^{\prime}).

For
\sigma=1
if
\sup_{\theta}\varphi_{n}(\theta)\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}+k_{6},
then there is an absolute constant
C
so that for all
n,k_{5}
sufficiently large with respect to
k_{6},
and all
\theta\in[0,2\pi]

\displaystyle\max_{|\theta^{\prime}-\theta|<\frac{2\pi}{4nk_{5}^{1-\delta_{\beta}}}}\biggl\{e^{\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}}\biggr\}\leq\biggl(\frac{2k_{5}}{2k_{5}-1}\biggr)\cdot\max_{\theta^{\prime}\in J(\theta)}\biggl\{e^{\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}}\biggr\}+\frac{Ce^{k_{6}}}{k_{5}^{\delta_{\beta}}}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\min_{|\theta^{\prime}-\theta|<\frac{2\pi}{4nk_{5}^{1-\delta_{\beta}}}}\biggl\{e^{\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}}\biggr\}\geq\biggl(\frac{2k_{5}}{2k_{5}-1}\biggr)\cdot\min_{\theta^{\prime}\in J(\theta)}\biggl\{e^{\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}}\biggr\}-\frac{Ce^{k_{6}}}{k_{5}^{\delta_{\beta}}}.

Proof.

For the first claim, we use that the Prüfer phases
\theta\mapsto\Psi_{k}(\theta)
are monotone increasing in
\theta
, see the discussion in the beginning of Section

2.1
, and recall that
\varphi_{n}(\theta)=\Psi_{n}(\theta)-(n+1)\theta
.
For the second claim, we apply Theorem
8.2
to the polynomial
Q
such that
|Q(e^{i\theta})|^{2}=e^{\varphi_{n}(\theta)}
for all real
\theta
. This has degree
n
and the theorem applies with
m=2k_{5}
and
b\asymp k_{5}^{1-\delta_{\beta}}.

∎

To take advantage of this deterministic result, we then use some first and second moment estimates which when combined with Lemma
3.1
imply Proposition
2.14
. The first of these is essentially a triviality that shows that we can disregard near maxima that occur near the boundary of an interval
\widehat{I}_{j},
more specifically those mesh points that are not contained in
K=\cup_{j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}}I_{j}
(recall the definition of
I_{j}
in (
2.24
)).

Lemma 3.2
.

For all
k_{2},k_{4},k_{5},k_{6}

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{4k_{5}n}\mathbf{1}\{\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n}\not\in K\}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]\xrightarrow[k_{1}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Proof.

For any
I_{j}
the fraction of angles
\theta\in I_{j}
so that
\theta\not\in K
vanishes like
\tfrac{\log k_{1}}{k_{1}}
as
k_{1}\to\infty.
In particular, the left-hand-side of the display tends to
0
like
O_{k_{p},p\geq 2}((\log k_{1})/k_{1})
(compare with the proof of Proposition
2.5
).
∎

From here we can also give a quick proof of Proposition
2.6
:

Proof of Proposition
2.6
.

We focus on the harder case
\sigma=1
, since the proof for
\sigma=i
is immediate.
We first observe that on the event
\mathscr{G}_{n},
globally
\theta\mapsto\varphi_{n}(\theta)
is bounded by
k_{6}
, and hence we always have
W_{j}\leq k_{6}
for any
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}.

Suppose that
\widehat{W}_{j}\geq-k_{7}
, and hence there is a
\theta
in a neighborhood of
\widehat{I}_{j}
at which
\varphi_{n}(\theta)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}\geq-k_{7}.

Using the first conclusion of Lemma
3.1
, there must be a
\theta^{\prime}\in J(\theta)
at which

e^{\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}}\geq\biggl(\frac{2k_{5}-1}{2k_{5}}\biggr)\biggl(e^{-k_{7}}-\frac{Ce^{k_{6}}}{k_{5}^{\delta_{\beta}}}\biggr).

By virtue of Lemma
3.2
, we may assume that
\theta^{\prime}\in I_{j}.
Making
k_{5}
large, we conclude

\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}\geq-k_{7}-o_{k_{5}}(1).

As
k_{6}
is much larger that
k_{7},
this concludes the proof.
∎

The second probabilistic input we need in order to prove Proposition
2.14
is that on the finite sets
J(\theta)
for
\theta\in I_{j}
at which
\mathscr{L}(\theta)
occurs, the process
\varphi
can be taken nearly constant (or more specifically its oscillation is no more than
k_{5}^{-\delta_{\beta}}
) simultaneously for all
\theta
for which
\mathscr{L}(\theta)
holds. Define the event

\mathscr{J}(\theta)\coloneqq\biggl\{\bigl(\max_{\theta^{\prime}\in J(\theta)}\{\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})\}-\min_{\theta^{\prime}\in J(\theta)}\{\varphi_{n}(\theta^{\prime})\}\bigr)\leq k_{5}^{-\delta_{\beta}}\biggr\}.

(3.2)

Thus we show:

Lemma 3.3
.

For all
k_{6}

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{4k_{5}n}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}({n}_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{O}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\mathscr{O}^{\Psi}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{J}^{c}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\xrightarrow[k_{5},k_{4},k_{2},k_{1}\to\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0.

Proposition
2.14
follows immediately from Lemmas
3.3
and
3.1
in the case
\sigma=1
.

Proof.

We may additionally work on the event
\mathscr{G}_{n}^{1},
using which we may replace
\varphi_{n}(\theta_{\ell}+\tfrac{\theta}{n})
by
\mathfrak{U}_{T_{+}}(\theta)
.
The main technical work is contained in Proposition
4.8
.
Under this proposition, we have an estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}({n}_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{O}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\mathscr{O}^{\Psi}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{J}^{c}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}]

\displaystyle\leq C(k_{6})k_{5}^{-(1+2\delta_{\beta})(1-\delta_{\beta})}\frac{\bigl(\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\varphi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\bigr)\exp\bigl(\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\varphi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})-2m_{n_{1}^{+}}\bigr)}{k_{1}^{+}(\log k_{1})^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}({n}_{1}^{+})\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{n_{1}^{+}}].

We have used here that
\varphi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})=\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}}-n\theta_{j})
, the initial conditions from (
2.43
).
Bounding above the probability, which proceeds in the same fashion as (
2.19
)–(
2.23
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{k_{1},n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{4k_{5}n}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{L}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\mathscr{R}_{j}^{2}({n}_{1}^{+})\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{O}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\cap\mathscr{O}^{\Psi}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{J}^{c}(\tfrac{\pi j}{2k_{5}n})\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathscr{G}_{n}\}~|~\mathscr{F}_{k_{2}}]

\displaystyle\leq C(k_{6})k_{5}^{-\delta_{\beta}+2\delta_{\beta}^{2}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(6k_{6}+\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta))-\log(k_{2})}\bigl(C_{\beta}k_{6}+\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)\bigr)d\theta

\displaystyle+k_{5}^{-\delta_{\beta}+2\delta_{\beta}^{2}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)+k_{6})-\log(k_{2})}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)\not\in[s_{k_{2}}^{-},s_{k_{2}}^{+}]}\right\}\bigl|c_{\beta}k_{6}+\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta)\bigr|d\theta.

Using Theorem
1.6
we have on sending
k_{2}\to\infty
that this upper bound converges almost surely.
On afterward sending
k_{5}
to infinity, the result follows.
∎

4
Bessel bridges

In what follows
\theta_{0}
will be a fixed angle in
[0,2\pi].

Recall from (
2.7
) that for
\sigma\in\left\{1,i\right\},

G_{k+1}(\theta_{0})=G_{k}(\theta_{0})-2\Re\{\sigma\bigl(\tfrac{Z_{k}}{\beta_{k}}\bigr)e^{i\Psi_{k}(\theta_{0})}\},\quad G_{0}(\theta_{0})=\theta_{0}.

Recall further, see (
2.5
), (
2.7
),
the complex Brownian motion
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathbb{C}}_{t}(\theta_{0})
with the
normalization
[\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{\mathbb{C}}(\theta_{0}),\overline{\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{\mathbb{C}}(\theta_{0})}]=2t,
and the standard real Brownian motion
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}=\Re(\sigma\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{\mathbb{C}}(\theta_{0}))
for any
t\geq 0,
so that
G_{k}(\theta_{0})=\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k}}(\theta_{0})
for any
k\geq 0.

We shall work conditionally on the endpoints
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
of various intervals
[t_{0},t_{1}]
, after which the process is a standard Brownian bridge. Further conditioning this bridge to lie below the line
t\mapsto\alpha t,
on the interval
[t_{0},t_{1}]
the process
\alpha t-\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
has the law of a
3
–dimensional Bessel process bridge
. It remains a semimartingale after this conditioning (with respect to the appropriate augmented filtration), and moreover it is a strong solution to an SDE. We record these facts and some distributional facts about this Bessel bridge in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(B_{t}:t\geq 0)
be a standard real Brownian motion.
For any
\alpha,c_{0},c_{1}>0
and any
0<t_{0}<t_{1},

let
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}:t\in[t_{0},t_{1}])
be the strong solution of

\mathfrak{X}_{t}-\mathfrak{X}_{t_{0}}=\alpha(t-t_{0})+(B_{t}-B_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{X}_{s}-\alpha s}-\frac{\mathfrak{X}_{s}-(\alpha s-c_{1})}{t_{1}-s}\right)\,ds,\quad\mathfrak{X}_{t_{0}}=\alpha t_{0}-c_{0}.

Then
(\mathfrak{Z}_{t}:t\in[t_{0},t_{1}])
has the same law
as
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}:t\in[t_{0},t_{1}])

when conditioning on

\mathfrak{Z}_{t_{0}}=\alpha t_{0}-c_{0},\quad\mathfrak{Z}_{t_{1}}=\alpha t_{1}-c_{1},\quad{\text{and}}\quad\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\leq\alpha t\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in[t_{0},t_{1}].

For any
t\in(t_{0},t_{1}),
the density of

y_{t}=\alpha t-\mathfrak{X}_{t}

is given by

f(u)=Z(t_{1}-t_{0},c_{0},c_{1})\sinh\biggl(\frac{c_{0}u}{t-t_{0}}\biggr)\sinh\biggl(\frac{uc_{1}}{t_{1}-t}\biggr)\exp\biggl(-\frac{u^{2}}{2(t-t_{0})}-\frac{u^{2}}{2(t_{1}-t)}\biggr),\quad u\geq 0,

where
Z(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is a normalizing constant (given explicitly in the proof text).
Provided
(c_{0}^{2}+c_{1}^{2})\leq(t_{1}-t_{0})
we therefore have the estimates, with
s=\min\{t-t_{0},t_{1}-t\}
, for some absolute constant
C

\mathbb{P}(\alpha t-\mathfrak{X}_{t}\leq x)\leq\frac{Cx^{3}}{s^{3/2}}\quad\text{for all}\quad x\leq\sqrt{s}.

Furthermore, with
i\in\{0,1\}
depending on
whichever achieves the minimum of
\min\{|t-t_{i}|\}
in the definition of
s
,

\mathbb{P}(\alpha t-\mathfrak{X}_{t}\in du)\leq Ce^{-(u-c_{i})^{2}/(2s)}\frac{u^{2}du}{s^{3/2}}\quad\text{for all}\quad u\geq 0.

(4.1)

Proof.

See
[
RY99
, Chapter XI]
for a good exposition.
Setting
X_{t}=\alpha t-\mathfrak{Z}_{t},
the SDE reduces to a standard result on a Brownian motion conditioned to stay positive (c.f.
[
RY99
, Exercise XI.3.11.2]
,
[
Rob13
]
).
The density
f

is given in
[
RY99
, Chapter XI.3]
. The normalizing constant
Z
is given by

Z=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{t_{1}-t_{0}}{(t-t_{0})(t_{1}-t)}}\exp\biggl(-\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{2(t-t_{0})}+\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{2(t_{1}-t_{0})}-\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2(t_{1}-t)}+\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2(t_{1}-t_{0})}\biggr)\!\operatorname{csch}\biggl(\frac{c_{0}c_{1}}{t_{1}-t_{0}}\biggr).

Under the assumption on
c_{0}^{2}+c_{1}^{2}
and using the numerical inequality
x\leq\sinh(x)
for all
x\geq 0
we may bound above the normalizing constant
Z

Z\leq C\biggl(\frac{t_{1}-t_{0}}{c_{0}c_{1}}\biggr)\biggl(\frac{t_{1}-t_{0}}{(t-t_{0})(t_{1}-t)}\biggr)^{1/2}\exp\biggl(-\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{2(t-t_{0})}-\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2(t_{1}-t)}\biggr).

Using
\sinh(x)\leq xe^{x}
for all
x\geq 0
, we arrive at the density bound for another absolute constant
C>0

f(u)\leq Cu^{2}\biggl(\frac{t_{1}-t_{0}}{(t-t_{0})(t_{1}-t)}\biggr)^{3/2}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(u-c_{0})^{2}}{2(t-t_{0})}-\frac{(u-c_{1})^{2}}{2(t_{1}-t)}\biggr).

Hence with the same constant
C
, we have

f(u)\leq\frac{2^{3/2}Cu^{2}}{s^{3/2}}\quad\text{for all}\quad u\leq\sqrt{s}.

(4.2)

The final conclusion follows similarly.
∎

We also note that a Bessel bridge has good oscillation properties on short time windows, provided its endpoints are not brought close to the barrier:

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose that
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}:t\in[0,1])
has the law of a
3
-dimensional Bessel bridge with
\mathfrak{X}_{0}=x_{0}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{1}=x_{1}
. Then if
x_{1},x_{0}\geq 1
, there is an absolute constant
c>0
so that

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\mathfrak{X}_{t}-(x_{1}-x_{0})t-x_{0}|\geq s+c\biggr)\leq\exp(-s^{2}/c).

Proof.

We can realize the Bessel bridge by taking a Brownian Bridge
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
with the same endpoints and conditioning it to remain positive. Let
\mathcal{H}
be the event that
this Brownian bridge is positive. It then suffices to prove that

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl\{\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\mathfrak{Z}_{t}-(x_{1}-x_{0})t-x_{0}|\geq s+c\biggr\}~|~\mathcal{H}\biggr)\leq\exp(-s^{2}/c).

This is a standard fact for
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
without the conditioning, i.e. there is a
c>0
sufficiently large that

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl\{\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\mathfrak{Z}_{t}-(x_{1}-x_{0})t-x_{0}|\geq s+c\biggr\}\biggr)\leq\exp(-s^{2}/c).

The event
\mathcal{H}
has probability bounded below by some absolute constant (indeed it is possible to compute it), but note that by monotonicity it is bounded below by the same probability where we take
x_{1}=x_{0}=1,
which in any case is some number
p>0
, and so

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl\{\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\mathfrak{Z}_{t}-(x_{1}-x_{0})t-x_{0}|\geq s+c\biggr\}~|~\mathcal{H}\biggr)\leq\exp(-s^{2}/c)/p.

Then increasing
c
, the claimed bound follows.
∎

As our first application, we show that conditionally on the process lying slightly below a concave barrier, the process in fact tends to stay in the entropic envelope. Recall the event

\mathscr{G}_{n}
from Lemma
2.4
,
and the event
\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)
from (
2.15
).

Lemma 4.3
.

On the event that

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}\in\sqrt{2}(\log k_{2}-[(\log k_{2})^{0.49},(\log k_{2})^{0.51}])

and
that

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}\in\sqrt{2}m_{n}+[-k_{6},k_{6}],

there is a constant
c>0
sufficiently small
so that for all
n\gg k_{2}\gg k_{4}\gg k_{5}
sufficiently large

\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)^{c}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})\leq\frac{(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})e^{-c(\log k_{5})^{2}}}{\log n}.

Proof.

We will work conditionally on
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}
and
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}
throughout the proof, and we will work on the
\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})
–measurable event in the statement of the lemma.

We recall that on the event
\mathscr{G}_{n}

we have (see (
2.9
))

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{2^{k}}}\leq\sqrt{\beta}k_{6}+\sqrt{2}A_{2^{k}}^{\ll}\quad\text{for all }\log_{2}k_{2}\leq k\leq\log_{2}n.

(4.3)

From
[
CMN18
, Lemma A.5]
, the probability of this event is bounded above by

\mathbb{P}(\eqref{eq:aha-1}|\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})\leq C_{\beta}\frac{(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})_{+}k_{6}}{\log(n/k_{2})}.

(4.4)

We can fill in this barrier and slightly increase its height
and define the event

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\leq\sqrt{2}(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log\log n}{\log n})t+g(t),\quad g(t)\coloneqq\biggl(\frac{t(H_{n}-t+(\log k_{5})^{50})}{H_{n}}\biggr)^{1/50}\quad\text{for all }H_{k_{2}}\leq t\leq H_{n}.

(4.5)

By a simple union bound estimate over each interval
[H_{2^{k-1}},H_{2^{k}}]

for all
k_{5}
sufficiently large

\mathbb{P}(\eqref{eq:aha0}^{c}|\eqref{eq:aha-1},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})\leq e^{-c(k_{6})(\log k_{5})^{2}}.

(4.6)

Note that we therefore have
\mathbb{P}(\eqref{eq:aha0}^{c}\cap\eqref{eq:aha-1}|\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})
is the same order as the bound claimed in the lemma, and it suffices to, going forward, bound
\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)^{c}\cap\eqref{eq:aha0}|\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})
(which will be lower order for
k_{4}\gg k_{5}
).

Recall that under
\mathbb{P},
conditional on
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}},
the process

\mathfrak{B}_{t}=\mathfrak{Z}_{t}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}-\frac{\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}}{H_{n}-H_{k_{2}}}(t-H_{k_{2}})

is a bridge starting and ending an
0.

Define a change of measure by

\displaystyle\frac{d{\mathbb{Q}}}{d\mathbb{P}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\exp\left(\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}g^{\prime}(t)d\mathfrak{Z}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}(g^{\prime}(t))^{2}dt\right)

(4.7)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(F(\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})-\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}\bigl\{g^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{B}_{t}+\tfrac{(g^{\prime}(t))^{2}}{2}\bigr\}dt\right),

for some (linear) functional
F.

Then, without conditioning on its endpoints, under
{\mathbb{Q}},
the process

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}-g(t)

is a standard Brownian motion for
t\in[H_{k_{2}},H_{n}],
and after conditioning this process is a Brownian bridge. Moreover, we see that the conditional Radon–Nikodym derivative is just given by (
4.7
) with
F=0.

Estimating the probability
{\mathbb{Q}}(\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{c}(\theta)~|~\eqref{eq:aha0})
is straightforward, given that there is an explicit probabilistic description for the conditional process. So, our goal is to show that
\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{c}(\theta)\cap\eqref{eq:aha0})
is not much larger.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{c}(\theta)\cap\eqref{eq:aha0}~|~|\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{Q}}\bigl[\tfrac{d\mathbb{P}}{d{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{c}(\theta)\cap\eqref{eq:aha0}}\right\}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}\bigr]

(4.8)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{Q}}\bigl[\tfrac{d\mathbb{P}}{d{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{c}(\theta)}\right\}~|~\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}\bigr]{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl[\eqref{eq:aha0}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}\bigr].

Conditioning on (
4.5
),
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},
and
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}},

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}-g(t)
has the law of a Bessel bridge under
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Thus under
{\mathbb{Q}}
we can use the estimates in Lemma
4.1
,
to estimate the probability of
\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)
under
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

We first establish that under the conditional measure
{\mathbb{Q}}(\cdot~|~\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})
, the process
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
is not outside the entropic barrier of half the size at
integer times
up to
H_{n}-k_{4}+1
, (c.f. (
2.13
))

\tilde{A}_{t}^{n,\pm}\coloneqq t+\begin{cases}-0.5t^{1/2\mp 2/5}&\text{ if }t\leq\frac{1}{2}\log(n),\\
-0.5(\log n-t)^{1/2\mp 2/5}-\frac{3}{4}\log\log n&\text{ if }\frac{1}{2}\log(n)<t\leq\log(n).\end{cases}

(4.9)

For bounding the event of exceeding the entropic barrier we use the summability of the powers in Lemma
4.1
, to conclude

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\notin[\tilde{A}_{t}^{n,-},\tilde{A}_{t}^{n,+}]\text{ for some integer $t\leq H_{n}-k_{4}+1$}~|~\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})

\displaystyle\leq C\bigl((\log k_{2})^{-1/5}+k_{4}^{-1/5}\bigr).

We may then fill in the gaps by again using union bound together with the oscillation estimate Lemma
4.2
over each interval to conclude for some larger constant
C>0

{\mathbb{Q}}(\widehat{\mathscr{R}}(\theta)|\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})\geq e^{-C(\log k_{2})^{-1/5}-Ck_{4}^{-1/5}}

(4.10)

for all
k_{2},k_{4}
sufficiently large.
We will show that we can reduce to this case by bounding the expression

{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl[\bigl(\tfrac{d\mathbb{P}}{d{\mathbb{Q}}}\bigr)^{2}~|~\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}\bigr].

Let
\alpha=\sqrt{2}(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log\log n}{\log n})-\frac{\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}}{H_{n}-H_{k_{2}}}=O(\tfrac{(\log k_{2})^{0.51}+k_{6}}{\log n}),
and which is positive for all
k_{2}
and
n
sufficiently large.
Under
{\mathbb{Q}}
and conditioned on
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}
, the process

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{t}

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{B}_{t}+g(t)+\alpha(t-H_{k_{2}})+\sqrt{2}(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log\log n}{\log n})H_{k_{2}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{Z}_{t}+g(t)+\sqrt{2}(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log\log n}{\log n})t

is a Brownian bridge
and the event (
4.5
) becomes

\mathfrak{X}_{t}\geq 0\quad\text{for all }\quad t\in[H_{k_{2}},H_{n}].

Thus, conditionally on (
4.5
),
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}:t\in[H_{k_{2}},H_{n}])
has the law of a Bessel bridge (c.f. Lemma
4.1
).

The conditional Radon–Nikodym derivative can be expressed in terms of
\mathfrak{X}
by

\frac{d\mathbb{P}}{d{\mathbb{Q}}}=\exp\left(\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}\bigl\{-g^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{X}_{t}+g^{\prime\prime}(t)(\alpha(t-H_{k_{2}})+\sqrt{2}(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log\log n}{\log n})H_{k_{2}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})-\tfrac{(g^{\prime}(t))^{2}}{2}\bigr\}dt\right).

(4.11)

We can estimate the function
g
for a sufficiently large absolute constant
L>0,

|g^{\prime\prime}(t)|\leq L(t^{-99/50}\wedge(H_{n}-t+\log k_{5})^{-99/50}),

(4.12)

and we also observe that
g
is concave, recall (
4.5
).
From the
concavity of
g
and the convexity of the exponential, we can therefore bound

\biggl(\frac{d\mathbb{P}}{d{\mathbb{Q}}}\biggr)^{2}\leq\exp\left(\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}-2g^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{X}_{t}dt\right).

(4.13)

From Lemma
4.1
, we have a subgaussian estimate on
\mathfrak{X}_{t}
.
Recall the subgaussian norm

\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\mathbb{E}[e^{X^{2}/t^{2}}~|~\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}]\leq 2\},

such that

\|\mathfrak{X}_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\begin{cases}(\log k_{2})^{0.51}+\sqrt{t}&t\leq\tfrac{1}{2}H_{n}\\
k_{6}+\sqrt{H_{n}-t}&t\leq H_{n}-k_{4}\\
\end{cases}

Hence using Jensen’s inequality,

\biggl\|\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}-2g^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{X}_{t}dt\biggr\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}2g^{\prime\prime}(t)\|\mathfrak{X}_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}dt.

Splitting the integral and using the bound on
g^{\prime\prime}
and
\|\mathfrak{X}_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}
,
we have

\int_{H_{k_{2}}}^{H_{n}}2g^{\prime\prime}(t)\|\mathfrak{X}_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}dt\leq C(k_{6},L)\biggl((\log k_{2})^{-47/100}+k_{4}^{-48/100}\biggr).

In summary to control of the Radon–Nikodym derivative we conclude for all
k_{4}
and
k_{2}
sufficiently large there is an absolute constant
C>0
so that

{\mathbb{Q}}\biggl(\biggl(\frac{d\mathbb{P}}{d{\mathbb{Q}}}\biggr)^{2}~|~\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}\biggr)\leq e^{Ck_{4}^{-24/25}+C(\log k_{2})^{-47/50}}.

(4.14)

Hence
using Cauchy-Schwarz, we get that for all
k_{2}
and
k_{4}
sufficiently large

{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl[\tfrac{d\mathbb{P}}{d{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{c}(\theta)}\right\}~|~\eqref{eq:aha0},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}}\bigr]\leq(\log k_{2})^{-1/5}+k_{4}^{-1/5}.

(4.15)

Finally, the
{\mathbb{Q}}
probability of (
4.5
), conditionally on
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}},
is the probability that the Brownian bridge
\mathfrak{X}_{t}
stays positive for all time.
This probability is in fact explicit
[
KS91
, (3.40)]
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}(\eqref{eq:aha0}|\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{n}})

\displaystyle=1-\exp\left(-\frac{2\mathfrak{X}_{H_{k_{2}}}\mathfrak{X}_{H_{n}}}{H_{n}-H_{k_{2}}}\right)

(4.16)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})\log k_{5}}{\log n},

for all
n,k_{2}
sufficiently large and some
C>0
.
For
n
large,
this contribution is negligible in comparison to (
4.6
).
Hence by (
4.8
) and (
4.15
), the proof is complete.
∎

By a similar argument, we can actually estimate the density of the Bessel process endpoint killed when it exits an entropic envelope.

Lemma 4.4
.

For notational convenience, let
s=H_{n_{1}^{+}}
.
Fix
p\geq 2
.
On the event that

\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}\in\sqrt{2}(\log k_{2}-[(\log k_{2})^{0.49},(\log k_{2})^{0.51}])

and

y\in\sqrt{2}(s-\frac{3}{4}\log\log n-[A_{s}^{p-1,-},A_{s}^{p-1,+}]),

\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\{\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{s}\in[y,y+dy]\}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})\\
&=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{y}{n_{1}^{+}}\bigl(\sqrt{2}H_{k_{2}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}\bigr)\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}y+\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}-\log k_{2}+o_{k_{2}}\bigr)dy\end{aligned}.

(4.17)

Furthermore for all
y\in\sqrt{2}(s-\frac{3}{4}\log\log n-[A_{s}^{p,-},A_{s}^{p,+}])
, the density is upper bounded by the right hand side.
With
y\in\sqrt{2}(s-\frac{3}{4}\log\log n-[A_{s}^{p-1,-},A_{s}^{p-1,+}])
,
if we introduce
\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}
(see (
2.71
)) as the alteration of (
2.27
), where we only put the entropic envelope restriction at
t
in the set
\{H_{2^{k}}:k\in{\mathbb{N}}\}
,
we furthermore have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bigl(\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\bigr)^{c}\cap\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\{\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{s}\in[y,y+dy]\}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})

(4.18)

\displaystyle\leq o_{k_{2}}\times\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{y}{n_{1}^{+}}\bigl(\sqrt{2}H_{k_{2}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}\bigr)\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}y+\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}-\log k_{2}\bigr)dy.

In the lemma and also below, we write
P(dy)\leq fdy
for a (sub)-probability measure
P
when we mean that
the density of
P
with respect to Lebesgue measure is bounded by
f
.

Proof.

Define the event
\mathscr{E}
that

\mathfrak{X}_{t}\coloneqq\sqrt{2}\bigl(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log s}{s}\bigr)t-\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\geq 0\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in[H_{k_{2}},s],

(4.19)

and note that
\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\subset\mathscr{E}
.

Define the change of measure

\frac{d{\mathbb{Q}}}{d\mathbb{P}}=\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}\bigl(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log s}{s}\bigr)(\mathfrak{Z}_{s}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})-\bigl(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log s}{s}\bigr)^{2}(s-H_{k_{2}})\bigr).

Under
{\mathbb{Q}}
,
\mathfrak{X}_{t}
is a standard Brownian motion. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\{\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{s}\in[y,y+dy]\}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})

(4.20)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\{\mathfrak{X}_{s}\in[y,y+dy]\}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})

\displaystyle\times\exp\bigl(-s+\tfrac{3}{2}\log s+\sqrt{2}y+\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}-H_{k_{2}}+o_{n}\bigr).

Define for convenience

t=s-H_{k_{2}}

and

x=\sqrt{2}\bigl(1-\tfrac{3}{4}\tfrac{\log s}{s}\bigr)H_{k_{2}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}.

Under
{\mathbb{Q}}((\cdot)~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})
,
\mathfrak{X}_{t}
has a gaussian density of mean
\mathfrak{X}_{H_{k_{2}}}
and variance
t
. However by the restriction on
y
, the gaussian trivializes, and so

{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\{\mathfrak{X}_{s}\in[y,y+dy]\}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})={\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y)\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi t}}(1+o_{n}).

To produce the upper bound on the density in (
4.17
),
we use the inclusion
\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\subset\mathscr{E}
and then bound, using (
4.16
),

{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y)\leq{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{E}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y)=1-\exp(-2xy/t).

(4.21)

Hence we conclude, from combining everything with (
4.20
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\cap\{\sqrt{2}m_{n_{1}^{+}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{s}\in[y,y+dy]\}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{yx}{t^{3/2}}\exp\bigl(-s+\tfrac{3}{2}\log s+\sqrt{2}y-(x^{2}+y^{2})/2t+\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}-H_{k_{2}}+o_{n}\bigr)dy

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{yx}{n_{1}^{+}}\exp\bigl(\sqrt{2}y+\sqrt{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}-\log k_{2}+o_{k_{2}}\bigr)dy.

For the lower bound in (
4.17
), we condition on
\mathscr{E}
and express

{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y)={\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{E}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y){\mathbb{Q}}(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y,\mathscr{E}).

Thus it suffices to give a vanishing upper bound on

{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\bigl(\mathscr{R}^{p}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\bigr)^{c}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y,\mathscr{E}\bigr)=o_{k_{2}}.

(4.22)

Conditionally on
\mathscr{E}
,
\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}}
and

\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y
,

\mathfrak{X}_{t}
is a Bessel-3 bridge on
[H_{k_{2}},s]
.
As this determines the Radon–Nikodym derivative, and using
the extra assumption from the statement of the lemma that
y
is far from the
edge of the
entropic window, the argument
is now similar to what is shown in Lemma
4.3
:
one starts by bounding the probability that the Bessel bridge escapes the entropic envelope at times in
H_{2^{k}}
with
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and then between two integer times use a gaussian tail bound for the oscillation from Lemma
4.2
. We omit further details.

To see (
4.18
), note that
the same oscillation lemma shows that with
p=2
,

{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathscr{E}^{c}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y,\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}\bigr)=o_{k_{2}},\;{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathscr{E}^{c}\cap\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr)=o_{k_{2}}\cdot{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathscr{E}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr)

(4.23)

which implies the claim (
4.18
)
since

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\bigl(\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\bigr)^{c}\cap\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\bigl(\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\bigr)^{c}\cap\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{E}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr)+{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\bigl(\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\bigr)^{c}\cap\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{E}^{c}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathscr{E}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr)\cdot{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\bigl(\mathscr{R}^{2}_{j}(n_{1}^{+})\bigr)^{c}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y,\mathscr{E}\bigr)+{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathscr{V}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{E}^{c}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq o_{k_{2}}{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathscr{E}~|~\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathfrak{X}_{s}=y\bigr),

where we used (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) in the last inequality.
The conclusion follow from the explicit expression for the right hand side,
see e.g. (
4.21
).
∎

4.1
The coarse oscillation bound

In this section, we consider a single
j\in\mathcal{D}_{n/k_{1}}
, and hence we
write simply the barrier event

\displaystyle{\mathscr{R}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\forall~\log k_{2}\leq t\leq\log\widehat{n}_{1}:\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{4,-}\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}(\theta_{0})\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}A_{t}^{4,+}\right\}.

(4.24)

In this section, our goal is to estimate the probability of the oscillation event

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{O}}\coloneqq\left\{\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}|\Psi_{k}(\theta^{-})-\Psi_{k}(\theta^{+})|\leq\tfrac{k_{1}(\log k_{1})^{50}}{k_{1}^{+}}\right\},

(4.25)

with
\theta^{\pm}=\theta_{0}+\frac{\pm 2\pi k_{1}}{n},
when we condition on
\mathscr{R}.

Let
(\mathscr{H}_{t}:t\geq 0)
be the join of the filtrations
(recalling (
2.2
))

\bigl((\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathbb{C}}_{s}:0\leq s\leq t):t\geq 0\bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad\bigl(({X}_{k},{Y_{k}},\Gamma_{k}^{a}:\forall~k~,H_{k}\leq t):t\geq 0\bigr),

where we further augment by
\mathfrak{Z}_{\log{\widehat{n}_{1}}}.

Note that
G_{k}
and
\Psi_{k}
are adapted to
\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Let
\tau
be any
\mathscr{H}
stopping time such that for all
\log k_{2}\leq t\leq\tau,

\mathfrak{Z}_{t}
satisfies

\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{{\beta}}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\in\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{{\beta}}}[{A}_{t}^{4,-},{A}_{t}^{4,+}].

(4.26)

Then (see (
4.24
)), if
\tau
is just the first time (
4.26
) fails, then on
\mathscr{R}
it follows
\tau>\log\widehat{n}_{1}.

We will let
\mathcal{B}
be the event that
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}\leq\sqrt{2}t
for all
t\geq H_{k_{2}}
and
\mathfrak{Z}_{\log{\widehat{n}_{1}}}\in[\mathcal{A}_{\log\widehat{n_{1}}}^{4,-},\mathcal{A}_{\log\widehat{n}_{1}}^{4,+}].

Then conditionally on
\mathcal{B},
we can use Lemma
4.1
to compute the behavior of increments of
\mathfrak{Z}.

Lemma 4.5
.

Set

\Delta_{k+1}\coloneqq\mathfrak{Z}_{\tau\wedge H_{k+1}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{\tau\wedge H_{k}}.

For
k_{2}\leq k\leq\log n_{1}^{+},
there is a constant
C>0
so that for all
k_{2}
and
k_{1}
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k+1}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]=(\sqrt{2}\pm c(k_{1},k_{2}))\mathbb{E}[(\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k})|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}],

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k+1}^{2}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]-\mathbb{E}[(\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k})|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]\right|\leq C(\mathbb{E}[(\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k})^{2}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}],

where the meaning of the first line is that the LHS is bounded above and below by the RHS, choosing signs appropriately, and where

c(k_{1},k_{2})\leq C((\log k_{2})^{-1/18}+(\log k_{1})^{-1/100}).

for some constant
C>0.

Proof.

We can express the increment
\Delta_{k+1},
using Lemma
4.1
, as

\displaystyle\Delta_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}(\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k})+B_{\tau\wedge H_{k+1}}-B_{\tau\wedge H_{k}}+\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime},

(4.27)

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm where\ }\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime}\eqqcolon\int_{\tau\wedge H_{k}}^{\tau\wedge H_{k+1}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{Z}_{s}-\sqrt{2}s}-\frac{\mathfrak{Z}_{s}-\mathfrak{Z}_{\log\widehat{n}_{1}}-\sqrt{2}(s-\log\widehat{n}_{1})}{\log\widehat{n}_{1}-s}\right)ds

and
B_{(\cdot)}
is a
\mathscr{H}
–adapted standard Brownian motion.
The increment
\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime}
we can control using the barriers. By construction, for
s\leq\tau
for all
k_{1}
and
n
sufficiently large

-\sqrt{2}(\log k_{2})^{17/18}\leq\mathfrak{Z}_{s}-\sqrt{2}s\leq-\sqrt{2}(\log k_{2})^{1/18}.

For
\tau\geq s\geq\frac{1}{2}\log n,
we can also bound

-\sqrt{2}(\log n-s)^{17/18}\leq\mathfrak{Z}_{s}-\sqrt{2}(s-\tfrac{3}{4}\log\log n)\leq-\sqrt{2}(\log n-s)^{1/18}.

In particular, for
k\leq\log n_{1}^{+}
and all
n
sufficiently large

|\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime}|\leq(\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k})\biggl((\log k_{2})^{-1/18}+2(\log k_{1})^{-1/100}\biggr).

(4.28)

As
B_{\tau\wedge(\cdot)}
is a martingale,

\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k+1}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]=\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\mathbb{E}[(\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k})|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}],

and using the bound (
4.28
), the claim concerning the first moment follows.
For the second moment, we use

\left|\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k+1}^{2}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{k+1}-\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime})^{2}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]}\right|\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime})^{2}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]}.

(4.29)

Meanwhile, we have the exact formula

\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{k+1}-\Delta_{k+1}^{\prime})^{2}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]=2\mathbb{E}[(\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k})^{2}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]+\mathbb{E}[\tau\wedge H_{k+1}-\tau\wedge H_{k}|\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}].

Hence using (
4.28
) and (
4.29
), the bound for the second moment follows.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

There is a deterministic constant

C_{\beta}
so that for
all
k_{1}
sufficiently large, depending on
k_{2},

and
all
n
sufficiently large, depending on
k_{1}
,
it holds uniformly in
\theta_{0}
that

\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\mathscr{O}}^{c}\cap\mathscr{R}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]\leq C_{\beta}(\log k_{1})^{-50}\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

As the conditional probability of
\mathcal{B}
is explicit, we conclude:

Corollary 4.7
.

There is a constant
C_{\beta}
so that for all
n,k_{1}
sufficiently large
it holds uniformly in
\theta_{0}
that

\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\mathscr{O}}^{c}\cap\mathscr{R}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}}]\leq C_{\beta}\frac{\left(\sqrt{2}\log k_{2}-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\theta_{0})+k_{6}\right)_{+}\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}m_{n}-G_{\widehat{n}_{1}}(\theta_{0})\right)_{+}}{(\log k_{1})^{50}\log(\widehat{n}_{1}/k_{2})},\quad a.s.

Proof.

We multiply the result of Proposition
4.6
by
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}}],

which is the probability that a Brownian bridge stays below a straight barrier (see (
4.16
)).
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.6
.

With some abuse of notation, let

\psi_{k}(\theta-\theta_{0})\coloneqq\Psi_{k}(\theta)-\Psi_{k}(\theta_{0})
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
for any
\theta\geq\theta_{0}.

We will show that for all
k_{1}
and
n
sufficiently large (depending on
k_{2}
) there is a constant
C_{\beta}
so that

\mathbb{P}[\{\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}\psi_{k}(\theta_{+}-\theta_{0})>\tfrac{k_{1}(\log k_{1})^{50}}{4k_{1}^{+}}\}\cap\mathscr{R}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]\leq C_{\beta}(\log k_{1})^{-50}\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

(4.30)

The analogous bound for

|\psi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\theta_{-}-\theta_{0})|=-\psi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\theta_{-}-\theta_{0})

holds by the same argument after making appropriate sign changes. We just show (
4.30
).
The proof will use tail estimates and computations
contained in Section
7
.

We will just write
\theta
for
\theta_{+}-\theta_{0}.

Further, since our estimates will not depend on
\theta_{0}
, in the rest of the proof we take
\theta_{0}=0
.
Let
\tau
be the first time
t\geq\log k_{2}
that either (
4.26
) fails, or for some
H_{k}\leq t
where
k_{2}\leq k

either
\psi_{H_{k}}(\theta)\geq\pi

or

\max_{t\in[H_{k},H_{k+1}]}|\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{\mathbb{C}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{H_{k}}^{\mathbb{C}}|^{2}>\frac{32\log k}{k+1}\quad\text{or}\quad|\sqrt{\Gamma^{a}_{k}}-\beta_{k}|>4\sqrt{\log H_{k}},

(4.31)

recall (
2.1
).
Define

Z_{k}^{\tau}\coloneqq-e^{-i\Psi_{k}(\theta)}\sqrt{\frac{k+1}{2}}\bigl(\mathfrak{Z}_{\tau\wedge H_{k+1}}^{\mathbb{C}}-\mathfrak{Z}_{\tau\wedge H_{k}}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\quad\text{and $\gamma_{k}^{\tau}=\frac{Z_{k}^{\tau}}{\sqrt{|Z_{k}^{\tau}|^{2}+\Gamma_{k}^{a}}}.$}

For any
\theta>0,
let
\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)
solve (c.f. (
1.9
))

\psi_{k+1}^{\tau}(\theta)=\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)+\begin{cases}\theta-2\Im\left(\log(1-\gamma_{k}^{\tau}e^{i\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)})-\log(1-\gamma_{k}^{\tau})\right)&\text{if }H_{k}<\tau,\\
0&\text{else},\end{cases}

and
\psi_{0}^{\tau}(\theta)=\theta.

Then on the event
\mathscr{R}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}
(compare with
(
2.8
)) if
\{\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}\psi^{\tau}_{k}(\theta)<\pi\}
we must actually have
\tau>H_{n_{1}^{+}}.
Thus for any
t>0

\mathbb{P}[\{\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}\psi_{k}(\theta)>t\}\cap\mathscr{R}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]\leq\mathbb{P}[\{\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}\psi^{\tau}_{k}(\theta)>t\}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]\quad\operatorname{a.s.}

(4.32)

On the event
\tau>H_{n_{1}^{+}},

\psi_{n_{1}^{+}}^{\tau}(\theta)=\psi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\theta).

By construction,
\psi^{\tau}_{k}(\theta)\geq 0
almost surely (see Lemma
2.1
).

From Lemma
7.2
we have that for
k_{2}
sufficiently large,
the relative Prüfer phases (recall (
1.9
)) satisfy for some absolute constant
C
and any
H_{k}<\tau

\displaystyle\psi_{k+1}^{\tau}(\theta)-\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\theta+2\Im\biggl(\frac{(e^{i\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)}-1)Z_{k}^{\tau}}{\sqrt{\Gamma_{k}^{a}}}+\frac{(e^{2i\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)}-1)(Z_{k}^{\tau})^{2}}{2{\Gamma_{k}^{a}}}\biggr)+\frac{C\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)|Z_{k}^{\tau}|^{3}}{{(\Gamma_{k}^{a})^{3/2}}}.

(4.33)

The case
\sigma=1
:

We will estimate the conditional expectation of (
4.33
) given
\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}}

using Lemma
4.5
.
We note using Lemma
4.5
, we have for
H_{k}<\tau

\mathbb{E}[Z^{\tau}_{k}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]=\frac{(-\sqrt{2}+O((\log k_{2})^{-1/18}+(\log k_{1})^{-1/100}))}{\sqrt{k+1}},

and moreover the imaginary part of the expectation is
0
. In the same way

\mathbb{E}[(Z^{\tau}_{k})^{2}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]=\mathbb{E}[\Re\left((Z^{\tau}_{k})^{2}\right)~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]\leq\frac{C}{k+1},

Hence using that
\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)\in[0,\pi)
for some
C_{\beta}>0
, which implies that the
O(1/k)
terms have a negative sign,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\psi_{k+1}^{\tau}(\theta)-\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]\leq\theta+\frac{C_{\beta}\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)(\log k)^{3}}{(k+1)^{3/2}}\quad\text{for}\quad k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}.

(4.34)

The remainder of the real case will be covered by the argument for the imaginary cases, but the argument for the real case is simpler and given below.
If we define the increasing function
k\mapsto P_{k}
by the recurrence

P_{k+1}\coloneqq P_{k}+\theta+\frac{C_{\beta}(\log k)^{3}P_{k}}{(k+1)^{3/2}},\quad\text{for all $k\geq 0$ and}\quad P_{0}\coloneqq\theta,

(4.35)

then
\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)-P_{k}
is a supermartingale started at
0.

Hence for all
t\geq 0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)>t+P_{n_{1}^{+}}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}(\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)-P_{k})>t~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]

(4.36)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)+P_{n_{1}^{+}}}{t},

with the final inequality following from the same argument
as Doob’s inequality applied to the super-martingale
\psi_{k}^{\tau}-P_{k}
.
The recurrence for
P_{k}
(
4.35
) is easily solved, and it can be checked that

P_{k}\leq C_{\beta}(k+1)\theta\qquad\text{for any}\quad k\leq n_{1}^{+}.

(4.37)

for some other sufficiently large
C_{\beta}.
Hence as
\theta\leq Cn_{1}^{-1},
we have
P_{n_{1}^{+}}\leq C_{\beta}e^{-(\log k_{1})^{(29/30)}}.
Thus (
4.36
) and (
4.32
) imply (
4.30
).

The imaginary case
\sigma=\pm i
:

In this case, to estimate (
4.33
),
we first use that for
H_{k}<\tau

We note using Lemma
4.5
, we have for
H_{k}<\tau

\mathbb{E}[Z^{\tau}_{k}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]=\pm i\frac{(\sqrt{2}+O((\log k_{2})^{-1/18}+(\log k_{1})^{-1/100}))}{\sqrt{k+1}},

and moreover the
real
part of the expectation is
0
; the
\pm
depends on the sign of
\sigma.
In the same way

\mathbb{E}[(Z^{\tau}_{k})^{2}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]=\mathbb{E}[\Re\left((Z^{\tau}_{k})^{2}\right)~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]\quad\text{and}\quad\left|\mathbb{E}[\Re\left((Z^{\tau}_{k})^{2}\right)~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]\right|\leq\frac{C}{k+1},

We note that there will also be a sign change in the
(1-\cos)
term. For either of
\sigma=\pm i
it will be necessary to consider the (less advantageous) case considered on account of needing to consider the case
\theta_{-}
(in which
\theta<0
).

Applying Lemma
4.5
and bounding the cosine,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\psi_{k+1}^{\tau}(\theta)-\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]\leq\theta+\frac{C_{\beta}(\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta))^{2}}{(k+1)}+\frac{C_{\beta}\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)(\log k)^{3}}{(k+1)^{3/2}}\quad\text{for}\quad k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}.

(4.38)

Let
\eta(x)=(\log\tfrac{1}{x})^{-100}e^{(\log\tfrac{1}{x})^{29/30}}
for
x\in(0,1)

and define a stopping time
\vartheta
as the first
k\geq k_{2}
such that

\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)>\eta(\tfrac{k+1}{n_{1}})(k+1)\theta.

Then the stopped process
\psi_{k\wedge\vartheta}^{\tau}
satisfies
for

k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}
,

\mathbb{E}[\psi_{\vartheta\wedge(k+1)}^{\tau}(\theta)-\psi_{\vartheta\wedge k}^{\tau}(\theta)~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k}},\mathcal{B}]\leq\theta+C_{\beta}\eta^{2}(\tfrac{k+1}{n_{1}})(k+1)\theta^{2}+\frac{C_{\beta}\psi_{\vartheta\wedge k}^{\tau}(\theta)(\log k)^{3}}{(k+1)^{3/2}}.

(4.39)

If we define the increasing function
k\mapsto P_{k}
by the recurrence

P_{k+1}\coloneqq P_{k}+\theta+C_{\beta}\eta^{2}(\tfrac{k+1}{n_{1}})(k+1)\theta^{2}+\frac{C_{\beta}P_{k}(\log k)^{3}}{(k+1)^{3/2}},\quad\text{for all $k\geq 0$ and}\quad P_{0}\coloneqq\theta,

(4.40)

then
\psi_{k\wedge\vartheta}^{\tau}(\theta)-P_{k\wedge\vartheta}
is a supermartingale started at
0.

The recurrence for
P_{k}
is easily solved explicitly, and in particular there is a constant
C_{\beta}
sufficiently large that for any
0\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+},

k\theta\leq P_{k}-P_{0}\leq C_{\beta}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\bigl\{\theta+\eta^{2}(\tfrac{\ell+1}{n_{1}})(\ell+1)\theta^{2}\bigr\}

(4.41)

for all
k_{1}
sufficiently large.
If we take
c
as the maximum of
x\eta^{2}(x)
on
[0,1],
then we can further bound

P_{k}-P_{0}\leq(1+2c)C_{\beta}k\theta.

(4.42)

Moreover, for all
t\geq 0
and any
m\leq n_{1}^{+},
by the same argument as in Doob’s inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq m}\psi_{k\wedge\vartheta}^{\tau}(\theta)>t+P_{m}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq m}(\psi_{k\wedge\vartheta}^{\tau}(\theta)-P_{k\wedge\vartheta})>t~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]

(4.43)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)-P_{k_{2}})+P_{m}}{t}.

We can use this bound to control the probability that
\vartheta\in[2^{\ell-1},2^{\ell}].
For this to happen for some
\ell\geq\log_{2}k_{2}
we must have for some
c_{\beta}
sufficiently small

\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq 2^{\ell}}\psi_{k\wedge\vartheta}^{\tau}(\theta)>P_{2^{\ell}}(1+c_{\beta}\eta(\tfrac{2^{\ell-1}}{n_{1}})).

Hence summing over
\log_{2}k_{2}\leq\ell\leq\log_{2}n_{1}^{+},
and using (
4.42
) and (
4.43
)
and increasing
C_{\beta}
as needed between the inequalities,

\mathbb{P}(\vartheta\leq n_{1}^{+})\leq C_{\beta}\sum_{\ell=\log_{2}k_{2}}^{\log_{2}(n_{1}^{+})}\frac{\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)+2^{\ell}\theta}{2^{\ell}\theta\eta(\tfrac{2^{\ell-1}}{n_{1}})}\leq C_{\beta}\biggl(\frac{\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)}{k_{2}\theta\eta(\tfrac{k_{2}}{n_{1}})}+\frac{(\log\tfrac{n_{1}^{+}}{n_{1}})^{\tfrac{1}{30}}}{\eta(\tfrac{n_{1}^{+}}{n_{1}})}\biggr).

On the event
\{\vartheta>n_{1}^{+}\}\cap\mathscr{G}_{n},
we thus have that
\tau>n_{1}^{+}
and

\psi_{n_{1}^{+}}(\theta)=\psi_{n_{1}^{+}}^{\tau}(\theta)\leq\eta(\tfrac{n_{1}^{+}+1}{n_{1}})(n_{1}^{+}+1)\theta.

Hence if we apply (
4.43
) again with
t=(\log k_{1})^{50}P_{n_{1}^{+}},
we
conclude that for all
k_{1}
sufficiently large

\mathbb{P}[\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)>(\log k_{1})^{50}P_{n_{1}^{+}}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]\leq C_{\beta}\biggl(\frac{\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)}{k_{2}\theta\eta(\tfrac{k_{2}}{n_{1}})}+\frac{(\log\tfrac{n_{1}^{+}}{n_{1}})^{\tfrac{1}{30}}}{\eta(\tfrac{n_{1}^{+}}{n_{1}})}\biggr)+2\frac{\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)+P_{n_{1}^{+}}}{(\log k_{1})^{50}P_{n_{1}^{+}}}.

To conclude the proof, we observe that
\theta\mapsto\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)
is a continuously differentiable function, and

|\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}(\theta)|\leq\bigl\{\sup_{\theta_{0}\in[0,2\pi]}|(\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau})^{\prime}(\theta_{0})|\bigr\}\theta.

Hence on taking
n\to\infty
the
\psi_{k_{2}}^{\tau}
terms tend to
0
almost surely, and we conclude

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}[\max_{k_{2}\leq k\leq n_{1}^{+}}\psi_{k}^{\tau}(\theta)>(\log k_{1})^{50}P_{n_{1}^{+}}~|~\mathscr{H}_{H_{k_{2}}},\mathcal{B}]\leq C_{\beta}\biggl(\frac{(\log\tfrac{n_{1}^{+}}{n_{1}})^{\tfrac{1}{30}}}{\eta(\tfrac{n_{1}^{+}}{n_{1}})}\biggr)+2(\log k_{1})^{-50},

which completes the proof by how
\eta
was chosen.
∎

4.2
The fine oscillation bound

In this section, we develop an estimate of continuity for the real part of the field, where we consider a high value of the field
\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(\theta^{\prime})
and then give a continuity estimate for
\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(\theta)
for
\theta\in[\theta^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}+\theta_{0}]
for small
\theta_{0}.
Without loss of generality we will take
\theta^{\prime}=0.

Proposition 4.8
.

(
\sigma=1
).
We suppose that for
\alpha>4,\theta_{0}>0
are given and satisfy

(\log k_{1})^{-\alpha/2}\leq\theta_{0}\leq\varepsilon

(4.44)

where the constant
\varepsilon
is a small positive constant,
to be determined, that depends on
\beta>0
.
We will condition on

(\mathfrak{L}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta)
.
We assume that the oscillation of the initial conditions are small in that

\max_{|\theta|\leq\theta_{0}}|\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta)-\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0)|\leq(\log k_{1})^{-\alpha}.

(4.45)

We consider large endpoints in the sense that

-k_{6}\leq\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0)\leq k_{6}.

(4.46)

Then there is
\delta=\delta(\beta)>0

and a constant
C_{\beta}
sufficiently large
so that for any fixed set
S
in
[0,\theta_{0}]
of cardinality at most
e^{\theta_{0}^{-\delta}}
,
the event

\mathscr{O}_{*}\coloneqq\{\sup_{\theta\in S}|\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(\theta)-\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0)|\leq\theta_{0}^{\delta}\quad\text{and}\quad\Im\bigl(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{+}}(\theta_{0})-\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0)\bigr)\leq\theta_{0}^{\delta}\}

satisfies,
on the event (
4.45
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathscr{O}_{*}^{c}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)\cap\eqref{eq7:lb}~|~(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta}(k_{6})\frac{\theta_{0}^{1+\delta}(\log k_{5})\bigl(-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}(T_{+}-T_{-})-\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)\bigr)\exp\bigl(\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)+2(T_{+}-T_{-})\bigr)}{k_{1}^{+}(\log k_{1})^{3/2}}.

Remark 4.9
.

An extension of the argument shows that in the case that
\alpha=\infty
(which is to say the initial conditions are
0
) we may in fact bound the
\delta
–Hölder exponent on the interval by a constant with the same probability (up to constants). This is by applying a chaining argument, and effectively applying this proposition repeatedly to control the process on intervals of size
\theta_{0}{2^{-k}}
for
k=0,1,2,\dots
. Step
1
would remain the same (albeit with
\mathscr{O}_{*}
being the Hölder continuity event). Steps 2-5 (control on the difference of the imaginary part) should be generalized from the difference of the imaginary part over the interval
[0,\theta_{0}]
to the interval
[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]
, but essentially no details change. Step
6
would change similarly. Finally, the chaining would be done.

Proof.

We let
d\mathfrak{X}_{t}=\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\Re(e^{i\Im\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{t}(0)}d\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{j})
and let
d\mathfrak{B}_{t}=\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\Im(e^{i\Im\mathfrak{L}_{t}^{j}(0)}d\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{j}),
for
t\in[T_{-},T_{+}]
. We also set the initial conditions
\mathfrak{B}_{T_{-}}=0
and
\mathfrak{X}_{T_{-}}=\mathfrak{-U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)
. Hence the process

\mathfrak{X}
equals
-\mathfrak{U}^{j}
.

We let
\mathscr{H}
be the filtration generated by
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{B}
with
\mathfrak{X}_{T_{+}}
and
(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}{(\theta)}:\theta)
adjoined.
For a large integer absolute constant
k_{*}
we set
T_{*}=T_{+}-k_{*}
and we condition on the event that we remain below the concave barrier

\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{t}(0)\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\bigl(t-T_{+}+2\bigl({T_{+}-t+(\log k_{5})^{50}}\bigr)^{1/50}\bigr)\eqqcolon\ell(t)\quad\text{for all }t\in[T_{-},T_{*}].

(4.47)

This barrier is above the concave barrier in
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0).

As in the definitions and manipulations between (
4.7
)–(
4.13
) from Lemma
4.3
, we define a measure
\mathbb{Q}
which is mutually absolutely continuous with respect to
\mathbb{P}
and which flattens the curvature in (
4.47
). For this measure, there is a
(\mathbb{Q},\mathscr{H})
–Brownian motion
(X_{t}:t\in[T_{-},T_{+}])

such that

d\mathfrak{X}_{t}=dX_{t}+\biggl(-\ell^{\prime}(t)+\frac{\mathbf{1}\left\{{t\leq T_{*}}\right\}}{\ell(t)+\mathfrak{X}_{t}}+\frac{\mathfrak{X}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{X}_{t}+\ell(T_{*})-\ell(t)}{T_{+}-t}\biggr)dt,\quad\mathfrak{X}_{T_{-}}=-\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0).

(4.48)

In the above, we have extended the definition of
\ell
for
t\geq T_{*}
by taking it constant and equal to
\ell(T_{*})
.
Under
\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathfrak{B}
remains a
(\mathbb{Q},\mathscr{H})
–Brownian motion.
Moreover from (
4.13
), we have a bound on the Radon–Nikodym derivative

\frac{d\mathbb{P}}{d\mathbb{Q}}\leq\exp\biggl(\int_{T_{-}}^{T_{*}}\!\!\!\!\!\!-\ell^{\prime\prime}(t)(\ell(t)+\mathfrak{X}_{t})dt\biggr).

(4.49)

We also introduce
\mathfrak{D}_{t}=\ell(t\wedge T_{*})+\mathfrak{X}_{t}
, which under
\mathbb{Q}
is an
\mathscr{H}
–adapted Bessel bridge with endpoints

\mathfrak{D}_{T_{-}}\leq 2\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\exp\bigl(\tfrac{9}{10}(\log k_{1})^{29/30}\bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\bigl(((\log k_{5})^{50}+k_{*})^{1/50}+k_{6}\bigr).

The process
\mathfrak{D}
describes the distance of
\mathfrak{X}
from
the barrier.

Step 1: reduction to a conditional probability.

We will show in this section that the problem can be reduced to
showing

{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathscr{O}_{*}^{c}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta),\eqref{eq7:ray}\bigr)\leq\theta_{0}^{1+\delta}

(4.50)

for some

\delta>0
(note that
\delta
also hides in the definition of
\mathscr{O}_{*}
) depending on
\beta
and all
k_{4},k_{5}
bigger than some constant also
depending only
\beta
.
Suppose we have established (
4.50
).
Using the change of measure, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathscr{O}_{*}^{c}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta),\eqref{eq7:ray}\bigr)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{Q}}\left[\frac{d\mathbb{P}}{d\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{1}\left\{{\mathscr{O}_{*}^{c}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)}\right\}~\middle|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta),\eqref{eq7:ray}\right].

Applying Hölder’s inequality, there is
\lambda>1
sufficiently large that

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathscr{O}_{*}^{c}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta),\eqref{eq7:ray}\bigr)\leq{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\exp\biggl(\int_{T_{-}}^{T_{*}}\!\!\!\!\!\!-\lambda\ell^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{D}_{t}dt\biggr)\right)^{1/\lambda}\theta_{0}^{1+\delta/2}.

Controlling the Radon–Nikodym derivative is the same argument as the argument between (
4.13
) and (
4.14
).
We have for some absolute constant
L>0

-\ell^{\prime\prime}(t)\leq L(T_{+}-t+(\log k_{5})^{50})^{-99/50}

and from Lemma
4.1
for some constant
C_{\beta}

\|\mathfrak{D}_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C_{\beta}((\log k_{5})+\sqrt{T_{+}-t}),

where
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{2}}
is the subgaussian norm with respect to the conditional probability measure given as
\mathbb{Q}(\cdot~|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta),\eqref{eq7:ray})
.
Hence by convexity of the norm

\biggl\|\int_{T_{-}}^{T_{*}}\!\!\!\!\!\!-\ell^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{D}_{t}dt\biggr\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\int_{T_{-}}^{T_{*}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\bigl\|-\ell^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{D}_{t}dt\bigr\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C_{\beta}(\log k_{5})^{-24}.

Thus we have after increasing
C_{\beta}
for all
\lambda\geq 1

{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\exp\biggl(\int_{T_{-}}^{T_{*}}\!\!\!\!\!\!-\lambda\ell^{\prime\prime}(t)\mathfrak{D}_{t}dt\biggr)\right)^{1/\lambda}\leq\exp\bigl(\lambda C_{\beta}(\log k_{5})^{-24}\bigr),

which is bounded by
2
for all
k_{5}
small (depending on
\lambda
, which in turn depends only on
\beta
).

Thus we have shown

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathscr{O}_{*}^{c}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta),\eqref{eq7:ray}\bigr)\leq\theta_{0}^{1+\delta}

(4.51)

for some constant
\delta>0
depending only on
\beta
and all
k_{4},k_{5}
bigger than some constant depending only on
\beta
.
The event (
4.47
) contains
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)
, and so

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathscr{O}_{*}^{c}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta)\bigr)\leq\theta_{0}^{1+\delta}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\eqref{eq7:ray}~|~\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0),(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta)\bigr).

We then take expectation on both sides of the equation over
\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{+}}(0)
satisfying (
4.46
).
Let
\mathbb{M}
be the change of measure that flattens the linear part of
\ell
(with respect to the conditional measure
\mathbb{P}(\cdot~|~(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta))
)

\frac{d\mathbb{M}}{d\mathbb{P}}=\exp\biggl(\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}(\mathfrak{U}_{T_{+}}^{j}(0)-\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0))-(T_{+}-T_{-})\biggr),

under which

t\mapsto\mathfrak{U}_{t}^{j}(0)-\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}(t-T_{-})

is a speed–
(\tfrac{4}{\beta})
Brownian motion.
Note that on (
4.46
) the change of measure
\frac{d\mathbb{M}}{d\mathbb{P}}
is controlled up to constants by

\frac{d\mathbb{M}}{d\mathbb{P}}\geq C(k_{6})\exp\biggl(-\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)-(T_{+}-T_{-})\biggr).

In particular, we have (using
T_{+}-T_{-}=\log k_{1}^{+}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\eqref{eq7:ray}\cap\eqref{eq7:lb}~|~(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(k_{6})}{k_{1}^{+}}\exp\biggl(\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{2}}\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)+2(T_{+}-T_{-})\biggr)\mathbb{M}\bigl(\eqref{eq7:ray}\cap\eqref{eq7:lb}~|~(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta)\bigr).

Using barrier estimates as in
[
CMN18
, Corollary A.6]

we can bound the
\mathbb{M}
–probability
for values of
\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0)
given by
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)

\mathbb{M}\bigl(\eqref{eq7:ray}\cap\eqref{eq7:lb}~|~(\mathfrak{L}^{j}_{T_{-}}(\theta):\theta)\bigr)\leq\frac{C(k_{6})(\log k_{5})\bigl(-\mathfrak{U}_{T_{-}}^{j}(0)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}(T_{+}-T_{-})\bigr)}{(\log k_{1})^{3/2}}.

This concludes the reduction of the lemma to (
4.50
).

Step 2: Finding a good event on which the slope of the Bessel bridge is tame.

We introduce a random variable to control the slopes of the Bessel bridge:

\mathfrak{u}\coloneqq\max_{k\geq k_{*}:T_{+}-k\geq T_{-}}k^{0.49}\times\biggl|\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}-k}}{k}\biggr|.

Conditioning on
\mathfrak{D}_{T_{*}}
, by the Gaussian tail bound for the Brownian bridge increment
\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{T_{*}}
and the Gaussian tail bound for the Bessel bridge increment (c.f. Lemma
4.1
)
\mathfrak{D}_{T_{*}}-\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}-k}
, there is a Gaussian tail bound for
\mathfrak{u}
of the form

\mathbb{Q}\left(\biggl|\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}-k}}{k}\biggr|>C\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}(1+x)~\middle|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}}\right)\leq Ce^{-kx^{2}},

for some
C
sufficiently large and all
k\geq k_{*}.

Thus, summing in
k
we may assume that the event

E_{0}\coloneqq\{\mathfrak{u}\leq\tfrac{\widehat{\delta}}{3}\bigl(\log(1/\theta_{0})\bigr)^{0.51}\}

(4.52)

occurs with
(\mathbb{Q}~|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}})
–conditional probability at least
1-C_{\beta}e^{-c_{\beta}\widehat{\delta}^{2}(\log(1/\theta_{0}))^{1.01}}
for all
\widehat{\delta},\theta_{0}>0
sufficiently small.

Step 3: Finding a good event with small oscillations.

The oscillations of
\mathfrak{X}_{t}
must be controlled, especially near the endpoint
t=T_{+},

to control its contribution to the diffusions
d\mathfrak{X}_{t}+id\mathfrak{B}_{t}.

These oscillations are ultimately controlled by those of the underlying Brownian motion
{X}_{t}
. Define the event

E_{1}
, for
\widehat{\delta}
to be determined as a function of
\beta
,

\max\biggl\{\frac{|X_{u}-X_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}},\frac{|\mathfrak{X}_{u}-\mathfrak{X}_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}\biggr\}\leq\tfrac{\widehat{\delta}}{3}\bigl(\log(1/\theta_{0})^{0.51}(T_{+}-u+1)^{0.49}\bigr),\forall~T_{+}\geq u\geq s\geq T_{-},|u-s|\leq 1.

We may bound this probability by reducing the statement to a union bound over sets

\operatorname{CP}_{k}\coloneqq\left\{(u,s):|T_{+}-u-k|\leq 2,|T_{+}-s-k|\leq 2\right\}

for integer
k.

First we will need a basic input: from oscillation theory of Brownian motion, there is an absolute constant
C>0
so that for all
x>0,

\mathbb{Q}\biggl(\sup_{(u,s)\in\operatorname{CP}_{k}}\frac{|X_{u}-X_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}>\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}C(1+x)~\bigg|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}}\biggr)\leq e^{-x^{2}}.

(4.53)

This follows e.g. from the Borel–Tsirelson-Ibragimov-Sudakov inequality, together with
[
RY99
, Theorem I.2.1]
.
Taking a union bound over
k
,
we conclude that with
(\mathbb{Q}~|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}})
–conditional probability
1-C_{\beta}e^{-c_{\beta}\widehat{\delta}^{2}(\log(1/\theta_{0}))^{2}}
for all
\widehat{\delta},\theta_{0}>0
sufficiently small,

\frac{|X_{u}-X_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}\leq\tfrac{\widehat{\delta}}{3}\bigl(\log(1/\theta_{0})^{0.51}(T_{+}-u+1)^{0.49}\bigr),\quad\forall~T_{+}\geq u\geq s\geq T_{-},|u-s|\leq 1.

(4.54)

To control the oscillations of
\mathfrak{X},
it suffices to control the oscillations of
\mathfrak{D}
in its place, as for any
T_{+}\geq u\geq s\geq T_{-}
with
|u-s|\leq 1

|\mathfrak{X}_{u}-\mathfrak{X}_{s}|\leq|\mathfrak{D}_{u}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}|+\bigl(\max_{T_{-}\leq t\leq T_{+}}|\ell^{\prime}(t)|\bigr)|u-s|,

and
\ell^{\prime}
remains bounded.

For the control on
\mathfrak{D}
we consider separately the cases of
k\leq k_{*}-2
and
k\geq k_{*}-2
. In the former case, if we condition on the value of
\mathfrak{X}_{T_{*}}
then the process
\bigl(\mathfrak{X}_{t}:t\in[T_{*},T_{+}]\bigr)
is a speed–
({\tfrac{4}{\beta}})
Brownian bridge. Its slope can be controlled by
\mathfrak{u}
, and after removing its slope, the same bound (
4.53
) holds, with possibly different constants, and so
taking a union bound over
k
, we control the probability of
E_{1}
failing for these
k
.

For
k\geq k_{*}-2
, integrating the differential representation for
\mathfrak{D}_{t}
, we have that for
(u,s)\in\operatorname{CP}_{k}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{u}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}=X_{u}-X_{s}+\int_{s}^{u}\biggl(\frac{\mathbf{1}\left\{{t\leq T_{*}}\right\}}{\mathfrak{D}_{t}}\biggr)dt+\int_{s}^{u}\biggl(\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{t}}{T_{+}-t}\biggr)dt.

On the event
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)
, we may bound
\mathfrak{D}_{t}\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}(\log k_{5})
(which is due to the concave barrier in
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(\theta)
being shifted by a constant factor from the conditioning (
4.47
)), for all
t\in(s,u)
for which
t\leq T_{*}
. To the second
integral, we add and subtract
\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}-k+2}
in the following way

\int_{s}^{u}\biggl(\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{t}}{T_{+}-t}\biggr)dt=\int_{s}^{u}\biggl(\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}-k+2}}{T_{+}-t}\biggr)dt+\int_{s}^{u}\biggl(\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}-k+2}-\mathfrak{D}_{t}}{T_{+}-t}\biggr)dt.

The first part we control in the same way as above. As for the second, we may bound it above to create the following implicit bound

\displaystyle\sup_{(u,s)\in\operatorname{CP}_{k}}\frac{|\mathfrak{D}_{u}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{(u,s)\in\operatorname{CP}_{k}}\frac{|X_{u}-X_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}+(\mathfrak{u}+C_{\beta})+\frac{C}{k_{*}-4}\biggl(\max_{\kappa=k_{*}-2,\dots,k_{*}+2}\sup_{(u,s)\in\operatorname{CP}_{\kappa}}\frac{|\mathfrak{D}_{u}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}\biggr).

Here
C_{\beta}
controls the Bessel generator term and
C
is an absolute constant. Hence, on taking maxima over both sides, we conclude for all
k_{*}
larger than an absolute constant

\sup_{(u,s)\in\operatorname{CP}_{k}}\frac{|\mathfrak{D}_{u}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}\leq 2\biggl(\max_{\kappa=k_{*}-2,\dots,k_{*}+2}\sup_{(u,s)\in\operatorname{CP}_{\kappa}}\frac{|{X}_{u}-{X}_{s}|}{|u-s|^{1/4}}+\mathfrak{u}+C_{\beta}\biggr).

Using (
4.54
) and (
4.52
), we conclude that there is some
C_{\beta},c_{\beta}>0
such that for all sufficiently small
\theta_{0}
and
\widehat{\delta}
,

{\mathbb{Q}}(E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{0}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}})\leq C_{\beta}e^{-c_{\beta}\widehat{\delta}^{2}(\log(1/\theta_{0}))^{1.01}}.

(4.55)

Step 4: Finding a good event on which the imaginary part can not explode.

This will turn out to be the most probabilistically expensive part.
Let
E_{2}
be the event, for
C_{\beta},\delta_{\beta}
to be determined,

\mathfrak{X}_{t}-\mathfrak{X}_{s}\leq\left(1+\frac{2}{\beta}-3\widehat{\delta}\right)(t-s)-\delta_{\beta}\bigl(\log(\theta_{0})\bigr)+\log_{+}(T_{+}-t),\quad\forall\,T_{+}\geq t\geq s\geq T_{-}.

(4.56)

As we will work on the event
E_{1}
it suffices to control the above on integer–valued points, hence it instead suffices to bound the probability

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{t}-\mathfrak{X}_{s}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{2}{\beta}-3\widehat{\delta}\right)(t-s)-(\delta_{\beta}-2\widehat{\delta})\bigl(\log(\theta_{0})\bigr)+(1-2\widehat{\delta})\log_{+}(T_{+}-t),

\displaystyle\forall\ T_{+}\geq t\geq s\geq T_{-}\quad\text{for which}\quad t,s\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

We can furthermore formulate a sufficient bound in terms of
\mathfrak{D}
using that

|\ell^{\prime}(t)-\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}|\leq\widehat{\delta}
(which holds provided
k_{5}
is chosen sufficiently large with respect to
\widehat{\delta}
), so that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{t}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{2}{\beta}+\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta}}-4\widehat{\delta}\right)(t-s)-(\delta_{\beta}-2\widehat{\delta})\bigl(\log(\theta_{0})\bigr)+(1-2\widehat{\delta})\log_{+}(T_{+}-t),

(4.57)

\displaystyle\forall\ T_{+}\geq t\geq s\geq T_{-}\quad\text{for which}\quad t,s\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

If
s\geq T_{*}
, then as in the lead-up to (
4.52
), conditioning on the value of
\mathfrak{D}_{T_{*}}
the process

(\mathfrak{D}_{t}-\mathfrak{D}_{T_{*}}:t\in[T_{*},T_{+}])

is a speed–
({\tfrac{4}{\beta}})
Brownian bridge under
{\mathbb{Q}}
with slope at most
\mathfrak{u}
.
Hence we have a tail bound that for any
t\geq s
with
s\geq T_{*}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}}\left(\bigl\{\mathfrak{D}_{t}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}>\delta+\tfrac{\widehat{\delta}}{3}(t-s)\bigr\}\cap E_{0}~\middle|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\beta\widehat{\delta}^{2}}{8(t-s)}\right).

(4.58)

Using this, we have that the portion of (
4.57
)
for which
t,s
with
T_{*}\leq s\leq t\leq T_{+}
can be controlled by
\widehat{\delta}\log(1/\theta_{0})
, which is less than the bound stated in (
4.57
), with
probability

1-C_{\beta}e^{-c_{\beta}\widehat{\delta}^{2}(\log(1/\theta_{0}))^{2}}
for all
\widehat{\delta},\theta_{0}>0
sufficiently small.
Hence, we may reduce the problem to showing

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{t}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{2}{\beta}+\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta}}-4\widehat{\delta}\right)(t-s)-(\delta_{\beta}-3\widehat{\delta})\bigl(\log(\theta_{0})\bigr)+(1-2\widehat{\delta})\log_{+}(T_{+}-t),

(4.59)

\displaystyle\forall\ T_{*}\geq t\geq s\geq T_{-}\quad\text{for which}\quad t,s\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

For all
s\leq t\leq T_{*}
, we begin by recalling that the Bessel bridge SDE has strong solutions:

d\mathfrak{D}_{t}=dX_{t}+\biggl(\frac{\mathbf{1}\left\{{t\leq T_{*}}\right\}}{\mathfrak{D}_{t}}+\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{t}}{T_{+}-t}\biggr)dt,\quad\mathfrak{D}_{T_{-}}+\ell(T_{-})=-\mathfrak{U}^{j}_{T_{-}}(0).

For all
k_{5}
sufficiently large (with respect to
\widehat{\delta}
) on
\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)
, we may bound above the Bessel generator term by
\widehat{\delta}
.
The slope we bound on
E_{0}\cap E_{1}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)
by comparing
t
to its (integer) ceiling

\biggl|\frac{\mathfrak{D}_{T_{+}}-\mathfrak{D}_{t}}{T_{+}-t}\biggr|\leq\frac{2\widehat{\delta}}{3}\frac{\log(1/\theta_{0})^{0.51}}{(T_{+}-t)^{0.51}}.

(4.60)

When
T_{+}-t\geq\log(1/\theta_{0})
we can just as well bound the above by
\frac{2\widehat{\delta}}{3}
. We conclude that we have the bound
on

E_{0}\cap E_{1}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)

\mathfrak{D}_{t}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}\leq X_{t}-X_{s}+\frac{5\widehat{\delta}}{3}(t-s)+\frac{5\widehat{\delta}}{3}\log(1/\theta_{0})\quad\text{for all}\quad T_{-}\leq s\leq t\leq T_{*}.

(4.61)

Thus for any
t\leq T_{*}
and
w>0
we have the bound, using the probability that Brownian motion hits a line,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}}\left(\exists~s\in[T_{-},t]:\mathfrak{D}_{t}-\mathfrak{D}_{s}>w+\biggl(1+\frac{2}{\beta}+\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta}}-4\widehat{\delta}\biggr)(t-s)~\bigg|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\beta}{2}\biggl(1+\frac{2}{\beta}+\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta}}-\frac{17\widehat{\delta}}{3}\biggr)\biggl(w-\frac{5\widehat{\delta}}{3}\log(1/\theta_{0})\biggr)\right).

We pick a
\delta_{\beta}\in(0,1)
and an
\eta_{\beta}
so that

\frac{\beta}{2}\biggl(1+\frac{2}{\beta}+\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta}}\biggr)\delta_{\beta}>\eta_{\beta}>1.

(4.62)

We apply the tail bound just above with
w=(\delta_{\beta}-3\widehat{\delta})\bigl(\log(1/(\theta_{0}))\bigr)+\bigl(1-2\widehat{\delta}\bigr)\log_{+}(T_{+}-t),
from which it follows that for all
\widehat{\delta}
sufficiently small (as a function of
\beta
),

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}}\left(\biggl\{\exists~s\in[T_{-},t]:\text{ \eqref{eq:tlv5} fails }\biggr\}\cap E_{0}\cap E_{1}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~\bigg|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}}\right)\leq(T_{+}-t)^{-(1/2)\log(1/\theta_{0})}\theta_{0}^{\eta_{\beta}+\widehat{\delta}}

Summing in
t
, it is seen that for
\widehat{\delta}
sufficiently small and
\theta_{0}
sufficiently small to absorb the constants,

{\mathbb{Q}}((E_{2})^{c}\cap E_{0}\cap E_{1}\cap\mathscr{P}_{j}^{\prime}(0)~|~\mathscr{H}_{T_{-}})\leq\theta_{0}^{\eta_{\beta}+\widehat{\delta}/2}.

(4.63)

Step 5: A tail bound for the change in the imaginary part.

We estimate the imaginary part of

\Delta_{t}=\Delta_{t}(\theta_{0})=\Im(\mathfrak{L}_{t}(\theta_{0})-\mathfrak{L}_{t}(0)).

This satisfies the SDE

d\Delta_{t}=\theta_{0}e^{t}k_{1}^{-1}dt+\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}\Im\biggl((e^{i\Delta_{t}}-1)e^{i\Im\mathfrak{L}_{t}(0)}d\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{j}\biggr),

which has almost surely non-negative solutions.
Then we express

d\Delta_{t}=\theta_{0}e^{t}k_{1}^{-1}dt+\Delta_{t}d\mathfrak{X}_{t}+\xi_{1}(\Delta_{t})d\mathfrak{X}_{t}+\xi_{2}(\Delta_{t})d\mathfrak{B}_{t},

where
\xi_{1}(x)
and
\xi_{2}(x)
are bounded by
C_{\beta}x^{2}.

Let
M_{t}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{X}_{t}-\tfrac{2}{\beta}t\right).

Then we have from Itô’s Lemma

d\biggl(\frac{\Delta_{t}}{M_{t}}\biggr)=\frac{1}{M_{t}}\biggl(\theta_{0}e^{t}k_{1}^{-1}dt+\xi_{1}(\Delta_{t})(d\mathfrak{X}_{t}-\tfrac{4}{\beta}dt)+\xi_{2}(\Delta_{t})d\mathfrak{B}_{t}\biggr).

(4.64)

This we can integrate to conclude for
