Therefore, there exists a constant
\alpha=\alpha(\lambda,\beta)

so that

\left|\tfrac{d\ell_{t}^{c}}{dt}\right|\leq\alpha\ell_{t},\quad\left|\tfrac{d\ell_{t}^{s}}{dt}\right|\leq\alpha\ell_{t}.

(10.10)

From now on, we take
4\lambda^{2}/\beta=a
(
a\sim 2
is the exponent we will need for Chebychev’s inequality).
Define
\hat{\ell}_{t}=\ell_{t}e^{-a(t-T_{-})}
, and similarly

\hat{\ell}_{t}^{c},\hat{\ell}_{t}^{s}
.
Then,

\frac{d\hat{\ell}_{t}}{dt}=ae^{-a(t-T_{-})}\big(\cos(\theta_{t})\ell_{t}^{c}-\sin(\theta_{t})\ell_{t}^{s}\big)=a\big(\cos(\theta_{t})\hat{\ell}_{t}^{c}-\sin(\theta_{t})\hat{\ell}_{t}^{s}\big),

and therefore, with
t\geq T_{-}
,

\hat{\ell}_{t}-\hat{\ell}_{T_{-}}=a\int_{T_{-}}^{t}\big(\cos(\theta_{u})\hat{\ell}_{u}^{c}-\sin(\theta_{u})\hat{\ell}_{u}^{s}\big)du.

Using the change of variables

e^{u}=s
, we have that
\theta_{u}=\theta s/k_{1}
and
thus we obtain that

\hat{\ell}_{t}-\hat{\ell}_{T_{-}}=a\int_{e^{T_{-}}}^{e^{t}}s^{-1}\big(\cos(\theta s/k_{1})\hat{\ell}^{c}_{\log s}-\sin(\theta s/k_{1})\hat{\ell}^{s}_{\log s}\big)ds.

(10.11)

Consider first the case
\theta e^{T_{-}}/k_{1}>c
for some fixed constant

c>1
, that is
T_{*}\leq T_{-}-\log c
.
Set

T_{\ell}=T_{-}+\ell
,
\ell\geq 0
. We consider first
s\in[e^{T_{\ell}},e^{T_{\ell+1}}]\eqqcolon I_{\ell}
.
Let

F_{c}(s)=\int_{e^{T_{\ell}}}^{s}u^{-1}\cos(\theta u/k_{1})du,\quad F_{s}(s)=\int_{e^{T_{\ell}}}^{s}u^{-1}\sin(\theta u/k_{1})du.

Then,
for
s\in I_{\ell}
, we have (using integration by parts) that

|F_{c}(s)|,|F_{s}(s)|\leq 3\tfrac{k_{1}e^{-T_{\ell}}}{\theta}=:b_{\ell}.

Performing in (
10.11
) integration by parts and using (
10.10
) and that
|\hat{\ell}_{t}^{c}|,|\hat{\ell}_{t}^{s}|\leq\hat{\ell}_{t}
, we obtain
(using that
4ab_{\ell}\ll 1
for
\lambda
bounded by say
4
if
c
is chosen large enough)
that
for
e^{t}\in I_{\ell}
,

\hat{\ell}_{t}\leq\hat{\ell}_{T_{\ell}}+4ab_{\ell}\hat{\ell}_{t}+4\alpha ab_{\ell}\int_{e^{T_{\ell}}}^{e^{t}}s^{-1}\hat{\ell}_{\log s}ds=\hat{\ell}_{T_{\ell}}+4ab_{\ell}\hat{\ell}_{t}+4\alpha ab_{\ell}\int_{T_{\ell}}^{t}\hat{\ell}_{u}du.

An application of Gronwall’s inequality then gives that
for such
t
,

\hat{\ell}_{t}\leq\tfrac{1}{1-4ab_{\ell}}\hat{\ell}_{T_{\ell}}e^{4ab_{\ell}\alpha(t-T_{\ell})/(1-4ab_{\ell})}.

Unravelling the definitions, we have obtained that for
e^{t}\in I_{\ell}
,

\mathbb{E}\big(e^{\lambda(R_{t}+R_{t}(\theta))}\big)=\ell_{t}\leq\left(\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\tfrac{1}{1-4ab_{j}}\right)e^{a(t-T_{-})+4a\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}ab_{j}\alpha(T_{j+1}-T_{j})/(1-4ab_{j})}\leq Ce^{a(t-T_{-})},

(10.12)

uniformly in the initial conditions of
\hat{I}_{T_{-}}(\theta)
,
where
C
is a universal constant independent of
\ell
and we used that

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}ab_{j}\alpha(T_{j+1}-T_{j})/(1-4ab_{j})
is uniformly bounded.
In particular, for
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
with
|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}|\geq ck_{1}e^{-T_{-}}
,
using Chebycheff’s inequality with
\lambda\sim 2

we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R_{{T_{+}}}(\theta_{i})\geq\sqrt{8/\beta}((h_{i}-k_{7})/\sqrt{2}+(T_{+}-T_{-})),i=1,2)

(10.13)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\big(\sqrt{2}(T_{+}-T_{-})+(h_{1}-k_{7})\big)^{2}/2(T_{+}-T_{-})},

which yields the bottom inequality in (
10.3
).

We next turn to the case
T_{+}-k_{4}\geq T_{*}>T_{-}-\log c
. In that case, we apply the barrier estimate for time
(T_{*}-T_{-}-\log c)_{+}
and the same computation
as above for larger times. That is, assuming without loss of generality that
h=h_{1}
, we write

T_{*}^{\prime}=(T_{*}\vee T_{-})
and, with
\mathscr{P}(\theta_{i},h_{i})^{T_{*}^{\prime}}
the part of the barrier event
\mathscr{P}(\theta_{i},h_{i})
up to time
T_{*}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R_{{T_{+}}}(\theta_{i})\geq\sqrt{8/\beta}((h_{i}-k_{7})/\sqrt{2}+(T_{+}-T_{-}))\cap\mathscr{P}(\theta_{i},h_{i}),i=1,2)

(10.14)

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{\beta/8}R_{{T_{*}^{\prime}}}(\theta_{1})\in dz\cap\mathscr{P}(\theta_{1},h_{1})^{T_{*}^{\prime}})\times

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{P}\Big(R_{T_{+}}(\theta_{1})-R_{T_{*}^{\prime}}(\theta_{1})\geq\sqrt{8/\beta}((h_{1}-k_{7})/\sqrt{2}+(T_{+}-T_{-})-z),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad R_{T_{+}}(\theta_{2})-R_{T_{*}^{\prime}}(\theta_{2})\geq\sqrt{8/\beta}(-k_{7}/\sqrt{2}+(T_{+}-T_{*})+T_{*}^{1/14}\Big).

Note that the integration over the variable
z
in the right side of (
10.14
) is restricted in particular to the range
z\in[(T_{+}-T_{*}^{\prime})^{1/14},(T_{+}-T_{*}^{\prime})^{13/14}]
.
We control the second probability in the right hand side of (
10.14
) as in the case
T_{*}\leq T_{-}-\log c
, while the first probability is controlled by a standard
Gaussian bound. This yields the top inequality in (
10.3
). Further details are omitted.
∎

10.2
Regularity of the kernel

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
F
be the subset of
\mathcal{C}([-2\pi k_{1},0],\mathbb{C})
for which
\max_{\theta}|f(\theta)|>e^{-k_{7}}
(which corresponds to those decorations in
\Gamma_{k_{7}}^{+}
).
Let
\mathfrak{r}(x,\alpha,f)
be the Radon–Nikodym derivative of the measure
\mathfrak{s}(\alpha+x,e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}(\alpha+x)}df)
with respect to
\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}x}df)
on
F
. Then, uniformly in
|x|\leq(\log k_{1})^{17/18}
and
|\alpha|\leq 1
,

\mathfrak{r}(x,\alpha,f)=e^{\sqrt{2}\alpha}(1+o_{k_{1}}).

The same holds for
\mathfrak{p}
trivially by integrating over the random phase:

Corollary 10.3
.

Using the same
F
, and for the same set of
|x|\leq(\log k_{1})^{17/18}
,
the Radon-Nikodym derivative
\mathfrak{t}(x,\alpha,f)
of
\mathfrak{p}(\alpha+x,e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}(\alpha+x)}df)
with respect to
\mathfrak{p}(x,e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}x}df)
satisfies

\mathfrak{t}(x,\alpha,f)=e^{\sqrt{2}\alpha}(1+o_{k_{1}}).

Proof of Lemma
10.2
.

We recall the definition of
\mathfrak{U}_{t}^{o,j}(h)

from below (
2.54
).
In particular, we have a decomposition

\displaystyle\mathfrak{U}_{T_{+}}^{o}(\theta)=\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)+(\mathfrak{U}_{T_{+}}^{o}(\theta)-\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)),

(10.15)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{T_{+}}^{o}(\theta)=\mathfrak{L}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)+(\mathfrak{L}_{T_{+}}^{o}(\theta)-\mathfrak{L}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)),

where the entire process
\{(\mathfrak{L}_{T_{+}}^{o}(\theta)-\mathfrak{L}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)):\theta\}
is independent of
\mathfrak{L}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)
(and hence also
\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)
).
Then
D_{j}^{o}
can be decomposed as

D_{j}^{o}(x)\eqqcolon e^{\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)}\times\widehat{D}(x+\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)),

(10.16)

which makes
\widehat{D}(x)
independent of
\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)
. Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}}(x,\cdot)
be the law of
\widehat{D}(x)
.
Note to have
D_{j}^{o}(x)\in e^{\sqrt{4/\beta}x}F
, we must have

\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\mathcal{A}_{T_{\dagger}}^{-}\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{4}{\beta}}x+\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)\leq\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{\beta}}\mathcal{A}_{T_{\dagger}}^{+},

as if not, then by definition
D_{j}^{o}\equiv 0
. Define the interval

J=\sqrt{2}(T_{\dagger}-T_{-})+[\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}_{T_{\dagger}}^{-},\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}_{T_{\dagger}}^{+}],

which has width which is at most
O((\log k_{1}^{+})^{13/14})
and allows us to write the previous display as
x+\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)+\sqrt{2}(T_{\dagger}-T_{-})\in J
.
Thus we have a representation for
f\in F
, with
T=T_{\dagger}-T_{-}
, and where integrate over the law of
y=x+\sqrt{\tfrac{\beta}{4}}\mathfrak{U}_{T_{\dagger}}^{o}(0)+\sqrt{2}T
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{\sqrt{{4}/{\beta}}x}df)

\displaystyle=\int_{J}e^{-(y-x-\sqrt{2}T)^{2}/(2T)}\widehat{\mathfrak{s}}(y-\sqrt{2}T,e^{\sqrt{{4}/{\beta}}(y-\sqrt{2}T)}df)\,\frac{dy}{\sqrt{2\pi T}}.

Hence this probability is differentiable in
x
and in fact

\frac{d}{dx}\mathfrak{s}(e^{\sqrt{{4}/{\beta}}x}df)=\int_{J}(\sqrt{2}-(x-y)/T)e^{-(y-x-\sqrt{2}T)^{2}/(2T)}\widehat{\mathfrak{s}}(y-\sqrt{2}T,e^{\sqrt{{4}/{\beta}}(y-\sqrt{2}T)}df)\,\frac{dy}{\sqrt{2\pi T}}.

On the assumptions on
x,y
, the factor
(\sqrt{2}-(x-y)/T)
is bounded uniformly over the
x
and
y
considered by
\sqrt{2}+(\log k_{1})^{-\delta}
for some
\delta>0,
and hence we conclude

\frac{d}{dx}\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{\sqrt{{4}/{\beta}}x}df)=(\sqrt{2}+o_{k_{1}})\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{\sqrt{{4}/{\beta}}x}df).

Hence from Gronwall’s inequality, we conclude the claimed estimate.
∎

10.3
Mixing of phase

The final technical estimate that must be done for the decoration, which only concerns the case
\sigma=1
, is the mixing of phase. Specifically, we must estimate the statistical difference between

\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{i\alpha+x}df)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{i2\pi U+x}df),

where
U\overset{\mathscr{L}}{=}\operatorname{Unif}([0,1])
. This is the measure that appears in
\overline{\mathfrak{m}}
in (
2.79
).
Once more we let
F
be the subset of
\mathcal{C}([-2\pi k_{1},0],\mathbb{C})
for which
\max_{\theta}|f(\theta)|>e^{-k_{7}}
.

We use a representation that is similar to (
10.16
), namely that

D_{j}^{o}(x)\eqqcolon e^{iW}\times\widecheck{D}(x),

(10.17)

where
W
is a centered Gaussian variable, independent of
\widecheck{D}
of variance
\tfrac{4}{\beta}(T_{\dagger}-T_{-})
, corresponding to the imaginary part of the increment of
\mathfrak{L}_{T_{\dagger}}-\mathfrak{L}_{T_{-}}
.
Now by virtue of the variance being large, we can make a total variation comparison of
e^{i(\alpha+W)}
to
e^{iU}
.

Lemma 10.4
.

With
\operatorname{dtv}
the total variance distance, for a real Gaussian

Z

of variance
V
and a uniform variable
U
on
[0,1]
, there is an absolute constant
C>0
so that

\sup_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}}\operatorname{dtv}(e^{i(\alpha+Z)},e^{i2\pi U})\leq Ce^{-2\pi^{2}V}.

Proof.

By periodicity, we may take
\alpha\in[0,2\pi]
. We compute the Fourier coefficients of the law of
e^{i(\alpha+Z)}
. Note that the
k
-th coefficient is given by

\mathbb{E}e^{i2\pi k(\alpha+Z)}=e^{i2\pi k\alpha}e^{-2\pi^{2}k^{2}V}.

As the total variation distance is given by integrating the

\text{L}^{1}
–distance of the densities, we have

\sup_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}}\operatorname{dtv}(e^{i(\alpha+Z)},e^{i2\pi U})\leq{2\pi}\sum_{k\neq 0}\bigl|\mathbb{E}e^{i2\pi k(\alpha+Z)}\bigr|\leq Ce^{-2\pi^{2}V}.

∎

This leads to:

Corollary 10.5
.

Over any subset
F\subseteq\mathcal{C}((-2\pi k_{1},0),{\mathbb{C}})
for which
e^{i\alpha}F=F
for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\sup_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}}d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{i\alpha+x}df),\mathbb{E}\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{i2\pi U+x}df))\leq Ce^{-(8/\beta)\pi^{2}(T_{\dagger}-T)}\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{x}F).

Proof.

Rescaling both sides of the equation by
\mathfrak{s}(e^{x}F),
we then have a bound for the bounded variation distance of two probability measures,

\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{i\alpha+x}df)/\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{x}F)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{i2\pi U+x}df)/\mathfrak{s}(x,e^{x}F).

From (
10.17
), we have that the phase
e^{iW}
is independent of
\widecheck{D}
, and hence we have a coupling of these two measures that holds with probability
1-\operatorname{dtv}(e^{i(\alpha+Z)},e^{i2\pi U})
. By the definition of bounded–Lipschitz metric, the claimed bound follows.
∎

Appendices

Appendix A
Point processes

We record some elementary properties about point processes, and in particular approximation of point processes by Poisson processes.
We will let
\Gamma
be a complete separable metric space, with a metric
\partial_{0}
which is bounded by
1.

We will use the metrics
\partial_{1}
and
\partial_{2}
from the introduction.
We also define two metrics over finite point measures on
\Gamma
and their laws: for any finite point measures

\xi_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\delta_{y_{i}}
and
\xi_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{z_{i}}

on
\Gamma
,

d_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\coloneqq\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }m=n=0,\\
\min_{\pi}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{0}(y_{i},z_{\pi(i)}),&\text{if }m=n>0,\\
1,&\text{if }m\neq n,\\
\end{cases}

with the minimum being
over all permutations of
\{1,2,\dots,n\}.

Finally, for two laws on point processes
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
we let

d_{2}(Q_{1},Q_{2})\coloneqq\inf_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})}\mathbb{E}(d_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})).

We note that it is possible to bound

\partial_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\leq d_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\max\{|\xi_{1}|,|\xi_{2}|\}

where
|\xi_{j}|
is the cardinality of the point set for
j=1,2
,
and hence

\partial_{2}(Q_{1},Q_{2})\leq\inf_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})}\biggl\{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(d_{1}^{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}))}\sqrt{\vskip 30.00005pt\mathbb{E}(|\xi_{1}|^{2}+|\xi_{2}|^{2})}\biggr\}.

(A.1)

We will formulate a corollary of
[
CX11
]
for Poisson process approximation with local dependency structure.
We let
\left\{\Xi_{i}:i\in\mathcal{I}\right\}
be a set of Bernoulli point processes, so that
\Xi_{i}(\Gamma)\in\{0,1\}.
We will suppose that
elements of this set have local dependence, in that for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
there are sets
A_{i}\subseteq B_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{I}
with
i\in A_{i}
so that

\forall~i\in\mathcal{I}\quad\{\Xi_{j}:j\in A_{i}\}\quad\text{is independent of}\quad\{\Xi_{j}:j\in\mathcal{I}\setminus B_{i}\}.

(A.2)

For all
i\in\mathcal{I}
we let
{\lambda}_{i}
be the intensity measure of
\Xi_{i}
, and we set
\Xi:=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\Xi_{i}
,
{\lambda}\coloneqq\sum_{i}\mathbf{\lambda}_{i}
and
\Lambda\coloneqq{\lambda}(\Gamma).

Theorem A.1
.

Suppose
\left\{\Xi_{i}:i\in\mathcal{I}\right\}
satisfy (
A.2
), and define for any
i\in\mathcal{I},

T_{i}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in A_{i}\setminus\{i\}}\Xi_{i}(\Gamma),\quad P_{i}\coloneqq\Xi_{i}(\Gamma),\quad\text{and}\quad L_{i}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}\setminus B_{i}}\mathbb{E}\bigl(P_{j}\bigr).

Then there is a numerical constant
C>0
so that for a Poisson process
\Pi
with intensity
\lambda,

\partial_{2}(\Xi,\Pi)\leq C(\operatorname{Var}(\Xi(\Gamma))+3\Lambda)^{3/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\biggl(\frac{\mathbb{E}(P_{i})\mathbb{E}(T_{i})+\mathbb{E}(T_{i}P_{i})+(\mathbb{E}(P_{i}))^{2}}{L_{i}^{2}}\biggr).

Proof.

We start by using
[
CX11
, (2.6)]
to establish that there is a numerical constant
C>0
so that

d_{2}(\Xi,\Pi)\leq C\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\biggl(\frac{1}{L_{i}}+\frac{\operatorname{Var}(\Xi(\Gamma))}{L_{i}^{2}}\biggr)\cdot\bigl(\mathbb{E}(P_{i})\mathbb{E}(T_{i})+\mathbb{E}(T_{i}P_{i})+(\mathbb{E}(P_{i}))^{2}\bigr).

(A.3)

We note that in the notation of that equation
|V_{i}|=T_{i},
and their

\lambda

are our
\lambda.
Finally, we bound their
\kappa_{i}
by

\operatorname{Var}(|\Xi(\Gamma)|)/L_{i},

and we bound
\sqrt{\kappa_{i}}
by
1+\kappa_{i}.

We also note that
[
CX11
]
requires that
\Gamma
is a locally compact metric space. As
\lambda
is a finite Borel measure on
\Gamma,
it is tight. Hence there are compact sets
K_{i}
so that with
\tilde{\Gamma}=\cup_{i=1}^{\infty}K_{i},

\lambda(\Gamma\setminus\tilde{\Gamma})=0.
The space
\tilde{\Gamma}
is locally compact, and both point processes
\Xi
and
\Pi
put
0
mass on
\Gamma\setminus\tilde{\Gamma}
with probability
1.
Hence we may apply the Theorem of
[
CX11
]
to
\Xi
and
\Pi
considered as point processes on
\tilde{\Gamma}
with the same metrics
\partial_{0},d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. Furthermore, coupling the point processes as random measures on
\tilde{\Gamma}
gives a coupling of the point processes as random measures on
\Gamma,
and hence (
A.3
) follows.

From (
A.1
), and using that
d_{1}
is bounded by
1,

\partial_{2}(\Xi,\Pi)\leq d_{2}(\Xi,\Pi)\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(\Xi(\Gamma))+3\Lambda}.

As
L_{i}\leq\Lambda,
the claim follows.
∎

We also formulate a change-of-intensity lemma that controls the distance between two Poisson processes of similar intensity. Define for any two finite Borel measures
\pi
and
\lambda
on
\Gamma,

d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\pi,\lambda)=\sup_{f}\biggl|\int_{\Gamma}fd(\pi-\lambda)\biggr|,

(A.4)

with the supremum over all
f
with both
|f(x)-f(y)|\leq\partial_{0}(x,y)
for all
x,y\in\Gamma
and
|f(x)|\leq 1
for all
x\in\Gamma.
Then:

Theorem A.2
.

Let
\pi
and
\lambda
be two finite measures on
\Gamma.

Let
\alpha=\min\{\pi(\Gamma),\lambda(\Gamma)\}.
Then

d_{2}(\Pi(\pi),\Pi(\lambda))\leq\tfrac{1-e^{-\alpha}}{\alpha}d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\pi,\lambda)\quad\text{and}\quad\partial_{2}(\Pi(\pi),\Pi(\lambda))\leq\sqrt{2}\max\{\pi(\Gamma),\lambda(\Gamma)\}\tfrac{1-e^{-\alpha}}{\alpha}d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\pi,\lambda).

Proof.

The first is nearly a corollary of
[
BX95
, Theorem 1.2]
.
To derive it, apply Lemma 2.6 directly to the second line of the displayed equation at the top of
[
BX95
, p.259]
.
The second follows from the first by using (
A.1
).
∎

Corollary A.3
.

For any compact
\Gamma
, and for any
\epsilon>0
, there is a finite list
f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{n}
of non-negative functions
f_{j}
with
|f_{j}(x)-f_{j}(y)|\leq\partial_{0}(x,y)
and
|f_{j}(x)|\leq 1
for all
x,y\in\Gamma
and a
\delta
so that if

\max_{j=1,\dots,n}\biggl|\int_{\Gamma}f_{j}d(\pi-\lambda)\biggr|\leq\delta

then

\partial_{2}(\Pi(\pi),\Pi(\lambda))\leq\epsilon.

Proof.

By Arzelà–Ascoli, we can find a list
\{g_{j}\}
for
j=1,\dots,n
so that for all
1
–Lipschitz, 1–bounded
f
,

\min_{j}\sup_{x\in\Gamma}|f(x)-g_{j}(x)|\leq\epsilon/2.

Let
\{f_{j}\}
be all the positive and negative parts of the functions in this list.
We may also assume the constant-1 function is in the list. Now suppose that

\max_{j=1,\dots,n}\biggl|\int_{\Gamma}f_{j}d(\pi-\lambda)\biggr|\leq\delta.

Then
|\pi(\Gamma)-\lambda(\Gamma)|\leq\delta
, and so by Theorem
A.2

\partial_{2}(\Pi(\pi),\Pi(\lambda))\leq\sqrt{2}\max\{\pi(\Gamma),\lambda(\Gamma)\}\tfrac{1-e^{-\alpha}}{\alpha}d_{\operatorname{BL}}(\pi,\lambda)\leq\sqrt{2}\tfrac{\max\{\pi(\Gamma),\lambda(\Gamma)\}}{\max\{\pi(\Gamma),\lambda(\Gamma)\}-{\delta}}(\epsilon/2+\delta).

If
\max\{\pi(\Gamma),\lambda(\Gamma)\}=0,
there was nothing to prove in the first place. Otherwise by taking
\delta
sufficiently small, we conclude the claim.
∎

Appendix B
Auxilliary one and two-rays estimates

We collect in this appendix the key two-rays estimates used in the bulk
of the evolution. The argument were developed in details in
[
CMN18
]
,
and in fact even our tex file is based on theirs. The
hurried reader may skip the content of
the appendix, keeping only the statements of Propositions

B.2
,
B.3
and Lemmas

B.4
,
B.5
,
B.6
,

B.8
and
B.9

in mind.

Throughout, we adopt exactly, for ease of reference,
the notation of
[
CMN18
]
.

Further notation:

For any
n\geq p
,
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
, we define

\displaystyle Z_{n}^{(p)}(\theta)\coloneqq Z_{n}(\theta)-Z_{p}(\theta)=\sum_{j=p}^{n-1}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{\mathbb{C}}}{\sqrt{j+1}}e^{i((j+1)\theta+A_{j}(\theta))}.

We stress that
Z_{n}^{(p)}
depends implicitly of
\beta
because of the Prüfer phase. Similarly,
we define

\displaystyle A_{n}^{(p)}(\theta)\coloneqq\sum_{j=p}^{n-1}a_{j}(\theta)=A_{n}(\theta)-A_{p}(\theta),\ .

where

a_{j}(\theta)\coloneqq A_{j+1}(\theta)-A_{j}(\theta).

More generally, for any family of quantities depending on an index
k
, we will denote the difference
of the quantities indexed by
k
and
p
by the same notation, with
k
as an index and
p
as
an superscript.

In the following, it will be convenient to study the field at times which are powers of
2
.

B.1
The Chhaibi-Madaule-Najnudel coupling

As in
[
CMN18
]
,
we introduce a new process in order to gain more independence. Recall that

N=\log_{2}n
.

A more independent field:

For each fixed
\theta
,
(Z_{2^{k}}(\theta))_{k\geq 0}
is a complex Gaussian random walk. Moreover we
could compute the correlations of
Z_{2^{k}}(\theta)
and
Z_{2^{k}}(\theta^{\prime})
and observe that
they behave logarithmically with respect to the distance between
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
modulo
2\pi
.
However,
Z
is not globally Gaussian, so we cannot directly apply known results on the maximum of Gaussian fields,
but we will still provide its approximative branching structure. To achieve this aim we will gain
some independence by making small changes on
Z
.

Let us fix some integer
r
, which will be assumed to be larger than some suitable universal constant.
For
l\geq r
, we denote

\Delta=\Delta^{(l)}\coloneqq e^{\sqrt{\log r}}+100\lfloor\log^{2}l\rfloor
. Observe that for any
N\geq r
, we can rewrite formula
[
CMN18
, (3.3)]
as:

\displaystyle Z_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r})}(\theta)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=r}^{N-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta}-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}+j}\frac{e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}+j}(\theta)}}{\sqrt{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}+j+1}}\right).

(B.1)

Note that
\Delta^{(l)}
and
l-\Delta^{(l)}

are strictly positive if
l\geq r
and
r
is large enough.
Now, let
Z^{(2^{r},\Delta)}
be the process defined by

\displaystyle Z_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=r}^{N-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta}-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}+j}e^{ij\theta}\right)\frac{e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}}(\theta)}}{\sqrt{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}}}.

(B.2)

Observe that
Z_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r})}(\theta)
and
Z_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)
only differ
by the change in the square root of the denominator, and by the replacement of
some increments of the Prüfer phases by their mean. The following is a slight variation of
[
CMN18
, Proposition 5.2]
; the additional statement
(
B.4
) actually follows from the proof
in
[
CMN18
]
.

Proposition B.1
.

For
r
large enough,

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{\theta\in[0,2\pi)}|Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l},\Delta)}(\theta)-Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}(\theta)|\geq 2l^{-2}\right)<+\infty

In particular, as
\sum_{l\geq r}2l^{-2}<+\infty
, we have that almost surely,

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}\sup_{\theta\in[0,2\pi)}|Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l},\Delta)}(\theta)-Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}(\theta)|<\infty\

(B.3)

and

\displaystyle\limsup_{r\to\infty}\sum_{l\geq r}\sup_{\theta\in[0,2\pi)}|Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l},\Delta)}(\theta)-Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}(\theta)|=0.

(B.4)

In the proof of Proposition
B.1
,

[
CMN18
]
introduce the variables,
for any
l\geq 0
,
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
,

\displaystyle\blacksquare_{l}(\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l},\Delta)}(\theta)-Z_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}(\theta)

(B.5)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta}-1}\frac{e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}}(\theta)}}{\sqrt{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}}}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}+j}e^{ij\theta}\square_{j}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta})}(\theta)\right)\ ,

with

\displaystyle\left|\square_{j}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta})}(\theta)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\left|A_{j+2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta}}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta})}(\theta)\right|+2^{-\Delta}.

(B.6)

The main steps in the proof consist of the estimates

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\blacksquare_{l}(\theta)|\geq l^{-2}\right)\ll_{\beta}e^{-2^{\frac{1}{4}\Delta}}.

(B.7)

and, for
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}
:

\displaystyle\sup\left({\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{\lambda\Re\blacksquare_{l}(\theta)}\mathds{1}_{G_{l}(\theta)}\Big|\mathcal{G}_{2^{l}}\right),{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{\lambda\Im\blacksquare_{l}(\theta)}\mathds{1}_{G_{l}(\theta)}\Big|\mathcal{G}_{2^{l}}\right)\right)\leq

\displaystyle e^{\lambda^{2}\left(2^{-\frac{1}{4}\Delta}+2^{-\Delta}\right)^{2}}\ .

(B.8)

(see
[
CMN18
, (5.6), (5.8)]
.)

B.2
One and two ray estimates

In the sequel, for all fields denoted by
Z
with some indices and superscripts, we write
R
with the same indices
and superscripts for the real part of
\sigma
times the initial field (recall that
\sigma\in\{1,i,-i\}
).

An envelope for the paths of
R_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)
:

For
j\geq r
, let

\displaystyle\tau^{(r)}_{j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{l=r}^{j-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}\frac{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}=\sum_{l=r}^{j-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}\frac{1}{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}+p}=:(j-r)\log 2+\lambda_{j}^{(r)},

(B.9)

where
(\lambda_{j}^{(r)})_{j\geq r}
is a nonnegative and
increasing sequence, tending, when
j\rightarrow\infty
, to a limit
\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}
such that

\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}<\sum_{l\geq r}2^{-\Delta^{(l)}}\leq 2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}2^{-100\lfloor\log^{2}l\rfloor}\ll 2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}\to 0

when
r
goes to
\infty
.
Let
\alpha_{+}\coloneqq 1-\frac{1}{10}
and
\alpha_{-}\coloneqq\frac{1}{10}
,
and for
N\geq 2r
,
k\in[|r,N|]
, define

\displaystyle u_{k}^{(N)}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-k^{\alpha_{-}},&\text{if }k\leq\lfloor N/2\rfloor,\\
-(N-k)^{\alpha_{-}}-\frac{3}{4}\log N,&\text{if }\lfloor N/2\rfloor<k\leq N,\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle l_{k}^{(N)}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-k^{\alpha_{+}},&\text{if }k\leq\lfloor N/2\rfloor,\\
-(N-k)^{\alpha_{+}}-\frac{3}{4}\log N,&\text{if }\lfloor N/2\rfloor<k\leq N.\end{array}\right.

We then define an envelope by its lower bound and its upper bound at each
k\in[|r,N|]
:

\displaystyle U_{k}^{(N)}\coloneqq\tau^{(r)}_{k}+u_{k}^{(N)}\quad\text{and }\quad L_{k}^{(N)}\coloneqq\tau^{(r)}_{k}+l_{k}^{(N)}.

Fix
x<0
(depending on
k_{2}
),
z<0
(depending on
k_{1}^{+}
), and
\upsilon\in(0,1)
. Set
N_{+}=\log n_{1}^{+}
. The basic event we need to consider is, for
\theta\in[0,2\pi)_{2^{N}}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta)=\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta,x,z)\coloneqq

\displaystyle{\{\forall k\in[|r,N_{1}^{+}|],\,L_{k}^{(N)}\leq x+R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)\leq U_{k}^{(N)},x+R_{2^{N_{+}}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)\in\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\}}.

We will consider
x\in[-r^{1/20},-r^{19/20}]
and
z\in[-(k_{1}^{+})^{1/20},-(k_{1}^{+})^{19/20}]
.
The walk
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{r\leq k\leq N}
is a Gaussian random walk whose distribution is the same as
\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}(W_{\tau_{j}^{(r)}})_{r\leq k\leq N}
.
In the case where an event
\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta)
occurs for some
\theta\in[0,2\pi)_{2^{N}}
, it means that

x+R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)
is around
\tau_{k}^{(r)}
for
r\leq k\leq N
, i.e. the Brownian motion
W
is roughly growing linearly with rate
\sqrt{2}
. For this reason, in the sequel of this part of the paper,
we will often compare the probability of an event
Ev

concerning the random walk
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{r\leq k\leq N}
to the probability of a similar event
GEv
, where
a linear function
t\mapsto t\sqrt{2}
has been subtracted from the possible trajectories of the underlying Brownian motion
W
for which the event
Ev
is satisfied. If
Ev
depends only on the trajectory of
W
up to a certain time
T
, we get, by using the Girsanov transfomation, an equality of the form

{\mathbb{P}}[Ev((W_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T})]={\mathbb{P}}[GEv((W_{t}-t\sqrt{2})_{0\leq t\leq T})]={\mathbb{E}}[e^{-\sqrt{2}W_{T}-T}\mathds{1}_{GEv((W_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T})}],

and then the inequality

{\mathbb{P}}[Ev]\leq e^{-T-\sqrt{2}\mu}{\mathbb{P}}[GEv]

where
\mu
denotes the smallest possible value of
W_{T}
for which the event
GEv
can occur.

The following proposition gives a lower bound for the first moment of
\mathfrak{I}_{N}
.

Proposition B.2

(First moment of
\mathfrak{I}_{N}
)
.

For any
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
large enough
and
N
large enough depending on
r
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta))

\displaystyle\geq e^{2(x-z)}2^{r-N_{+}}e^{-2\upsilon-2\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,N}),

(B.14)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta))

\displaystyle\leq e^{2(x-z)}2^{r-N_{+}}e^{2\upsilon+2\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,N}),

(B.15)

where, with
W
being a standard Brownian motion,

\displaystyle Event_{r,N}=Event_{r,N}(x,z)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left\{\forall j\in[|r,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}\leq x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{j}}\leq u_{j}^{(N)},x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}\in\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\}.

Further, for
x,z
as above, the asymptotics of
{\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,N})
are given
in Lemma
C.1
below.

Proof.

Since
(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{r\leq j\leq N_{+}}
is a Gaussian random walk whose distribution does not depend on
\theta
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta)\right)=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\forall j\in[|r,N_{+}|],\,L_{j}^{(N)}\leq x+R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(0)\leq U_{j}^{(N)},x+R_{2^{N_{+}}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(0)\in\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right).

More precisely, we know that
(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(0))_{j\geq r}
is distributed like
\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}(W_{\tau^{(r)}_{j}})_{j\geq r}
. By Girsanov’s transform, with density
e^{\sqrt{2}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}-\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta))

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}\left(W_{\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}+\sqrt{2}\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}\right)+\tau_{N_{+}}^{(r)}}\mathds{1}_{\{\forall j\in[|r,N_{+}]|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}\leq x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{j}}\leq u_{j}^{(N)},x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)}\}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}}\mathds{1}_{\{\forall j\in[|r,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}\leq x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{j}}\leq u_{j}^{(N)},x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{N_{+}}}\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{r-N_{+}}e^{2(x-z)-2\upsilon-\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Event_{r,N}\right),

with a similar upper bound replacing
-2\upsilon-\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}
by

2\upsilon+\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}
.
Using Lemma
C.1
completes the proof of
the right inequality in (
B.14
).
∎

We will also need a similar estimate for shorter times.
Let
t<N/4
and set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{t,f}(\theta)=\mathfrak{I}_{N,t}(\theta,x,z):=

\displaystyle{\{\forall k\in[|r,t|],\,L_{k}^{(N)}\leq x+R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)\leq U_{k}^{(N)},x+R_{2^{t}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)\in\tau^{(r)}_{t}+[z,z+\upsilon)\}}.

Note that because of our choice of
t
,
only the first part of the barrier is employed in
\mathfrak{I}_{t,f}
,
and therefore
\mathfrak{I}_{t,f}(\theta)
does not depend on
N
. We have the following
analogue of Proposition
B.2
.

Proposition B.3

(First moment of
\mathfrak{I}_{N,t}
)
.

For any
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
C>0
,
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
large enough,

x
as above,
z\in[-\sqrt{t}/C,-C\sqrt{t}]

and
t
large enough depending on
r
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{I}_{t,f}(\theta))

\displaystyle\geq e^{2(x-z)}2^{r-N_{+}}e^{-2\upsilon-2\lambda_{\infty}^{(r)}}{\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,t,f})\gg_{C}\frac{|xz|}{t^{3/2}}e^{2(x-z)}2^{r-t},

(B.16)

where, with
W
being a standard Brownian motion,

\displaystyle Event_{r,t,f}=Event_{r,N,t}(x,z):=

\displaystyle\left\{\forall j\in[|r,t|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}\leq x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{j}}\leq u_{j}^{(N)},x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{t}}\in\tau^{(r)}_{t}+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\}.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition
B.2
, and is omitted.

B.3
Two rays estimate

We will bound here

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})={\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime},x,z,x^{\prime},z^{\prime}):={\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta,x,z)\cap\mathfrak{I}_{N}(\theta^{\prime},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})).

(B.17)

(For the sake of shortness of notation, when no confusion occurs
we write
{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
, omitting the
x,z,x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
from the notation.)
This study, which is technical, is based on the fact that the random walks

(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{r\leq j\leq N}
and
(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}))_{r\leq j\leq N}
are Gaussian random walks whose increments are approximately independent after some branching time which is roughly minus
the logarithm in base
2
of the distance modulo
2\pi
between
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
.

The general idea is as follows. For given
\theta,\theta^{\prime}
, we will consider the integer
\mathtt{k}
such that
2^{-\mathtt{k}}\leq\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}<2^{-(\mathtt{k}-1)}
,
||\cdot||
denoting the distance on the set
{\mathbb{R}}/2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}
. One can understand
\mathtt{k}
as the time of (approximate) branching between the field at
\theta
and at
\theta^{\prime}
. We will show that after some time
\mathtt{k}_{+}=\mathtt{k}_{+}(\mathtt{k})
“slightly larger” than
\mathtt{k}
, we are able to bring out independence between the increments of
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{k\geq r}
and
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}))_{k\geq r}
. By analogy with the Gaussian field, we will see this time as a time of decorrelation. It is defined as follows:

•

For
\mathtt{k}\leq N/2
, the time of decorrelation is
\mathtt{k}_{+}:=\mathtt{k}+3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}
. Recall that:

\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}:=e^{\sqrt{\log r}}+100\lfloor\log^{2}\mathtt{k}\rfloor\ .

In particular, for
\mathtt{k}\leq r/2
and
r
large enough, the walks
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{k\geq r}
and
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}))_{k\geq r}
will have “almost independent increments” from the starting time
r
, since
r\leq\mathtt{k}_{+}
.

•

For
N/2<\mathtt{k}\leq N_{+}
, we will require a faster decorrelation. We take
\mathtt{k}_{+}:=\mathtt{k}+3\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}
, where

\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}:=\lfloor r/100\rfloor+100\lfloor\log(N-\mathtt{k})\rfloor^{2}.

However, the price to pay is that we will have to modify our field
(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{r\leq j\leq N}
in the spirit of subsection
B.1
.

Our two rays estimates are divided to three lemmas,
numbered
B.4
,
B.5
and
B.6
.
The statement of each of these lemmas gives a suitable majorization of
{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
, for a given range of values of
\mathtt{k}
.

Lemma B.4

(Time of branching
\mathtt{k}\leq N/2
)
.

For any
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
r
large enough,
N
large enough depending on
r
,
\mathtt{k}\leq\frac{N}{2}
and
2^{-\mathtt{k}}\leq\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}<2^{-(\mathtt{k}-1)}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\ll\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|zz^{\prime}xx^{\prime}|2^{2r-2N_{+}+2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})},&\text{when }\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq r,\\
|zz^{\prime}x|e^{2(x-z-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N_{+}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{-\mathtt{k}_{+}^{\alpha_{-}}},&\text{when }\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq r.\end{array}\right.

The following lemma studies pairs whose time of branching happen before
r/2
. It refines the estimate obtained in the previous lemma.

Lemma B.5

(Time of branching
\mathtt{k}\leq r/2
)
.

For any
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
large enough,
\mathtt{k}\leq\frac{r}{2}
,
2^{-\mathtt{k}}\leq\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}\leq 2^{-(\mathtt{k}-1)}
and
N
large enough depending on
r
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta_{r,\upsilon})2^{2r-2N_{+}}e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}N^{3}{\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,N}(x,z)){\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,N}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})),

where

\underset{\upsilon\rightarrow 0}{\limsup}\,\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{\limsup}\,\eta_{r,\upsilon}=0.

Lemma B.6

(Time of branching
N/2<\mathtt{k}\leq N_{+}
)
.

For
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
large enough,
N
large enough depending on
r
,
N/2<\mathtt{k}\leq N_{+}
and
2^{-\mathtt{k}}\leq\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}<2^{-(\mathtt{k}-1)}
, we have
\mathtt{k}_{+}<N
and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\ll|xz|e^{2(x-z-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N_{+}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}},

(B.20)

Remark B.7
.

The proof of the Lemma
B.6
is the unique place where we use
(l_{j}^{(N)})_{r\leq j\leq N}
, the lower part of the envelope.

B.3.1
Dyadic case

We start by assuming
\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}=2^{-\mathtt{k}}
and prove Lemmas
B.4
and
B.6
in this dyadic case. This part is mainly for pedagogical purposes while laying the ground for the general case. It illustrates perfectly the machinery of the proof in a simpler setting.

It will be convenient to
denote, for any
l\in[|r,N_{+}|]
,
p\in[|0,2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1|]
,

\displaystyle I_{l,p}(\theta):=\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}+j}e^{i\theta j}}{\sqrt{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}}}\eqlaw{\mathcal{N}}^{{\mathbb{C}}}\left(0,(2^{\Delta^{(l)}}+p)^{-1}\right).

(B.21)

Recall that
R_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)
and
R_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})
can be written as

R_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)=\Re\left(\sigma\sum_{l=r}^{N-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}I_{l,p}(\theta)e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}(\theta)}\right),

R_{2^{N}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})=\Re\left(\sigma\sum_{l=r}^{N-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}(\theta^{\prime})}\right).

The crucial observation
is that for any
l\in[|\mathtt{k}_{+},N_{+}|]

(we easily check that
\mathtt{k}_{+}<N_{+}
if
r
is large enough,
N\geq r
and
\mathtt{k}\leq N/2
) and any
p\leq 2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1
, the random variables
I_{l,p}(\theta)
and
I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})
are independent and identically distributed. Indeed, they form a complex Gaussian vector, and they are uncorrelated, since for
l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
, one has
l-\Delta^{(l)}\geq\mathtt{k}
if
l\geq r
and
r
is large enough,
and then

\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}e^{i(\theta-\theta^{\prime})j}=\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}e^{\pm 2ij\pi/2^{\mathtt{k}}}=0.

We deduce that the increments of
R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)
and
R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})
after the time
\mathtt{k}_{+}
are independent and identically distributed. Recalling the definition of
\tau^{(r)}
in (
B.9
) and (
B.21
), it follows that

\displaystyle(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta))_{j\geq r}\eqlaw(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}))_{j\geq r}\eqlaw\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}(W_{\tau_{j}^{(r)}})_{j\geq r}.

For any
k\in[|r,N_{+}|]
,
x,z<0
, we introduce the events

\displaystyle Ev(k,x,z)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\forall j\in[|k,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{j}^{(k)}}-\tau^{(k)}_{j}+x\leq u_{j}^{(N)},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}}-\tau^{(k)}_{N_{+}}+x\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\},

\displaystyle GEv(k,x,z)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\forall j\in[|k,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{j}^{(k)}}+x\leq u_{j}^{(N)},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}}+x\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\},

(B.22)

Note that
GEv(r,x,z)=Event_{r,N}(x,z)
from Proposition
B.2
, we only keep the separate notation for
compatibility with
[
CMN18
]
. Furthermore note that
GEv(k,x,z)
is equal to the event obtained from
Ev(k,x,z)
after Girsanov transform with density
\exp\left(\sqrt{2}W_{\tau^{(k)}_{N_{+}}}-\tau^{(k)}_{N_{+}}\right)
. Performing the transform yields:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(k,x,z)\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}}{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}W_{\tau^{(k)}_{N_{+}}}}\mathds{1}_{GEv(k,x,z)}\right)\leq 2^{k-N_{+}}e^{2(x-z+\upsilon)}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(k,x,z)\right),

(B.23)

where in the last inequality we used the definition (
B.9
) of
\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}
and the fact that
e^{-\sqrt{2}W_{\tau^{(k)}_{N_{+}}}}\leq N^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{2(x-z+\upsilon)}
on
GEv(k,x,z)
.

In order to allow for more flexibility and for later use, let us record the following analogous events. For
k\in[|r,N_{+}|]
,
a\geq 0
,
E=(E_{j})_{j\geq k}
a sequence of reals such that
(\tau_{j}^{(k)}-E_{j})_{j\geq k}
is positive and nondecreasing,
x,z\in{\mathbb{R}}
, define

\displaystyle Ev(k,a,E,x.z)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\forall j\in[|k,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}-a\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{j}^{(k)}-E_{j}}+x-\tau^{(k)}_{j}\leq u_{j}^{(N)}+a,\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}-E_{N_{+}}}+x-\tau^{(k)}_{N_{+}}\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\},

(B.24)

\displaystyle GEv(k,a,E,x,z)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\forall j\in[|k,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}-a\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{j}^{(k)}-E_{j}}+x\leq u_{j}^{(N)}+a,\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}-E_{N_{+}}}+x\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\}.

(B.25)

Again, the event
GEv(k,a,E,x,z)
is, up to an error due to the time shift
E
, ”quasi equal” to what we obtain when we apply the Girsanov’ transform with density

\exp\left(\sqrt{2}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}-E_{N_{+}}}-(\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}-E_{N_{+}})\right)

to the event
Ev(k,a,E,x,z)
. This time, the inequality takes the form:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(k,a,E,x,z)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{k-N_{+}}e^{-E_{N_{+}}+2(x-z+a+\upsilon)}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(k,a+\sup_{k\leq j\leq N}|E_{j}|,E,x,z)\right).

(B.26)

Indeed, by the Girsanov transform and then using the barrier at time
N_{+}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(k,a,E,x,z)\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}+E_{N_{+}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}W_{\tau^{(k)}_{N_{+}}-E_{N_{+}}}}\mathds{1}_{\left\{\forall j\in[|k,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}-a\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{j}^{(k)}-E_{j}}+x-E_{j}\leq u_{j}^{(N)}+a\right\}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\mathds{1}_{\left\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}-E_{N_{+}}}+x\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{k-N_{+}}e^{E_{N_{+}}+2(x-z-E_{N_{+}}+a+\upsilon)}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\forall j\in[|k,N_{+}|],l_{j}^{(N)}\!-\!a\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{j}^{(k)}-E_{j}}+x-E_{j}\leq u_{j}^{(N)}\!+\!a,\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(k)}-E_{N_{+}}}+x\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+\upsilon)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{k-N_{+}}e^{-E_{N_{+}}+2(x-z+a+\upsilon)}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(k,a+\sup_{k\leq j\leq N_{+}}|E_{j}|,E,x,z)\right).

Proof of Lemma
B.4
in dyadic case.

When
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq r
:

The increments of
R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)
and
R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})
after the time
\mathtt{k}_{+}
are independent and identically distributed, thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta)=\quad

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,x,z)\right){\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Eq.\eqref{eq:girsanov1}}}{{\ll_{v}}}

\displaystyle 2^{2r-2N_{+}}e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(r,x,z)\right)\right)\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(r,x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)\right).

Finally by applying
[
CMN18
, (A.15)]

(with
E_{j-r}=\lambda_{j}^{(r)}/\log 2
, which implies
||E||\leq 1
for
r
large enough), we obtain (
B.4
).

When
\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq r
:

The increments of
R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)
and
R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})
after time
\mathtt{k}_{+}
are independent and identically distributed. Moreover,
all these increments are independent of
those of
R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)

for
j
between
r
and
\mathtt{k}_{+}
(we see this fact by first conditioning with respect to the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{G}_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}
). We then have:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime}x,z,x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,x,z)\right)\sup_{x^{\prime}+l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}\leq w\leq x^{\prime}+u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}}{\mathbb{P}}(Ev(\mathtt{k}_{+},w,z^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Eq.\eqref{eq:girsanov1}}}{{\ll}}

\displaystyle 2^{r-N_{+}}e^{2(x-z)}\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(r,x,z)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad 2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}\sup_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}\leq w+x^{\prime}\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}}\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{2(w+x^{\prime}-z)}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},w+x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\ll\quad

\displaystyle|xzz^{\prime}|2^{r-N_{+}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2(x-z-z^{\prime})}\sup_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}\leq w^{\prime}\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}}\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{2(w^{\prime})}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)\right).

If
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq N/4
, according to
[
CMN18
, (A.15]
,
for any
w^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)\ll|w^{\prime}||z^{\prime}|(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\ .

Recalling that
w^{\prime}\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}=-(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}\leq w^{\prime}\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}}\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{2(w^{\prime})}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)\right)\ll e^{-\mathtt{k}_{+}^{\alpha_{-}}}.

(B.27)

On the other hand, for
\mathtt{k}_{+}>N/4
, we can crudely bound
{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},w^{\prime},z;)\right)
by
1
, and using the fact that
w^{\prime}\leq-\mathtt{k}_{+}^{\alpha_{-}}
if
\mathtt{k}_{+}<N/2
and

w^{\prime}\leq-(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}\leq-0.9\mathtt{k}_{+}^{\alpha_{-}}
if
\mathtt{k}\leq N/2\leq\mathtt{k}_{+}
,
we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}\leq w^{\prime}\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}}\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{2w^{\prime}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},w^{\prime},z)\right)\right)\leq e^{2w^{\prime}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}\ll e^{-1.8\mathtt{k}_{+}^{\alpha_{-}}}N^{3/2}\ll e^{-\mathtt{k}_{+}^{\alpha_{-}}},

(B.28)

since
\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq N/5
for
N
large. (Recall that we take
N\to\infty
before we take
k_{1}\to\infty
.)

In all cases, by combining Eq. (
B.27
) and (
B.28
), we deduce:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\ll|xzz^{\prime}|2^{r-N_{+}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2(x-z-z^{\prime})}e^{-\mathtt{k}_{+}^{\alpha_{-}}}.

It concludes the proof of Lemma
B.4
when
\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}
is a negative power of
2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
B.6
in the dyadic case.

Now we shall study
{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
, when the branching between the field in
\theta
and the field in
\theta^{\prime}
appears after the time
N/2
. This time we shall prove that when one restricts to the paths which are in the envelope, the increments of the path of the field at
\theta
and at
\theta^{\prime}
are approximately independent after the time of decorrelation
k_{+}
. We recall that for this range

\mathtt{k}_{+}=\mathtt{k}+3\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}
where
\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}:=\lfloor\frac{r}{100}\rfloor+100\lfloor\log(N-\mathtt{k})\rfloor^{2}
, if

\mathtt{k}\leq N-1
.

We need to exhibit the independence between the increments of the processes
(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\Delta)}(\theta))_{j\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
and
(R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}))_{j\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
. The
crucial observation
we used in case of
\mathtt{k}\leq N/2
does not work anymore for such a short decorrelation time. We first need to modify our field using similar arguments to those used for the proof of Proposition
B.1
.

In the following we shall use the quantity

\displaystyle J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta):=\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+j}e^{i\theta j}}{\sqrt{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}}}\eqlaw{\mathcal{N}}^{{\mathbb{C}}}\left((0,2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+p)^{-1}\right)

Let
\mathtt{k}\in[N/2,N_{+}]
and
2^{-\mathtt{k}}\leq\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}<2^{-(\mathtt{k}-1)}
. We have, for
r
large enough, since
N-\mathtt{k}\geq r/2
is large,

\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq\mathtt{k}+(r/33)+300\log^{2}(N-\mathtt{k})\leq\mathtt{k}+(N-\mathtt{k})/16<N.

Recall that for
\tilde{\theta}\in\{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\}
,
r\leq k\leq N
,
R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\tilde{\theta})=\Re(\sigma Z_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\tilde{\theta}))
. Since for
l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
,
\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}\leq\Delta^{(l)}
, we can write for all
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq k\leq N
,
\tilde{\theta}\in\{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\}
:

\displaystyle Z_{2^{k}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\Delta)}(\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{k-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}I_{l,p}(\tilde{\theta})e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}(\tilde{\theta})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{k-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}+j}e^{i\tilde{\theta}j}}{\sqrt{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}}}e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}(\tilde{\theta})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{k-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+j}e^{i\tilde{\theta}\left\{j-\mathfrak{j}\right\}}}{\sqrt{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+\mathfrak{j}}}e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+\mathfrak{j}}(\tilde{\theta})}\right),\text{(with $\mathfrak{j}=\lfloor\frac{j}{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}\rfloor 2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}$)}

\displaystyle=Z_{2^{k}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\tilde{\theta})+\mathfrak{E}_{2^{k}}(\tilde{\theta})

where

\displaystyle Z_{2^{k}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{k-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\tilde{\theta})e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}(\tilde{\theta})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{2^{k}}(\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{k-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}\frac{e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}(\tilde{\theta})}}{\sqrt{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+j}e^{i\tilde{\theta}j}\lozenge_{j}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta})\right),

with

\displaystyle\left|\lozenge_{j}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\left|A_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+\mathfrak{j}}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta}))\right|+2^{-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}.

(B.29)

Indeed:

\displaystyle\lozenge_{j}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta})=

\displaystyle-1+\sqrt{\frac{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+\mathfrak{j}}}e^{iA_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+\mathfrak{j}}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-1+\left(1+\Theta\right)e^{iA_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+\mathfrak{j}}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta})},

with
|\Theta|\leq 2^{-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}

which implies inequality (
B.29
). In the following, we shall denote:

\displaystyle\blacklozenge^{(\mathtt{k})}_{l}(\tilde{\theta}):=\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}\frac{e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}(\tilde{\theta})}}{\sqrt{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+j}e^{i\tilde{\theta}j}\lozenge_{j}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta})\right).

Notice that, on the contrary of the proof of Proposition
B.1
, where
\Delta^{(l)}
varies with
l
, here we fix
\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}
as soon as we know that
2^{-\mathtt{k}}\leq\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}<2^{-(\mathtt{k}-1)}
. By using the same arguments used to prove (
B.7
) and (
B.8
), one can show similarly that for any
l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
,
\tilde{\theta}\in\{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\blacklozenge^{(\mathtt{k})}_{l}(\tilde{\theta})|\geq 2^{-\frac{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}{8}},\cap_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}\widetilde{G}_{l}(\tilde{\theta})\Big|{\mathcal{G}}_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}\right)\ll e^{-2^{\frac{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}{8}}},

(B.30)

\displaystyle\qquad{\mathbb{P}}\left((\cap_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}\widetilde{G}_{l}(\tilde{\theta}))^{c}\Big|{\mathcal{G}}_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}\right)\ll_{\beta}\exp\left(-\frac{\beta}{33}2^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}\right).

(B.31)

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{l}(\tilde{\theta}):=

\displaystyle\ \bigcap_{p=0}^{2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}\left\{\sup_{0\leq j\leq 2^{l-{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}-1}\left|A_{j+2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}^{(2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}})}(\tilde{\theta})\right|\leq 2^{-\frac{1}{4}\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}\right\}\ .

Moreover it is plain to observe that for any
r
large enough,
N
large enough depending on
r
, and
\mathtt{k}\in[|N/2,N-r/2|]
, and under the complement of the two events just above,

\displaystyle\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{N-1}|\blacklozenge^{(\mathtt{k})}_{l}(\tilde{\theta})|\leq\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{N-1}2^{-\frac{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}{8}}\leq(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})2^{-\frac{1}{8}\lfloor\frac{r}{100}\rfloor-\frac{25}{2}\lfloor\log(N-\mathtt{k})\rfloor^{2}}\leq 1.

(B.32)

So for any
\tilde{\theta}\in\{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\}
, we can replace
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\Delta)}(\tilde{\theta}))_{\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq k\leq N}
by
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\tilde{\theta}))_{\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq k\leq N}
with an error at most
1
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\forall j\in[|r,N_{+}|],\,L_{j}^{(N)}-1\leq x+R_{2^{j\wedge\mathtt{k}_{+}}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)+R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\theta)\mathds{1}_{{\{j>k_{0}\}}}\leq U_{j}^{(N)}+1,\,\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall j\in[|\mathtt{k}_{+},N_{+}|],\,L_{j}^{(N)}-1\leq x^{\prime}+R_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})+R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\theta^{\prime})\leq U_{j}^{(N)}+1,\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.x+R_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta)+R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\theta)\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+v],\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.x^{\prime}+R_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})+R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\theta^{\prime})\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+v]\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{\theta}\in\{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\}}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg(\mathds{1}_{\{\forall j\in[|r,\mathtt{k}_{+}|],\,L_{j}^{(N)}\leq\tilde{x}+R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\tilde{\theta})\leq U_{j}^{(N)}\}}\Big(\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{N_{+}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\blacklozenge^{(\mathtt{k})}_{l}(\tilde{\theta})|\geq 2^{-\frac{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}{8}},\cap_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}\tilde{G}_{l}(\tilde{\theta})\Big|{\mathcal{G}}_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\left((\cap_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}\tilde{G}_{l}(\tilde{\theta}))^{c}\Big|{\mathcal{G}}_{2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}\right)\Big)\Bigg),

\displaystyle=:{\mathbb{P}}_{N}^{(\Delta,\kappa)}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})+Q_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime}),

where
\tilde{x}=x^{\prime}
if
\tilde{\theta}=\theta^{\prime}
and
\tilde{x}=x
otherwise.

We first deal with the sum in
\tilde{\theta}\in\{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\}
, that is with
Q_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
. By using (
B.30
), then
the Girsanov transfom with density
e^{\sqrt{2}W_{\tau^{(r)}_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}}-\tau_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(r)}}
, and
[
CMN18
, Corollary A.6]

(when
\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq\frac{2N}{3}
), the sum is

\displaystyle\ll 2^{r-\mathtt{k}_{+}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{2(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{+}}}\left((N-\mathtt{k}_{+})e^{-2^{\frac{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}{8}}}+\exp\left(-\frac{\beta}{33}2^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}\right)\right),\;\text{when }\,\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq\frac{2N}{3},

\displaystyle\ll 2^{r-\mathtt{k}_{+}}\frac{N^{\frac{3}{2}}(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{2\alpha_{+}}}{(\mathtt{k}_{+}-r)^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{2(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{+}}}\left((N-\mathtt{k}_{+})e^{-2^{\frac{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}{8}}}+\exp\left(-\frac{\beta}{33}2^{\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}\right)\right),\;\text{when }\,\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq\frac{2N}{3}

which are both dominated by
|xz|e^{-(x-z-z^{\prime})}2^{-2N_{+}+\mathtt{k}_{+}+r}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}}
. It remains to bound the expression

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}^{(\Delta,\kappa)}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
.
Let
\tau_{j}^{(r,\mathtt{k}_{+})}:=\tau_{j}^{(r)}
if
j\leq\mathtt{k}_{+}
and
\tau_{j}^{(r,\mathtt{k}_{+})}:=\tau_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(r)}+\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{j-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}(2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+p)^{-1}
if
j\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
and
E^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})}_{j}:=\tau_{j}^{(r)}-\tau_{j}^{(r,\mathtt{k}_{+})}
, for any
r\leq j\leq N_{+}
. It is plain to check that
\sum_{j=r}^{N_{+}}|E^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})}_{j+1}-E^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})}_{j}|\ll 2^{-\frac{r}{100}}
and

\displaystyle(R_{2^{j\wedge\mathtt{k}_{+}}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime})+R_{2^{j}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\theta^{\prime})\mathds{1}_{{\{j>k_{0}\}}})_{r\leq j\leq N}\eqlaw\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}(W_{\tau_{j}^{(r,\mathtt{k}_{+})}})_{j\geq r}\ .

Now it suffices to reproduce the proof of Lemma
B.4
. In this first part, we assume
\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}=2^{-\mathtt{k}}
. In this case, we check the independence of
J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta)
and
J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta^{\prime})
for
l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
, since
\mathtt{k}_{+}-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}\geq\mathtt{k}
. We then show, by doing the suitable conditionings, that the increments of
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\theta))_{\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq k\leq N_{+}}
,
(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})})}(\theta^{\prime}))_{\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq k\leq N_{+}}
,

(R_{2^{k}}^{(2^{r},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}))_{r\leq k\leq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
are independent. Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}^{(\Delta,\kappa)}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,1,E^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})},x,z)\right)\max_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}-1\leq w\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}+1}{\mathbb{P}}(Ev(\mathtt{k}_{+},1,E^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})},w,z^{\prime})).

By the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
B.4
in the dyadic case we have

\displaystyle\max_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}-1\leq w\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}+1}{\mathbb{P}}(Ev(\mathtt{k}_{+},1,E^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})},w,z^{\prime}))\ll_{\upsilon}

\displaystyle|z^{\prime}|2^{-(N_{+}-\mathtt{k}_{+})}\max_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}-1\leq w\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}+1}|e^{2(w-z^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq|z^{\prime}|2^{-(N_{+}-\mathtt{k}_{+})}e^{-2z^{\prime}-(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha}_{-}},

where we used that in the stated range,
|w^{\prime}|\geq(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha}_{-}/2
,
and

{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,1,E^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})},x,z)\right)\ll|xz|2^{-N_{+}+r}.

Finally one gets

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\ll|xzz^{\prime}|2^{r-N_{+}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{-(N-\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}}e^{2(x-z-z^{\prime})}\ .

This concludes the proof of Lemma
B.6
, when
\frac{||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}{2\pi}
is a negative power of
2
.
∎

B.3.2
General case

Proof of Lemma
B.4
in general case.

Fix
\upsilon>0
,
r
large enough,
N
large enough depending on
r
, and
\mathtt{k}
such that
\mathtt{k}\leq\frac{N}{2}
. Unlike the previous dyadic case, for
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq l\leq N-1
and
p\leq 2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1
, the random variables
I_{l,p}(\theta)
and
I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})
are not rigorously independent. However observe that for any
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq l\leq N-1
, the absolute value of their correlations decreases exponentially with
l
. Indeed,
if
C_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime}):={\mathbb{E}}\left(I_{l,p}(\theta)\overline{I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})}\right)
, then

\displaystyle\left|C_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}}\left|\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}-1}e^{i(\theta-\theta^{\prime})j}\right|\leq\frac{4}{2^{l}||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}\ll 2^{\mathtt{k}-l}.

Since
{\mathbb{E}}\left(I_{l,p}(\theta)I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})\right)=0
, and
(I_{l,p}(\theta),I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime}))
is a centered complex Gaussian vector, one checks, by computing covariances, that it is possible to write

I_{l,p}(\theta)=\frac{C_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})}{C_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})+I^{ind}_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime}),

where the two terms of the sums are independent, with an expectation of the square equal to zero. Note that

C_{l,p}:=C_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})=\frac{2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}=(2^{\Delta^{(l)}}+p)^{-1}

does not depend on
\theta^{\prime}
. Moreover, we have by Pythagoras’ theorem:

{\mathbb{E}}[|I_{l,p}^{ind}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})|^{2}]={\mathbb{E}}[|I_{l,p}(\theta)|^{2}]-\left|\frac{C_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})}{C_{l,p}}\right|^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[|I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})|^{2}]=C_{l,p}-\frac{|C_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})|^{2}}{C_{l,p}}

Using this decomposition of
I_{l,p}(\theta)
and the measurability of the different quantities with respect to the
\sigma
-algebras of the form
\mathcal{G}_{j}
, we deduce that one can write:

\displaystyle(R_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\Delta)}(\theta))_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}=(R_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},ind)}+E_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}})})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}\ .

(B.33)

Here
(R_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},ind)})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
is a Gaussian process, independent of
(R_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}))_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
, and distributed as
(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})}_{l}-\mathtt{C}_{l}})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
with

\mathtt{C}_{l}:=\sum_{t=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{l-1}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(t)}}-1}\frac{|C_{t,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})|^{2}}{C_{t,p}}\ .

Notice that
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})}
implicitly depends of
\mathtt{k}_{+}
.
(E_{2^{l}}^{(2^{r})})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
on the other hand is defined by

\displaystyle E_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}=\Re\left(\sigma\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}(\theta)}\frac{C_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})}{C_{l,p}}I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})\right).

(B.34)

Furthermore notice that

Fact 1:

For any
l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|E_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}|\leq|E|_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}:=\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}|C_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})|(2^{\Delta^{(l)}}+p)|I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})|

is measurable with respect to the sigma field
\sigma\left({\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathbb{C}},\,t\in[|2^{l},2^{l+1}-1|]\right)
.

Fact 2:

The process
(R_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},ind)})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
is independent of the couple
(R_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},\Delta)}(\theta^{\prime}),|E|_{2^{l}}^{(2^{r})})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}

Fact 3:

\sup_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}|\mathtt{C}_{l}|\ll 2^{-\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}
if
r
is large enough. Indeed, in this case,
since
\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq 3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}\geq 3e^{\sqrt{\log r}}

is also large, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}|\mathtt{C}_{l}|

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{\infty}\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1}(2^{\mathtt{k}-l})^{2}(2^{\Delta^{(l)}}+p)\ll\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{\infty}2^{2\mathtt{k}-2l+2\Delta^{(l)}}\ll\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{\infty}2^{2\mathtt{k}-2l+2e^{\sqrt{\log r}}+200\log^{2}l}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq l\leq 100\mathtt{k}}2^{2\mathtt{k}-2l+2e^{\sqrt{\log r}}+300\log^{2}\mathtt{k}}+\sum_{l\geq\max(100\mathtt{k},\mathtt{k}_{+})}2^{2\mathtt{k}-2l+0.8\mathtt{k}_{+}+200\log^{2}l}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq l\leq 100\mathtt{k}}2^{2\mathtt{k}-2l+3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}+\sum_{l\geq\max(100\mathtt{k},\mathtt{k}_{+})}2^{2\mathtt{k}+0.8\mathtt{k}_{+}-1.99l}

\displaystyle\ll 2^{2\mathtt{k}-2\mathtt{k}_{+}+3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}+2^{2\mathtt{k}+0.8\mathtt{k}_{+}-1.99[(0.05)(100\mathtt{k})+0.95(\mathtt{k}_{+})]}\ll 2^{-3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}+2^{-\mathtt{k}_{+}}\ll 2^{-3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}.

It means (see
[
CMN18
, Lemma A.5]
)
that the process
(R_{2^{l}}^{(2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}},ind)})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
is very ”similar” to the process

\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}(W_{\tau_{l}^{(\mathtt{k}_{+})}})_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}
.
Moreover, if for
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq r\leq l
,

\mathtt{C}_{l}^{(r)}:=\mathtt{C}_{l}-\mathtt{C}_{r}
, then we have for
r
large enough:

\sup_{l\geq r}|\mathtt{C}_{l}^{(r)}|\leq\sup_{l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}|\mathtt{C}_{l}|\leq 2^{-\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}\leq 2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}\leq 1/2.

Fact 4:

|E|
is small. For any
m\geq 0
,
l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
, we introduce the event

\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(l)}:=\{|E|_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}\geq 2^{-\frac{\Delta^{(l)}}{4}}m\}\ .

For some universal constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
, and
m\geq 1/2
, the probability of
\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(l)}
is smaller than

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|E|_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}\geq 2^{-\frac{\Delta^{(l)}}{4}}m\right)\leq 2^{\Delta^{(l)}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(c2^{\mathtt{k}-l}|I_{l,0}(\theta^{\prime})|\geq m2^{-\frac{9}{4}\Delta^{(l)}}\right)

\displaystyle\ll 2^{\Delta^{(l)}}e^{-c^{\prime}m^{2}2^{2(l-\mathtt{k}-\frac{7}{4}\Delta^{(l)})}}

\displaystyle\ll e^{-c^{\prime}m^{2}2^{2(l-\mathtt{k}-2\Delta^{(l)})}}.

(B.35)

For
r
(and then
\mathtt{k}_{+}
and
l
) large enough and
l\leq 100\mathtt{k}
,
2\Delta^{(l)}\leq 3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}+(\log(c^{\prime})/\log 4)
, and
then

{\mathbb{P}}\left(|E|_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}\geq 2^{-\frac{\Delta^{(l)}}{4}}m\right)\ll e^{-m^{2}2^{2(l-\mathtt{k}_{+})}}.

If
r
is large enough and
l\geq\sup(100\mathtt{k},\mathtt{k}_{+})
(and therefore, also large), we use that

\displaystyle 2(l-\mathtt{k}-2\Delta^{(l)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l-\mathtt{k}_{+}+(l-2\mathtt{k}+\mathtt{k}_{+}-4\Delta^{(l)})=l-\mathtt{k}_{+}+(l-k+3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}-4\Delta^{(l)})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle l-\mathtt{k}+(l-\mathtt{k}-400(\log l)^{2})\geq l-\mathtt{k}+(0.99l-400(\log l)^{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle l-\mathtt{k}-\log c^{\prime},

and then, using (
B.35
),

{\mathbb{P}}\left(|E|_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}\geq 2^{-\frac{\Delta^{(l)}}{4}}m\right)\ll e^{-c^{\prime}m^{2}2^{2(l-\mathtt{k}-2\Delta^{(l)})}}\leq e^{-m^{2}(l-\mathtt{k}_{+})}.

Hence in any case, for
r
large and
l\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(l)}\right)={\mathbb{P}}\left(|E|_{2^{l+1}}^{(2^{l})}\geq 2^{-\frac{\Delta^{(l)}}{4}}m\right)\ll e^{-m^{2}2^{l-\mathtt{k}_{+}}}.

When
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq r
:
Using the decomposition (
B.33
) and Fact 2, and noticing that

\sum_{l=r}^{+\infty}m2^{-\frac{\Delta^{(l)}}{4}}\leq m2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}
,
for
r
large enough, we can affirm that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,0,0,x,z)\right){\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{m\geq 1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,0,0,x,z),\,\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(j)}\right){\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right),

(B.36)

where the Brownian motion involved in the event
Ev(r,0,0,x,z)
is suitably coupled with the complex Gaussian random walk whose increments are of the form

I_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime})e^{i\psi_{2^{l}+p2^{l-\Delta^{(l)}}}(\theta^{\prime})}

for
r\leq l\leq N-1
and
0\leq p\leq 2^{\Delta^{(l)}}-1
.

By using Eq. (
B.26
) and then the fact that

\sup_{r\leq j\leq N}\mathtt{C}^{(r)}_{j}\leq 2^{1-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}

if
r
is large enough, we have for any
m\geq 0
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-2(x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N_{+}}e^{2\upsilon+2(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}-\mathtt{C}^{(r)}_{N}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(r,(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}+\sup_{r\leq j\leq N}\mathtt{C}^{(r)}_{j},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-2(x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N_{+}}e^{2\upsilon+2(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}-\mathtt{C}^{(r)}_{N}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(r,2(m+2)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)\ .

(B.37)

Now, we invoke
[
CMN18
, Corollary A.6]

as, by the Fact 3, with the notation of the corollary,
||E||_{1}\leq 1
if
r
is large enough. Thus, we deduce, for
N
large enough depending on
r
, that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)\ll_{\upsilon}|x^{\prime}z^{\prime}|e^{2(x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N}e^{2m2^{-\frac{r}{400}}}(1+(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}})^{3}\ .

(B.38)

Similarly, to compute
{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,0,0,x,z),\,\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(j)}\right)
we will apply the Girsanov transform with the density
e^{\sqrt{2}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(r)}}-\tau_{N_{+}}^{(r)}}
. It requires to study what is the effect of this density on the event
\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(j)}
.
The increments of the complex random walk which
were
I_{j,p}(\theta^{\prime})e^{i\psi_{2^{j}+p2^{j-\Delta^{(j)}}}(\theta^{\prime})}
before the Girsanov transform, increase
by
\sigma^{-1}C_{j,p}
afterwards. Hence, between the two situations, before and after the Girsanov
transform,
|E|_{2^{j+1}}^{(2^{j})}
, defined as the sum, for
0\leq p\leq 2^{\Delta^{(j)}}-1
, of
the absolute value of the increments of the random walk multiplied by
|C_{j,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})|/C_{j,p}
, vary, for
r
large enough, at most by
2^{2\Delta^{(j)}+\mathtt{k}-j}
, since

\sum_{p=0}^{2^{\Delta^{(j)}}-1}|C_{j,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})|\ll 2^{\Delta^{(j)}+\mathtt{k}-j}.

Now, for
j\geq r\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
and
r
large enough, we have:

2^{2\Delta^{(j)}+\mathtt{k}-j}<\frac{1}{2}2^{-\frac{1}{4}\Delta^{(j)}}.

Indeed, for
j\leq 100\mathtt{k}
,

2^{2\Delta^{(j)}+\mathtt{k}-j}\leq 2^{2\Delta^{(j)}+\mathtt{k}-\mathtt{k}_{+}}\leq 2^{2\Delta^{(j)}-3\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}\leq 2^{-0.9\Delta^{(j)}},

and for
j\geq\max(\mathtt{k}_{+},100\mathtt{k})
(necessarily large, since
r
is large),

\displaystyle 2^{2\Delta^{(j)}+\mathtt{k}-j}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2\Delta^{(j)}-0.99j}\leq 2^{e^{\sqrt{\log r}}+200\log^{2}j-0.99j}\leq 2^{e^{\sqrt{\log j}}+200\log^{2}j-0.99j}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-0.96j}\leq 2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}-0.94j}\leq 2^{-\Delta^{(j)}}.

Hence, if for
m\geq 1
, before (respectively after) the Girsanov transform,
\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(j)}
occurs, then
\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{\frac{1}{2}m}^{(j)}
still occurs after (respectively before) the transform. Finally we get, for any
m\geq 1
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,0,0,x,z),\,\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(j)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{2(x-z)}2^{r-N}{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}W_{\tau_{N}^{(r)}}}\mathds{1}_{\{\forall j\in[|r,N_{+}|],\,l_{j}^{(N)}\leq x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{j}^{(r)}}\leq u_{j}^{(N)}\}}\mathds{1}_{\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{\frac{1}{2}m}^{(j)}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\mathds{1}_{x+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}W_{\tau_{N_{+}}^{(r)}}\in-\frac{3}{4}\log N+[z,z+v)}\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\upsilon}e^{2(x-z)}2^{r-N}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(r,0,0,x,z)\cap\left\{\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{\frac{1}{2}m}^{(j)}\right\}\right)

As
\mathtt{E}_{\frac{m}{2}}^{(l)}
is measurable with respect to
\sigma({\mathcal{N}}_{t}^{\mathbb{C}},\,t\in[|2^{l},2^{l+1}-1|])
, by applying

[
CMN18
, Corollary A.6]

and using the
Fact 4
we get, for
r
large enough and
N
large enough depending on
r
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(r,0,0,x,z)\cap\left\{\cup_{j\in[|r,N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{\frac{1}{2}m}^{(j)}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\upsilon}|xz|e^{2(x-z)}N^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{j\geq r}\sqrt{{\mathbb{P}}(\mathtt{E}_{\frac{1}{2}m}^{(j)})}

\displaystyle\ll|xz|e^{2(x-z)}N^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{j\geq r}e^{-(m^{2}/8)2^{j-\mathtt{k}_{+}}}

\displaystyle\ll|xz|e^{2(x-z)}N^{-\frac{3}{2}}e^{-2^{r-\mathtt{k}_{+}}m^{2}/8},

since
r\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}
.

By combining this inequality with (
B.38
) and (
B.3.2
), we get:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime},x,z,x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\ll_{\upsilon}|xzx^{\prime}z^{\prime}|e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{2(r-N_{+})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{m\geq 1}|xzx^{\prime}z^{\prime}|e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N_{+}}2^{2m2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}}}(1+(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}})^{3}2^{r-N}e^{-(m^{2}/8)2^{r-\mathtt{k}_{+}}}

\displaystyle\leq|xzx^{\prime}z^{\prime}|e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{2(r-N_{+})}\left[1+\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}2^{2m}(1+m)^{3}e^{-m^{2}/8}\right)e^{-(1/8)(2^{r-\mathtt{k}_{+}}-1)}\right]

\displaystyle\ll|xzx^{\prime}z^{\prime}|e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{2(r-N_{+})},

(B.39)

which concludes the case
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq r
.

When
\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq r
:
Using the decomposition (
B.33
) and Fact 2 and noticing that
\sum_{l=\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{+\infty}m2^{-\frac{\Delta^{(l)}}{4}}\leq m2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}

for
r
large enough, we can affirm that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime},x,z,x^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,0,0,x,z),\,\cup_{j\in[|\mathtt{k}_{+},N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(j)}\right)

(B.40)

\displaystyle\sup_{l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}\leq x^{\prime}+w\leq u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(\mathtt{k}_{+},(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C},x^{\prime}+w,z^{\prime})\right).

(B.41)

By a similar computation as what we have done in the case
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq r
, we get:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(r,0,0,x,z),\,\cup_{j\in[|\mathtt{k}_{+},N_{+}|]}\mathtt{E}_{m}^{(j)}\right)\ll_{\upsilon}|xz|e^{2(x-z)}2^{r-N_{+}-(m^{2}/8)}.

(B.42)

On the other hand, by using Eq. (
B.26
), for any
w^{\prime}\in[l_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)},u_{\mathtt{k}_{+}}^{(N)}]
, we obtain:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(\mathtt{k}_{+},(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\upsilon}e^{2(w^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2(m+1)2^{-\frac{r}{400}}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}+\sup_{\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq j\leq N}|\mathtt{C}_{j}|,\mathtt{C},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{2(w^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2(m+1)2^{-\frac{r}{400}}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(GEv(\mathtt{k}_{+},2(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right),

(B.43)

where we used that
\sup_{j\geq\mathtt{k}_{+}}|\mathtt{C}_{j}|\leq 2^{-\Delta^{(\mathtt{k})}}\leq\frac{1}{2}(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}
.

For
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq N/4
, we can use
[
CMN18
, Corollary A.6]

to deduce, for
w^{\prime}\leq u_{\mathtt{k}^{+}}^{(N)}=-(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Ev(\mathtt{k}_{+},(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\ll e^{2(w^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}}(1+2(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8})^{3}|w^{\prime}z^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\ll_{\upsilon}|w^{\prime}z^{\prime}|e^{2(w^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{3(m+1)-(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}}\ .

(B.44)

For
\mathtt{k}_{+}\geq N/4
, we bound the probability of the
GEv
event by
1
and use the fact that
w^{\prime}\leq-0.9(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}

(the factor
0.9
coming from the case
\mathtt{k}\leq N/2\leq\mathtt{k}_{+}
). We then get

\displaystyle P\left(Ev(\mathtt{k}_{+},(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C},w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\ll_{\upsilon}|w^{\prime}z^{\prime}|e^{2(w^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2(m+1)2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}}N^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\ll_{\upsilon}|z^{\prime}|e^{-2z^{\prime}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2m+2}e^{-(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}}(N^{3/2}e^{-0.8(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}})

\displaystyle\ll|z^{\prime}|e^{-2z^{\prime}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{2m+2}e^{-(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}}.

This again implies (
B.44
).
Finally, by combining this equation with (
B.42
) and (
B.40
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\ll_{\upsilon}|xzz^{\prime}|e^{2(x-z-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N_{+}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{-(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}}\sum_{m\geq 0}e^{3m+3-(m^{2}/8)}

\displaystyle\ll|xzz^{\prime}|e^{2(x-z-z^{\prime})}2^{r-N_{+}}2^{\mathtt{k}_{+}-N_{+}}e^{-(\mathtt{k}_{+})^{\alpha_{-}}}

which concludes the proof of Lemma
B.4
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
B.5
.

We can use (
B.37
) in order to get (for
r
large enough and
N
large enough depending on
r
):

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(Ev(r,0,0,x,z)){\mathbb{P}}(Ev(r,2^{-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\leq e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}e^{4\upsilon+2^{1-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}}2^{2(r-N_{+})}N^{3}{\mathbb{P}}(GEv(r,0,0,x,z)){\mathbb{P}}(GEv(r,2^{1-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})),

and then, by the majorization of the second term of (
B.3.2
) which is involved in (
B.39
):

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime},x,z,x^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}e^{4\upsilon+2^{1-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}}2^{2(r-N_{+})}N^{3}{\mathbb{P}}(GEv(r,0,0,x,z)){\mathbb{P}}(GEv(r,2^{1-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime}))

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}_{\upsilon}\left(2^{2(r-N_{+})}e^{-\frac{2^{r-\mathtt{k}_{+}}-1}{8}}\right).

Hence, we have:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}e^{4\upsilon+2^{1-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8}}2^{2(r-N_{+})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times N^{3}{\mathbb{P}}(GEv(r,0,0,x,z)){\mathbb{P}}(GEv(r,2^{1-e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8},\mathtt{C}^{(r)},x^{\prime},z^{\prime}))

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}_{\upsilon}\left(2^{2(r-N_{+})}e^{-\frac{2^{r-(r/2)-3e^{\sqrt{\log r}}/8-100\log^{2}(r/2)}-1}{8}}\right).

By applying
[
BRZ19
, Lemmas 2.1,2.3]
with the same argument as
in Lemma
C.1
,
using the fact that

||E||_{1}
goes to zero when
r
goes to infinity, we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq e^{2(x-z+x^{\prime}-z^{\prime})}e^{4\upsilon}2^{2(r-N_{+})}N^{3}{\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,N}(x,z)){\mathbb{P}}(Event_{r,N}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime}))(1+\eta_{r})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{O}_{\upsilon}\left(2^{2(r-N)}e^{-\frac{2^{r/3}-1}{8}}\right),

where
\eta_{r}
goes to zero when
r
goes to infinity. Using (
B.14
), we obtain Lemma
B.4
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
B.6
in the general case.

The general case needs to uses exactly the same arguments as
in the general case of the proof of Lemma
B.4
. This time, for
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq l\leq N-1
and
p\leq 2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1
, the random variables
J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta)
and
J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta^{\prime})
are not rigorously independent. However, we observe that for
\mathtt{k}_{+}\leq l\leq N_{+}
, the absolute value of their correlations, decreases exponentially with
l
. Indeed, if
C_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta,\theta^{\prime}):={\mathbb{E}}\left(J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta)\overline{J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta^{\prime})}\right)
, then

\displaystyle\left|C_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}}\left|\sum_{j=0}^{2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}-1}e^{i(\theta-\theta^{\prime})j}\right|\leq\frac{4}{2^{l}||\theta-\theta^{\prime}||}\ll 2^{\mathtt{k}-l}\ .

Since
{\mathbb{E}}\left(J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta)J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta^{\prime})\right)=0
, and the vector

(J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta),J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta^{\prime}))
is centered complex Gaussian, one checks, by computing covariances, that it is possible to write

J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta)=\frac{C_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})}{C_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}J_{l,p}^{(\mathtt{k})}(\theta^{\prime})+J^{(\mathtt{k}),ind}_{l,p}(\theta,\theta^{\prime}),

where the two terms of the sums are independent, with an expectation of the square
equal to zero. Note that

C^{(\mathtt{k})}_{l,p}:=C^{(\mathtt{k})}_{l,p}(\theta^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})=\frac{2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}{2^{l}+p2^{l-\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}}=(2^{\kappa^{(\mathtt{k})}}+p)^{-1},

does not depend on

\theta^{\prime}
. Moreover, we have by Pythagoras’ theorem:
